# The paused decisions review: what its finished agents returned

Run `wf_dee9e4e9-370`, stopped on 2026-11-18 at 17:04 UTC to save usage. Twelve agents had finished: the six collectors (every open question of a theme, with the default as built and its context) and the three lens reviews of two themes (the base case and valuation suggestions). No judge had run, so **nothing here is a decision yet**. See `README.md` in this folder for how to resume.

## collect:base-case

Theme: Deal performance against the sponsor's underwritten base case

### BC-1: When a position has more than one confirmed base case, which one is it compared with by default: the entry case, the latest re-underwriting, or both?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Options considered' item 2 and 'Open for the owner' (Case compared). Repeated in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 538 and /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/09_ROADMAP_AND_BACKLOG.md line 86.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. The entry case. `entryCaseOf` (packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts) returns the Confirmed version with the earliest case date. `UnderwritingService.read` (apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts) compares with it unless the request carries `?version=N`, which must name a Confirmed or Superseded version (`isComparableVersion`), else 404. The card (apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingCard.tsx, `VersionPicker`) shows a 'Compared with' dropdown only when two or more such versions exist; the pick is component state, not part of the address, so it is lost on reload. The static preview (apps/web/src/preview/routes/underwriting.ts) calls the same function. Three facts the record does not state: (1) 'entry case' only means 'earliest dated standing case': `underwritingKindOf` labels it so whatever its date, and nothing compares the case date with the position's entry date; (2) `isCaseDateAllowed` refuses a new Draft dated before an existing Confirmed case, so an original case cannot be added behind a re-underwriting that was confirmed first; (3) when the entry case is retired the default moves to the next earliest Confirmed case, with no word on the card beyond its label.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: the latest confirmed case (set aside because a revision would then hide the gap to what was first underwritten). Others seen: show both at once (the entry case judged, the latest re-underwriting beside it); judge against the latest case and keep the gap to the entry case as a second line; keep the reader's pick in the address so a link opens the same view; a config key for the default.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 10 (no figure taken from somewhere else) and rule 9 if the default becomes a setting. docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 11 (the comparison takes exactly one case). docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md section 13, the paragraph under the table. docs/04_MODULES.md M9 line 152. Route GET /api/v1/investments/{id}/underwriting (packages/contracts/src/routes-underwriting.ts; `underwritingQuery.version`, `underwritingRead.comparedVersion`). Table mon.underwriting_case (state, case_date, unique index underwriting_case_confirmed_date_idx, migration packages/db/migrations/0021_underwriting_case.sql). Page words in apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts (`versionLabels`, `comparableVersions`). Tests: packages/contracts/src/underwriting.test.ts; apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('compares the entry case unless the reader names another that stands or stood as the plan'); apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingCard.test.tsx; apps/web/src/preview/routes/underwriting.test.ts; apps/web/e2e/underwriting.spec.ts. Seed: scenario underwriting_reunderwritten (docs/14_SYNTHETIC_DATA_SPEC.md section 12, tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts, checked in tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts). A watchlist flag or a roll-up (BC-17, BC-18) would have to say which case it judges.

### BC-2: Between two plan dates, is the plan read on a straight line by days (ours, not the sponsor's), or is a position compared only on the dates the sponsor gave a figure for?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Options considered' item 1, 'Consequences' line 61 and 'Open for the owner' (Between plan dates). Rule text in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 11 rule 1. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 538.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. A straight line by days. `planAt` (packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts) returns a plan point's own figure on a plan date and, between two neighbouring plan dates, v0 + (v1 - v0) x days(d0, d) / days(d0, d1) with `interpolated: true`. Before the first date, after the last, or where a neighbour lacks the metric it returns no figure and a reason (`before_plan_start`, `past_plan_end`, `missing_plan_figure`); it never reaches for a further point and never extrapolates. The same line serves twelve-month revenue, twelve-month EBITDA and net debt. The flag travels in the contract (`interpolated` on each metric row and series point, packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts). On the page it is said once, under the chart ('Between plan dates the plan is a straight line, not a sponsor figure.', `STRAIGHT_LINE_NOTE` in apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts). The tiles ('X against a plan of Y', `metricTile`) do not mark an interpolated plan figure; the page reads the flag only for chart geometry (`planChart`). A status badge (ahead, on, behind) is given on an interpolated figure exactly as on a sponsor figure. Seeded plans hold one point a year (`planFrom` in tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts), so most seeded current quarters are judged against an interpolated figure.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: compare only on the dates the sponsor gave a figure for (a comparison about once a year, none in most quarters). Others seen: keep the line for the chart and the amounts but give no status badge to an interpolated figure; show the last plan-date comparison beside the interpolated one; say 'straight line' on the tile itself, not only under the chart; a constant growth rate line for revenue and EBITDA in place of a straight one; ask for quarterly plan points where the sponsor's model has them (a case already takes up to 120 points, `UNDERWRITING_MAX_POINTS`).
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 10 (never invent a number, never substitute the nearest available period) and rule 12 (tests first, golden fixture, Python cross-check). docs/08 section 11 rule 1, 'The series by date', worked example 1 and the property tests of section 9 ('The plan at a date lies between its two neighbours'). Golden fixture packages/calc/fixtures/27_plan_tracking.json; packages/calc/src/underwriting.test.ts; apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/underwriting.py (`plan_at`). docs/04_MODULES.md M9 acceptance line 161. docs/06_BRAND_AND_UI.md missing-value rule. Page: apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingCard.tsx (`PlanLines`, `Tiles`). Preview: apps/web/src/preview/routes/underwriting.ts. tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts judges the seeded ahead, on plan and behind scenarios on interpolated figures, so a change of rule can flip a scenario tag and the API test 'reads each seeded scenario as the scenario says' (apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts).

### BC-3: Is a second person's confirmation on the record itself enough for a base case, or must it go through a staging table and an approval step as valuations and quarterly figures do, and who may that second person be?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Options considered' item 3 and 'Open for the owner' (Approval). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md section 13. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md lines 507, 538 and 550.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. No staging table. The case lives in mon.underwriting_case as Draft, then Confirmed (packages/db/migrations/0021_underwriting_case.sql). The deal team records and edits a Draft; the deal team or an approver confirms, never the person named in `entered_by` (whoever last changed the figures; every edit resets it). That rule is held three times: `underwritingCaseMachine` (packages/workflows/src/machines.ts, `notSameAs('enteredBy')`), the API (`UnderwritingService.command`), and the database (check `confirmed_by <> entered_by`, trigger `mon.guard_underwriting_case`, and migration 0022_underwriting_point_writer.sql so only the person the Draft names writes its plan points). So a second deal team member is enough: no approver and nobody from operations is needed, and operations cannot confirm at all. Withdrawing a Draft takes one deal team member and a reason; retiring a Confirmed case takes one approver and a reason. A Confirmed case never changes; a revision is a new version; both tables carry history. The record's reason for no staging: the base case is not a core financial table of rule 5 (the accounting import and export never read it, no client report prints it, it changes no valuation, quarter or position). Verified: no file under apps/api/src/reports, apps/api/src/packages or the accounting adapters reads it.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: a staging table with an approval step, as for valuations and quarterly figures. Others seen: keep the record-level confirmation but require the confirmer to hold the approver role (narrows who can confirm); keep it as built and have the system run checks for the confirmer before the button is offered (plan point at the entry snapshot date against the snapshot on record, underwritten MOIC against the IRR and dates, see BC-8 and BC-13); revisit if a base case figure ever reaches a client report, the watchlist or a roll-up (BC-17, BC-18), which would remove the premise that nothing client-facing reads it.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 5 (staging plus approval for valuations, quarterly performance, positions), rule 6, rule 14. docs/05_SECURITY.md SEC-5.6 (segregation of duties), SEC-9.1 ('staging, validation, approval, then production for every external or AI-sourced value'; decision 0022 cites it as met by Draft then Confirmed), SEC-9.2. docs/03_DATA_MODEL.md section 1 conventions ('Staging: every intake writes to stg.*'; 'Approval columns on any table that can reach a client or the accounting system'; the table has neither). docs/18 sections 2 and 13. Routes POST /api/v1/underwriting-cases/{id}/commands and PATCH /api/v1/underwriting-cases/{id} (packages/contracts/src/routes-underwriting.ts). Tests: packages/workflows/src/machines.test.ts, packages/db/src/guards.test.ts, packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts (the claim step and the rows for a Draft somebody else entered), apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('goes from a Draft to the plan the card compares only once a second person confirms it'). Page actions: `caseActions` in apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts. Adding a role or a new permission is not the build's to take; narrowing the confirmer is.

### BC-4: When is a base case entered: only once the position exists, as built, or earlier (at investment committee or closing), and does a missing base case block or flag anything?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Open for the owner' (Timing and source: 'a case is entered once the position exists ... it does not block a closing'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 538.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. A case hangs on a position: the only way in is POST /api/v1/investments/{id}/underwriting (apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.controller.ts), and mon.underwriting_case.investment_id is not null, so nothing can be recorded while the deal is still an opportunity. The deal becomes a position at Closing > Closed (docs/18 section 4), so a case is always entered after closing. Nothing in the pipeline or the closing reads it: config/closing_checklist.json and config/diligence_templates.json have no base case item, and the gates of docs/18 sections 4 and 12 do not mention it. Nothing chases a missing one: no expected-document row, no data exception, no work list entry; the card says 'No base case recorded.' and the deal team may record one (one seeded position in five has none on purpose, docs/14 section 12). The case date is free text of a date: it is never compared with the position's entry date; with no Confirmed case any date stands, and later Drafts must be dated as a Confirmed case or after all of them (`isCaseDateAllowed`, packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts). Figures are typed by hand in US$ millions (apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingForm.tsx); nothing is read from a document.
- **alternatives:** The record names none beyond 'can change'. Seen: a closing checklist item for the sponsor's base case in config/closing_checklist.json, required or not (config, no code rule); record the case on the opportunity at investment committee and carry it to the position at conversion (the case table would need an opportunity link: a migration on this feature's own table, not one the accounting import or export reads); leave entry after closing but have the system raise a work list entry or a data exception when a held equity, continuation vehicle or credit position has no Confirmed case a set number of days after entry (threshold in config); warn when a first case is dated far from the entry date; read a Draft out of the sponsor's model or the committee memo by extraction, for a person to confirm.
- **context:** docs/18 sections 4 (deal stage), 12 (closing) and 13. docs/04_MODULES.md M8 (closing), M3 (documents), M9. Expected-document tracker apps/api/src/documents/expected.ts; data exceptions docs/18 section 9; work lists in config/role_focus.json and apps/web/src/lib/role-focus.ts. CLAUDE.md rule 9 (thresholds in config), rule 5 and SEC-9.1 if a model reads the figures (a person still confirms), 3c for any new job. Table mon.underwriting_case (migration 0021). Tests: apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('refuses a Draft the position cannot take: a second one, a revision with no reason, a date out of line, a foreign source'), apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting-form.test.ts, UnderwritingActions.test.tsx. Seed: docs/14 section 12 (entered a week after entry, confirmed two days later).

### BC-5: What counts as the source of a base case: is a free note on where the model is held enough, or must it be a document of the position with a page or a sheet and cell?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Open for the owner' (Timing and source: 'with a document or a note on where the model is held') and 'SEC IDs affected' (SEC-9.4). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md section 13, the confirm row.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. Either. Outside a Draft a case must name `source_document_id` or a non-blank `source_note` (check constraint in packages/db/migrations/0021_underwriting_case.sql; `hasSource` precondition of `confirm` in packages/workflows/src/machines.ts; `hasSource` in apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts). A source document must be a doc.document row of the same position that the caller can read (`requireSourceDocument`, one refusal sentence for 'not yours' and 'not this position's'); a page and a sheet-and-cell locator are optional and only allowed with a document (packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts). A note needs 3 characters or more and is otherwise unchecked. A Draft needs no source. The card links the document, or prints the note (`SourceValue` in apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingCard.tsx). Every seeded case uses a note ('Sponsor base case model at entry, held in the deal folder'), because the seed has no doc.document rows, so the document path is exercised only by tests.
- **alternatives:** The record names the two forms and no other choice. Seen: a document always (the note path removed, so every figure can be opened at its page); a note allowed only with a stated reason why no document is on the platform; a document required when the position has one of a fitting type (a model or a committee memo in the document hub) and a note only otherwise, decided by the system from the documents on hand; a page or cell required whenever a document is named.
- **context:** docs/05_SECURITY.md SEC-9.4 (click-to-source lineage: 'Every reported number links to its source document, page and extraction run'). docs/03_DATA_MODEL.md section 1 lineage columns (source_document_id, source_page, source_locator) and the row for underwriting_case. docs/18 section 13. Document hub: docs/04 M3, table doc.document, taxonomy of document types. Form: apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingForm.tsx and underwriting-form.ts (source fields, the list of the position's documents). Tests: apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts (a foreign source refused), packages/workflows/src/machines.test.ts (confirm refused without a source), packages/db/src/guards.test.ts. Seed text in tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts (`SOURCE_NOTE`, `LOAN_SOURCE_NOTE`) and docs/14 section 12. A wall: the source document is joined under row-level security, so it is named only to a reader who can open it (apps/api/src/underwriting/rows.ts).

### BC-6: Are the two tolerances right (EBITDA, revenue and net debt within 5% of plan are on plan; a gross IRR within 2 points of the underwritten IRR is on plan), and is one share for all three company figures the right shape?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Configuration' and 'Open for the owner' (Tolerances). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/definitions.json key `underwriting` (its note calls it 'a draft'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 11 ('The tolerances', rules 3 and 5).
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. config/definitions.json: `underwriting.onPlanTolerance` 0.05 and `underwriting.irrTolerance` 0.02. No default in code: `tolerancesOf` (apps/api/src/underwriting/comparison.ts) answers a 500 that names the key when one is missing, unreadable or negative; tools/synthetic/src/settings.ts (`underwritingTolerances`) and the preview (apps/web/src/preview/routes/underwriting.ts imports the config file) read the same keys. `planStatus` (packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts): ahead above the tolerance, behind below its negative, a value exactly on either edge is on plan. One relative tolerance serves revenue, EBITDA and net debt (net debt with the sign turned, less is better); the IRR tolerance is in absolute points whatever the length of the hold. No IRR status while the IRR carries a flag (held under `irr.minHoldYearsForDisplay`, no Locked valuation, several roots). Figures are held to ten places before they are judged, so a cent can sit on an edge (docs/08 section 11, 'Held as it travels'). The read returns the thresholds it used (`thresholds` in the contract). On the page behind reads as 'watch', ahead as 'good', on plan as neutral.
- **alternatives:** The record: any other values, as a configuration edit. Seen: a tolerance per metric (revenue, EBITDA, net debt each its own key); a tolerance by deal type; a band that is wider early in the plan and narrows as the planned exit nears; an uneven band (a small shortfall judged sooner than a small beat); a floor in money so a very small plan figure does not flip on a rounding amount; tie the EBITDA band to the firm's existing watchlist thresholds in the same file (`watchlist.ebitdaYoYDeclinePct` is 0.15) so one rule is not stated twice.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 9 (config over code), rule 10, rule 12. docs/08 section 11 rules 3 and 5 and the property tests of section 9 (scaling changes neither percent nor status; a higher EBITDA never lowers its status). Fixtures packages/calc/fixtures/27_plan_tracking.json (case 2 sits on both edges) and 28_underwriting_comparison.json; apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/underwriting.py (`status`). tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts judges the three status scenarios under the config values, and the scenarios are built 8 points of growth and 5 points of IRR away from the position's own (`UNDERWRITING_GROWTH_SHIFT`, `UNDERWRITING_IRR_SHIFT`), so a tolerance at or above those shifts breaks the dataset check. Tests: apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('refuses to answer without its tolerances: a 500 that names the key'), tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.test.ts. Page badges: `PLAN_STATUS_LABEL`, `planStatusTone` in apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts. What the owner's team calls 'on plan' in practice is a fact only the owner has; the system works whatever the values are.

### BC-7: Before the exit, what is a held position's gross MOIC set against: the MOIC it would show today had its flows compounded at the underwritten IRR (built, with no status), or the underwritten MOIC at exit, or something else, and should the MOIC gap be judged?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Options considered' item 4 and 'Open for the owner' (MOIC before the exit). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 11 rule 5 and worked examples 3 and 4.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. `returnsAgainstPlan` and `pathValue` (packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts). For a position held on an as-of date on or before the planned exit: path value = -sum(flow x (1 + r)^(days(flow date, as-of) / 365)) over our signed flows, path MOIC = (distributions + path value) / invested, MOIC gap = gross MOIC - path MOIC, zero when our IRR equals r. No path after the planned exit (`past_plan_end`) or with nothing invested (`no_invested_capital`). A realized position has no path: its gap is the gross MOIC less the underwritten MOIC at exit. The MOIC gap never carries a status; the IRR gap is the judged figure. The tile (`moicTile`, apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts) shows the gross MOIC, the signed gap 'against N on the underwritten path' and the underwritten MOIC 'at exit' (for a loan 'at repayment'), with no badge. The gross MOIC and IRR come from the position summary over our latest Locked mark (apps/api/src/portfolio/metrics.ts), so the gap moves with our own mark. Nothing checks that the underwritten MOIC agrees with the underwritten IRR and the dates of the case.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: against the underwritten MOIC at exit only (set aside because every young position would read as behind). Others seen: give the MOIC gap a status with its own tolerance in config; a path read from the plan itself (the equity value the plan implies at each plan date over the entry equity) in place of a path compounded at the IRR; show the exit target only, beside the share of planned time that has passed; no MOIC tile for a held position, since the IRR gap says the same thing.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rules 10 and 12. docs/08 sections 2 and 3 (MOIC, XIRR), section 11 rule 5, the property tests of section 9 ('With a NAV equal to the path value, the IRR of our flows is the underwritten IRR and the MOIC gap is zero'; 'a higher NAV never lowers the MOIC gap'). Fixture packages/calc/fixtures/28_underwriting_comparison.json; packages/calc/src/underwriting.test.ts; apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/underwriting.py (`path_value`, `returns`). Contract `planReturns` (moicOnPath, moicGap, pathNotCalculable) in packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts. Inputs: `comparisonInputs` in apps/api/src/underwriting/comparison.ts (`signedFlows`, the summary). Page tests apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.test.ts and UnderwritingCard.test.tsx. API test 'answers every figure by its rule over the figures returned with it'.

### BC-8: Should the seeded examples of 'ahead of plan', 'on plan' and 'behind plan' be rebuilt so the plan is one a sponsor could have underwritten (today the 'ahead' plan is a company that shrinks, and the scenario MOICs do not tie to their plans), and should the product itself check that a case's own figures tie?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 564, 'Not taken, and why' ('It is the owner's call: a floor on the scenario's plan growth, or an exit multiple solved so the plan's exit pays the underwritten MOIC'). Rule text in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/14_SYNTHETIC_DATA_SPEC.md section 12 (lines 141 and 144).
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts, the block for the three status scenarios: the plan's EBITDA and revenue grow at the position's own annualised EBITDA rate less 8 points (ahead), at that rate (on plan) or plus 8 points (behind), at the entry margin (`UNDERWRITING_GROWTH_SHIFT`); the underwritten IRR is the position's own gross IRR less 5 points, equal, or plus 5 points (`UNDERWRITING_IRR_SHIFT`), and the underwritten MOIC is the one that IRR stands for over the holding period, written over the MOIC the plan's exit would give. The exit multiple stays the entry multiple plus a drawn shift. `fit` ranks hosts so the strongest performer takes 'ahead', but no floor refuses a negative plan growth. In the generated small profile at seed 42 (tools/synthetic/.synthetic/dataset.json, read only): the 'ahead' plan has EBITDA falling 2.65% a year (16.4m to 14.8m over four years) with an underwritten 1.70x and 14.65% IRR, while its own exit (exit multiple x exit EBITDA less net debt, over entry equity) pays 1.09x; the 'on plan' case is underwritten at a 5.76% gross IRR and 1.47x against a plan exit of 1.64x; the 'behind' case 1.61x against 2.15x. Because the planned EBITDA growth driver of the 'ahead' case is negative, its progress row on the card reads as not calculable. tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts requires both the EBITDA status and the IRR status to match the tag. Separately, ordinary plans grow 4% to 12% a year while actuals drift -3% to 9%, so most ordinary seeded positions read behind plan (docs/14 line 141). The product never checks a recorded case for internal agreement (MOIC against IRR and dates, or against the plan's own exit): the contract, the machine and the database accept any positive MOIC with any IRR above -100%.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: a floor on the scenario's plan growth; or an exit multiple solved so the plan's exit pays the underwritten MOIC. Others seen: build the scenario plans as ordinary drawn plans (4% to 12% growth) and choose hosts whose actuals sit ahead of, on or behind them, leaving a scenario out when no host fits; derive the underwritten MOIC and IRR from the plan's exit as ordinary cases do and tune the plan, not the returns, to reach the wanted IRR status; a product rule that shows the confirmer where a case's figures do not tie (the MOIC the plan's exit pays against the MOIC entered; the IRR that MOIC stands for over the dates against the IRR entered), as a flag and never a block.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 1 (synthetic data only), rule 12 if a tie check becomes a calculation rule (docs/08 text, fixture, Python cross-check first). docs/14 section 12 (the scenario rules as written; changing them is a change to that text). Any change to scenario figures moves the seed 42 datasets in both profiles, the preview recordings (apps/web/dist-preview/preview/fixtures.json is rebuilt by `build:preview`), and every test that reads those positions: tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.test.ts (the SHA-256 pins hold the dataset without the cases and the equity cases as first built), apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('reads each seeded scenario as the scenario says'), apps/web/src/preview/routes/underwriting.test.ts, the preview probe (212 then 223 checks) and apps/web/e2e/underwriting.spec.ts. The demo videos and site media show these positions (decision 0015, 0020). Related: BC-3 (what the confirmer is shown), BC-7 (MOIC and IRR consistency), BC-13 (plan against the record at entry).

### BC-9: Is a loan's yield to maturity priced on the fair value of the latest approved credit quarter (the reported figure) or on our own latest Locked mark, given that the loan's IRR and MOIC on the same card already use the Locked mark?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 565, 'Not taken, and why' ('pricing it on the mark would change the Credit terms figure too'), and line 541 under 'Open items'. Rule text in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 11 rule 6 and section 10. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'The calculation', last bullet.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. On the credit quarter. `creditYieldToMaturity` (apps/api/src/performance/performance.service.ts) takes the latest approved mon.credit_performance row on or before the as-of date and prices its `fair_value` and `par_value` over the contractual flows to maturity (`yieldToMaturity`, packages/calc/src/credit.ts). It is the one place the yield is worked out: the Credit terms card (apps/web/src/pages/deal/PerformanceTab.tsx), the Analytics credit tab (apps/web/src/pages/analytics/CreditTab.tsx) and the base case read (`loadCreditYieldToMaturity`, called from apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts) all show that figure. The loan's gross MOIC and IRR on the same card come from the position summary over the latest Locked mon.valuation (apps/api/src/portfolio/metrics.ts, `latestLockedValuation`). So a mark of ours locked below the reported fair value moves the IRR gap and leaves the yield as it was. The card says so (`YIELD_NOTE`: 'Yield to maturity is priced on the fair value of the latest approved credit quarter, which our own mark does not change. It rises when that fair value falls, so it is not judged.'), and neither the yield nor the loss rate carries a status. The generator mirrors the rule (`loanYieldToMaturity` in tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts), as does the preview route.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: price it on our Locked mark (set aside because the Credit terms figure would change with it). Others seen: show both yields when the two values differ (at the reported fair value and at our mark), each labelled; price on the Locked mark when one exists for the quarter or later and on the credit row otherwise, in the one function, so all three pages change together; keep it as built. Pricing on the mark in the base case card alone would put two different figures called yield to maturity on one page.
- **context:** docs/08 section 10 (the yield to maturity row: 'XIRR of {-fair_value on the as-of date, ...}'; section 1 says a loan's terminal value is fair value plus accrued interest) and section 11 rule 6. CLAUDE.md rule 7 (one rule in one place), rule 10, rule 12 (a change is docs/08 first, then the credit fixtures and apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/credit.py). docs/04_MODULES.md M9 acceptance line 165 ('A loan marked below par reads behind plan on its IRR, and its yield to maturity carries no status'). Tables mon.credit_performance (fair_value, par_value), mon.credit_terms, mon.valuation. Contract: `yieldToMaturity` in packages/contracts/src/schemas.ts (credit terms detail) and in the comparison. Tests: apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('reads a loan on its returns alone ... the yield to maturity of its Credit terms'; 'records a loan's base case ... reads the loan behind once it is marked at 90% of par'), fixture 28 worked example 4. Decision 0023 raises no suggested mark for private credit, so a loan's Locked mark is always typed by a person through the valuation workflow.

### BC-10: Should a primary fund position hold a base case of its own kind, or none as built, and if none should the page say so?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Forms' ('Primary funds hold nothing yet: no card, and a create is refused'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md section 13, first row ('a primary fund has no base case'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/14_SYNTHETIC_DATA_SPEC.md section 12 ('primary funds have none').
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. None. `underwritingFormOf` (apps/api/src/underwriting/comparison.ts) answers 'equity' for the three equity deal types (`EQUITY_DEAL_TYPES` in packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts: co_invest_equity, cv_single_asset, cv_multi_asset), 'credit' for deal_type.private_credit and null for anything else. For null the read answers `form: null` with no versions and no comparison; the card renders nothing at all (`UnderwritingCard` returns null, so the reader is not told why); a create answers 422 'this kind of position holds no base case' (`UNDERWRITING_REFUSALS.noBaseCase`). The database allows only `form in ('equity','credit')` (migration 0021). The generator gives primary funds no case. Side fact: a multi-asset continuation vehicle takes the equity form, a single company plan, because it is in the equity list.
- **alternatives:** The record says 'nothing yet' and names no design. Seen: a fund form holding what a commitment is underwritten on (net IRR, net TVPI, the expected pace of drawdowns and distributions) compared with the fund's reported net figures and our own flows on the commitment; nothing until a fund commitment carries a mark at all (decision 0023 question 1, 'a position per commitment'); keep none and show one line on the Performance tab saying a primary fund holds no sponsor base case. A fund form needs a third value of `form` and new columns on mon.underwriting_case, a table of this feature that the accounting import and export do not read.
- **context:** Decision docs/decisions/0004-private-credit-and-lp-commitments.md (deal types and commitments). Decision docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md questions 1 and 10 (a fund commitment has no valuation record; no suggestion for a primary fund). docs/04_MODULES.md M9 ('Primary fund monitoring: fund NAV, calls, distributions, unfunded, reported net metrics') and acceptance line 164 ('a primary fund has no base case and a create is refused'). docs/03_DATA_MODEL.md: core.commitment, mon.cash_flow.commitment_id, pacing_plan, track_record (net_irr, net_moic; listed, not built). No net IRR or net MOIC of a position is worked out anywhere in packages/calc, the API or the schema today (BC-15). docs/08 section 11 has no fund rule, so a fund form is new financial math: rule 12 applies (spec text, golden fixture, Python cross-check first). Tests: apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('answers no form and nothing else for a position that holds no base case'). Note that for a fund the 'base case' is our own underwriting of the commitment, not a sponsor's plan for one company.

### BC-11: docs/18 says every transition emits an outbox event; no base case transition writes one (nor does any other workflow). Should the events be written now, or should the rule's text say what is true until an outbox writer exists?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Consequences' line 58 ('No outbox event is written, although docs/18 says every transition emits one ... The events are added when the API gains an outbox writer'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 528 ('Differences from the written design'). The rule: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md line 3.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. Nothing is written to ops.outbox by any service. The table exists (packages/db/migrations/0003_audit_ops.sql; packages/db/src/schema/ops.ts; policies in 0006_history_rls.sql: any signed-in user may insert, the service role reads and updates; classified in packages/db/src/classification.ts), but a search of apps/ and packages/workflows finds no insert into it and no relay that would publish from it. Every base case transition writes audit.event through the audit callback of `DbService.run` (apps/api/src/db/db.service.ts), with the actions underwriting_case.create, .edit, .confirm, .supersede, .withdraw and .retire and the state before and after (apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts). The same is true of valuations, closings, capital notices and valuation suggestions (decision 0023 line 75 records the same deviation). Nothing consumes events today.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: add the events when the API gains an outbox writer. Others seen: write the outbox row now, in one place, for every transition of every machine (beside the audit write, same transaction, a payload of ids, machine, command and states only), with no relay yet; the writer plus a relay behind a mock adapter with a kill switch, a health check and an Integration Health probe; change the sentence in docs/18 line 3 (and the matching lines in packages/workflows/README.md line 5, docs/17_ENGINEERING_STANDARDS.md section 5 and docs/02_ARCHITECTURE.md line 103) to say the audit event is the record of a transition and the outbox is not yet written; leave the deviation recorded in each decision as now.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 14 (every state change through the transition tables of docs/18), rule 8, section 3c (telemetry with no business content; a new integration ships with a kill switch, a health check and a probe). docs/05_SECURITY.md SEC-11.1 (audit trail) and SEC-11.4 (no figures in logs, which bounds an event payload). docs/17 section 5 (outbox pattern, dead-letter queue). Possible consumers named elsewhere and not built on events: 'alerts prior approvers' (docs/18 section 1), watchlist alerts to the deal team (docs/04 M9), Beach Ops (docs/15_AI_OPERATIONS.md). A real queue would be a new external service, which the build may not add; a row in a table of our own database is not. Tests that would move: `pnpm test:workflows` (packages/workflows/src/machines.test.ts), the access matrix row for ops.outbox (packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts), every API workflow test if a writer is asserted. This is one decision for all machines, not for the base case alone.

### BC-12: Should the comparison carry one overall verdict for a position (a blended score, or a rule such as 'the weakest figure decides'), or stay as built with EBITDA against plan as the headline and the other figures beside it?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Options considered' item 5 ('One blended score. Not built. It would need weights nobody has chosen'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 11 rule 3 ('The status of EBITDA is the headline; there is no blended score').
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. No blended score. `planHeadline` (packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts) returns the status of EBITDA against plan and the contract carries it as `headline`, but no page reads that field: the card simply puts the EBITDA tile first, then revenue, net debt, the gross IRR (each with its own badge) and the gross MOIC (no badge) (`Tiles` in apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingCard.tsx). Value creation progress carries no status. A loan has no headline; it is judged on its IRR gap alone. The headline is used by the dataset check (tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts).
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: one blended score (set aside for want of weights). Others seen: a verdict by rule with no weights (the weakest of the judged figures decides; or EBITDA decides unless the IRR is behind); show the EBITDA headline as one badge in the card's header so the field is used; no headline at all, and the field removed from the contract.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 9 (weights would be config) and rule 12 (a verdict rule is docs/08 text, fixture and cross-check first). docs/08 section 11 rule 3; fixtures 27 and 28; apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/underwriting.py (`headline`). Contract `underwritingComparison.headline` (packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts) and apps/api/openapi.json. A watchlist flag (BC-17) or a roll-up across positions (BC-18) would need one verdict per position, so this question is decided before or with them. The deal fit measures (docs/decisions/0018-deal-fit-measures.md) are the nearest precedent for draft weights in config.

### BC-13: A sponsor's model may define EBITDA (and revenue or net debt) differently from its quarterly reports. Should the system detect and show a difference in basis, or compare the two as given?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md, 'Consequences' line 60. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 540 under 'Open items'.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. Compared as given. A plan point's EBITDA is set against mon.quarterly_performance.ebitda_ltm of the current quarter with no adjustment (`metricRow` in packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts). The record says plan figures are 'on the basis the actuals use' (migration 0021 comments, docs/03 row for underwriting_case_point), but nothing checks it. The planned bridge starts from the entry snapshot on record, not from the plan's own first point (`plannedBridge`), so a plan whose first point differs from the snapshot is bridged from a figure the plan never held. The only help to a reader is the source of the case on the card. The case has a free `note` field and no field for the basis.
- **alternatives:** The record: compare as given, with the source on the card. Seen: when a plan holds a point on the entry snapshot's date, compare its EBITDA, revenue and net debt with the snapshot on record and show any difference beyond a tolerance in config to the person confirming and on the card (a flag, never a change to a figure); a basis label on the case (as reported, adjusted, pro forma) shown beside every plan figure; require the plan's first point to be the entry snapshot date so the check can always run. Rescaling the plan to the reported basis would invent numbers and is ruled out by rule 10.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 10 and rule 9. docs/08 section 4 (operating metrics), section 8 ('Entry and current quarter of a position', the one bridge rule) and section 11 rule 4. Tables mon.quarterly_performance (is_entry_snapshot), mon.underwriting_case_point. apps/api/src/analytics/value-creation.ts (`entryAndCurrent`, `bridgeOf`), apps/api/src/underwriting/comparison.ts. The seed's ordinary plans start from the entry snapshot as it is on record (docs/14 section 12, 'Point 0 is the entry snapshot as it is on record'), so a seeded mismatch does not exist and a check would need a new scenario or a test-only case. Related: BC-3 (what the second person is shown), BC-8 (whether a case's own figures tie).

Nothing is converted anywhere. The form types figures in US$ millions.

CLAUDE.md rule 10. Decision 0023 uses the same stance (`not_usd`: no suggestion for a position not held in US dollars; 'The book carries the currency a position is held in'). config/definitions.json `currencyStorage`. Tests: apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('refuses a body that is not a base case, field by field'), packages/contracts/src/underwriting.test.ts.

The comparison is worked out on read per position; a watchlist over the whole book would work it out for every position on each read or need a shared read model (docs/decisions/0008-shared-read-models-and-preview-engine.md). ### BC-19: When a loan's yield to maturity is missing, should the card say which reason applies (past maturity, no priced quarter, a fair value of zero or less) as every other missing figure of the comparison does?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 533, 'Differences from the written design' ('A loan's yield to maturity has no reason code when it is missing ... the card says "Not calculable this quarter."').
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. No reason travels. `creditYieldToMaturity` (apps/api/src/performance/performance.service.ts) returns null when there is no approved credit row on or before the date, when its fair value or par is missing, when the payment frequency is not a known one, and (inside `yieldToMaturity`, packages/calc/src/credit.ts) when maturity is not in the future or fair value is zero or less. The comparison carries `yieldToMaturity: string | null` with no reason beside it, and `yieldTiles` (apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts) prints the placeholder with 'Not calculable this quarter.' The Credit terms answer does carry `pastMaturity` (same service), which the base case read does not pass on. Every other missing figure of the comparison has a reason code (PLAN_METRIC_REASONS, PLAN_PATH_REASONS, PLAN_PROGRESS_REASONS in packages/calc/src/terms.ts).
- **alternatives:** The record names none; it records the gap. Seen: add a reason to the yield (for example past_maturity, no_priced_quarter, non_positive_fair_value) in the one function, so the Credit terms card and the base case card both say why; pass `pastMaturity` through to the base case read only; leave it (rule 10 is met by 'not calculable this quarter').
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 10 ('show the missing-value placeholder or "not calculable this quarter"') and decision 0022's own line 'A figure that cannot be worked out is missing and says why'. docs/08 section 10 ('Null when: maturity missing or past, fair value <= 0') and section 11 rule 6. docs/06_BRAND_AND_UI.md missing-value handling. Contract: packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts (`underwritingComparison.yieldToMaturity`), packages/contracts/src/schemas.ts (credit terms detail), apps/api/openapi.json. Fixture 28 and apps/worker-py/src/calc_check (a reason is part of the calculation's answer, so fixture and cross-check move with it). Seed: the `covenant_breach` position of the small profile is a held loan past maturity (docs/14 section 12).

### BC-20: Should the synthetic data carry a loan marked below par, so the 'behind plan because of our own mark' case is on a card and in the demo, or is the fixture and the API test enough?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 534, 'Differences from the written design' ('No marked-down loan is seeded, since changing a seeded mark would move recorded totals'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/14_SYNTHETIC_DATA_SPEC.md section 12, 'A loan case', last sentence.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. Not seeded. Every seeded loan carries a Confirmed case built from its own terms (yield = cash coupon + PIK coupon + discount spread over the years to maturity; a loss rate drawn between 0.5% and 2%; IRR = yield less loss rate; MOIC = 1 + IRR x years; tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts, the loans block). Seeded loans read on plan or behind on their own flows. The marked-down case is covered by golden fixture packages/calc/fixtures/28_underwriting_comparison.json (worked example 4: marked at 9,000,000 the IRR is 5.12% against 10%, behind) and by the API test 'records a loan's base case with its yield and loss rate, and reads the loan behind once it is marked at 90% of par' (apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts), which locks the mark through the valuation workflow.
- **alternatives:** The record names the reason it was not done and no other route. Seen: leave it (as built); add a scenario position of its own, drawn from a stream of its own after everything else, so no existing total moves but the dataset grows by one loan and its marks (the 'without the base cases' digests in tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.test.ts would still move, since a position is not a base case row); lower one existing loan's Locked mark (moves recorded totals, recordings and every test that reads them).
- **context:** docs/14 sections 3 and 12 (scenario tags, the loan case). CLAUDE.md rule 1 (synthetic only). The SHA-256 pins of the seed 42 dataset and of the equity cases (tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.test.ts), the preview recordings and probe, the demo media (apps/site, decisions 0015 and 0020), and portfolio totals asserted across apps/api/src/test. Related: BC-9 (which value the yield is priced on shows best on exactly this loan) and BC-8 (rebuilding the status scenarios moves the same pins, so the two could be done in one dataset change).

### BC-21: Should the form check a plan's revenue against the unit sanity bounds before it sends, or is the API's refusal (in the form's own words) enough?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 566, 'Not taken, and why' ('The revenue bounds are not checked in the form itself: they are the API's configuration, which the web app carries only in the preview').
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. The API checks. `revenueBounds` (apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts) reads `unitSanityBoundsUsd.revenueMin` and `revenueMax` from config/definitions.json, with no default in code, and `underwritingBodyIssues` (packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts) refuses a plan point outside them with a 400 that names the row and gives the bounds in dollars and in US$ millions, so a figure typed as 200 for 200,000,000 is refused and never guessed. The form (apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting-form.ts) does not know the bounds; it converts US$ millions to dollars exactly and shows every figure back in full on a review step before sending. Only the static preview carries the config (apps/web/src/preview/routes/underwriting.ts imports the file).
- **alternatives:** The record sets the form check aside and names no other route. Seen: return the bounds with the read (beside `thresholds`) so the form can check before sending with the same numbers; leave it (as built: one rule in one place, the refusal already reads in the form's terms).
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 9 (config over code; the web app must not hold its own copy of a threshold) and rule 11 (web to API through packages/contracts). docs/08 section 6 (units) and `unitSanityBoundsUsd` in config/definitions.json. docs/17 section 3 (validation problems, field paths). Tests: apps/api/src/test/underwriting.test.ts ('refuses a write without the revenue bounds of config: a 500 that names the key'), apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting-form.test.ts, UnderwritingActions.test.tsx (the refusal shown under the row).

### BC-22: Should the database itself move `entered_at` on every edit of a Draft, or is it enough that the API always sets it?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 567, 'Not taken, and why' ('A Draft edit need not move entered_at in the database: the API always sets it from its clock, and a fixed clock in tests would not move it').
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. The API sets it. `UnderwritingService.edit` writes `enteredBy` and `enteredAt: new Date(this.adapters.clock.now())` on every edit (apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts). The trigger `mon.guard_underwriting_case` (packages/db/migrations/0021_underwriting_case.sql) requires that an edit of a Draft inside a user transaction names the person acting as `entered_by`, and does not require `entered_at` to change. The history table records every change with its own time and actor.
- **alternatives:** The record sets a database rule aside and names no other. Seen: the trigger sets `entered_at` to the transaction time whenever a figure of a Draft changes in a user transaction (the API's injected clock and the database clock would then differ in tests with a fixed clock); the trigger requires the new value to be no earlier than the old; leave it (as built).
- **context:** docs/17_ENGINEERING_STANDARDS.md section 4 ('Clock is injected. No new Date() in domain code'), which is why the API and not the database owns the time. docs/05_SECURITY.md SEC-5.6 rests on `entered_by`, not on `entered_at`; SEC-9.2 (history) and SEC-11.1 (audit) already hold the time of each change. docs/18 section 13, last paragraph. Tests: packages/db/src/guards.test.ts, packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts (the claim statement sets both columns). A change is a new migration on this feature's own table.

### BC-23: The generator refuses the small profile under seed 14 because ten base case scenarios do not fit on the six positions left. Should scenarios be allowed to go missing or share a position on a small dataset, or is refusing that seed right?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 569, 'Not taken, and why', and line 554. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/14_SYNTHETIC_DATA_SPEC.md section 12, the paragraph after the scenario list.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. Refused. Each scenario takes one position of its own, first among positions with an ordinary case, then among those left without one (`pick`, `free`, `spare` in tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts). 'A scenario that still no position can carry is left out, never bent to fit, and the check reports it missing' (tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts), so `pnpm synth --profile small --seed 14` fails its check. Of the small profile's seeds 1 to 20, the thirteen listed in tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.test.ts pass; seed 42 in both profiles carries all ten and is the seed everything else is built on.
- **alternatives:** The record names none. Seen: leave it (as built: the canonical seed is unaffected); report a scenario that does not fit as a warning on small datasets and fail only on the default profile; let scenarios that do not conflict share one position (for example a gap in the plan on the position that is also behind plan); give the small profile more positions (moves every small dataset).
- **context:** docs/14 sections 2, 3 and 12 (profiles, scenario tags, the check). CLAUDE.md common commands (`pnpm synth --profile small --seed 42`, `pnpm synth:verify`). Tests: tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.test.ts ('fits its scenarios on ordinary seeds ...'). Nothing in the product depends on seeds other than 42; property style tests over several seeds do.

The record does not say that a pull request for the base case alone was opened.

### BC-26: The history tables of the base case (every past row image of a case and its plan points) are read by role alone at the database, so operations, approvers and auditors outside a wall can read a walled position's base case history. Should history follow the wall of its position?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md, 'Open for the owner' question 12 ('History tables and the wall', which says 'like every history table'), and /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 651. Decision 0022 itself states only that 'a walled position's base case is invisible to everyone outside the wall' (SEC-5.1 and SEC-5.3) and does not record the exception.
- **builtDefault:** Paths from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach. Role alone. `audit.add_history` (packages/db/migrations/0001_baseline.sql) creates `<table>_history` with one policy, `history_read`, for operations, approver and auditor, and migration 0021 calls it for mon.underwriting_case and mon.underwriting_case_point. The access matrix confirms it (packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts): on the fixture an actor outside the wall sees 1 case and 2 points, a wall member 3 and 6, while operations, approver and auditor see 5 rows of mon.underwriting_case_history and 6 of mon.underwriting_case_point_history, the walled ones included. The row image is the full row (figures, source, people). No API route reads a history table, so the gap is at the database line only.
- **alternatives:** Named in decision 0023: one migration that gives the history table of every walled record the visibility clause of its base table, with the access matrix to match (recorded there as waiting for the owner because it changes a security policy on existing tables). Others seen: wall only the history of tables added since the wall existed; leave it and forbid any route from reading history until it is walled (as built).
- **context:** docs/05_SECURITY.md SEC-5.1 (defense in depth: the database is a line of its own), SEC-5.3 (walls; searches and answers never reveal a walled record), SEC-9.2 (history kept). CLAUDE.md section 4 ('Ask before: ... changing security roles'); the change narrows who can read and adds no role. It is one decision for every history table (the list in packages/db/migrations/0006_history_rls.sql and later migrations), so it is likely decided once under the valuation suggestions or security theme; it is listed here because the two base case history tables are in scope and decision 0022's SEC note should then say what is true. Tests: `pnpm test:rls` (packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts rows for both history tables), packages/db/src/classification.ts.

## collect:valuation-suggestions

Theme: Valuation suggestions from capital account statements and sponsor reports

### VS-1: How does a fund commitment carry a mark, so that an approved fund-level capital account statement can raise a valuation suggestion?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md: 'Open for the owner' item 1 (line 103), 'Left out for now' (line 65), Context (line 14). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 614 ('The first blocks suggestions for fund statements'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/04_MODULES.md line 176. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/11_CAPABILITY_CHECKLIST.md rows E4 and E15. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md section 14, 'None when', first bullet (line 123).
- **builtDefault:** A fund-level statement (document type capital account statement, linked to no position) is extracted, reviewed and approved, and its approval writes nothing. SuggestionDerivationService.runApproved answers none with the reason fund_commitment when the run has no investment (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/suggestions/derivation.service.ts lines 157 to 160). suggestionBar (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts lines 212 to 225) gives the same reason for a position whose deal type is neither one of the three equity types nor credit, so a deal_type.primary_fund position raises none either. mon.valuation.investment_id and mon.valuation_suggestion.investment_id are NOT NULL, so neither can point at a commitment (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/migrations/0004_mon.sql line 85, 0023_valuation_suggestion.sql line 34). core.commitment (vehicle, sponsor fund, optional client) already carries approved cash flows and capital notices keyed by commitment_id and shows called, distributed and unfunded, with no value (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/portfolio/commitments.ts). The seeded fund-level statement lists one block per commitment with the four commitment lines and no balance of ours, its five balance keys are null, and the statement prompt tells the model to answer null for the five balance lines when several accounts are listed (STATEMENT_TASK in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/ai/src/registry.ts). The tracker expects one fund-level statement per sponsor fund with a commitment (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/documents/expected.service.ts, generate).
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: (a) recommended there, a position per commitment (core.investment with deal_type.primary_fund, as closing a primary fund deal already creates through /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/pipeline/convert.ts), kept out of the company-level exports and report sets, rule 12.1 then applying as written; (b) a valuation on the commitment, which changes mon.valuation, a table the accounting import and export read. Others seen: (c) a separate record for a commitment's reported balance with its own approval, which would be a second valuation workflow; (d) keep as built (read, reviewed, no suggestion) and show the reported balance on the commitments board as information only; (e) let mon.valuation_suggestion name a commitment while the mark still needs a home.
- **context:** Facts a decision touches. 1) core.investment.portfolio_company_id is NOT NULL (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/migrations/0002_core.sql line 179): a position per commitment needs a portfolio company row standing for the fund (convert.ts makes one from the deal's company name) or a schema change to core.investment. 2) One sponsor fund can have two commitments (the primary program vehicle and a client vehicle: scenario two_clients_one_fund, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/tools/synthetic/src/generate.ts lines 365 to 394), while a fund-level document is linked to the sponsor fund only, so which commitment a statement is for is not on the document row. A client-directed commitment is under the client rule (SEC-5.2); core.investment is under the wall only. 3) A primary fund position with Locked marks would flow into every reader of positions that does not filter by deal type (NAV series, portfolio list, weekly report, client look-through, reporting packages). The readers that filter today use EQUITY_DEAL_TYPES (controls-suggestion.ts line 155; /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/documents/expected.ts, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/monitoring/board.ts, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/analytics/value-creation.ts). 4) The contributions check of rule 12.1 would have to read approved contributions on the commitment; /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/suggestions/sources.ts reads flows by investment_id only. 5) The eleven seeded fund-level statements are pinned byte for byte by a test (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/14_SYNTHETIC_DATA_SPEC.md line 110); a fund statement that states our ending balance is a new or changed document, and the prompt line about several accounts would change with it (prompt version, evals). 6) Any new rule needs docs/08 section 12 text, a golden fixture and the Python cross-check first (CLAUDE.md rule 12). Rules in play: CLAUDE.md rule 5 (staging and approval), rule 7 (no second table or workflow for the same job), section 4 (ask before changing a table the accounting import or export depends on). docs/03_DATA_MODEL.md lines 48 and 132 speak of fund-level rows and metrics for primaries that no table holds yet. Public standards a decider may check (not consulted here): the IPEV Valuation Guidelines on valuing fund interests from the reported net asset value adjusted for cash flows since, and the ILPA reporting template's capital account lines.

### VS-2: In what order are the sources tried when more than one is on hand for a position and quarter?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md 'Open for the owner' item 2, first sentence (line 104), and 'Options considered' 2 (line 23). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md section 14, 'Sources and their order' (line 120). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/definitions.json key valuationSuggestions.sourceOrder (line 89), whose note calls the settings 'a draft set'.
- **builtDefault:** valuationSuggestions.sourceOrder is capital_account_balance, sponsor_equity_value, held_multiple. suggestionSettings refuses any list that is not the three bases, each exactly once (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts lines 104 to 115). selectSuggestion (lines 881 to 892) takes the first source on hand with a value; when none has one, the first on hand gives the reason; the others sit beside it as cross-checks and nothing is blended. readSources (lines 771 to 856) reads a report by rule 12.2 when the quarter states an enterprise value and by rule 12.3 when it states none, never both, so the order between the two report bases decides nothing today. derivationStep (lines 1128 to 1133) uses the order to mark a replacement as better_source: a statement approved while a report-based suggestion is Open replaces it, and a report approved after a statement changes only the cross-check.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: one list in config in the order built; set aside there: a blend of the sources. Others seen: a different order by deal type (a continuation vehicle against a co-investment); the statement as the only source of a suggestion with reports as cross-checks only; the newer document first.
- **context:** Changing the order is a config edit and no code. Touched: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/definitions.json; /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 12 ('Never blended'); docs/18 section 14; tests /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.test.ts and /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/test/suggestions.test.ts; the preview's /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/preview/routes/suggestionDerivation.ts; the workings card /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/valuations/SuggestionWorkings.tsx. An order by deal type would need a new config shape and a change to suggestionSettings. Public standard a decider may check (not consulted here): IPEV on the sponsor's reported value of our own interest against an estimate built from company figures.

### VS-3: Are the two report rules (12.2 and 12.3) the right set, and is the company's equity value enterprise value less net debt or the equity the report states?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md item 2 (line 104: 'The two report rules are a draft set. Equity is enterprise value less net debt; the other choice is the equity a report states'), Consequences (line 74). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md section 12 intro (line 202, 'A first draft for the owner to change') and rules 12.2 and 12.3 (lines 215 to 217). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 616 ('Rule 12.2 assumes our share of the company is unchanged since the last Locked mark').
- **builtDefault:** impliedStakeMark and heldMultipleMark in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/src/suggestion.ts: S = M0 x E1 / E0, where M0 is our latest Locked mark of an earlier quarter and E is enterprise value less net debt from mon.quarterly_performance (ev, net_debt) for the quarter that mark reports for and for the new quarter. The record's total_equity is never used ('may be book equity'). Rule 12.3 applies only when the new quarter has no enterprise value: EV1 = EBITDA1 x EV0 / EBITDA0, then 12.2. Each missing input gives a reason in a fixed order and no value. The method a draft carries is valuation_method.report_indication. Golden fixture /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/fixtures/30_suggested_mark.json and the Python check /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/suggestion.py. The report on hand is the approved quarter on record whatever wrote it, an approved extraction run or an approved accounting import (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/suggestions/sources.ts lines 337 to 353). The rule assumes our share of the company is unchanged since M0.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: the equity a report states (the total_equity column) in place of enterprise value less net debt. Others seen: scale by the sponsor fund's own fair value of its holding, which the report schema already reads (fund_fair_value, sponsor_ownership_pct, fund_cost in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/extraction_schema.json, none with a target column, so none is on the quarterly record); drop rule 12.3, since a held multiple is the platform's estimate and not a figure the sponsor stated; let the report rules be cross-checks only and never the suggestion; keep as built.
- **context:** Tests first (CLAUDE.md rule 12): any change needs docs/08 section 12 text, fixtures 30 and 31, property tests and the Python cross-check before code. Reading fund_fair_value from the record would need a column on mon.quarterly_performance, a table the accounting import writes; reading it from the approved run's final values (finalValuesOf in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts) would not. Related: VS-5 (cash flows since the mark), VS-6 (the method label), VS-7 (who stands behind a figure of the record). Rule 10: no value without every input. Public standards a decider may check (not consulted here): IPEV Valuation Guidelines on using the sponsor's reported value and on calibrating to a prior mark.

### VS-4: When a statement's own lines do not add up, or its contributions to date differ from the approved contributions on record, is its value suggested with a flag or held back?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md 'Options considered' 4 (line 25), item 3 (line 105), Consequences (line 72: 'None of them blocks'). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md rule 12.1 ('Two checks sit beside the value and change neither it nor its reason', line 211). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md line 577.
- **builtDefault:** statementMark (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/src/suggestion.ts lines 154 to 222) gives the value whatever the two checks say. The checks are worked out when a suggestion is read (suggestionReading, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts lines 942 to 983) and are not stored. The list shows the badges 'Does not tie' and 'Contributions differ', the workings card shows both checks, and the confirmation before accept repeats each raised flag as a sentence (flagBadges and flagSentences in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts lines 229 to 292). Accept is not gated on them: valuationSuggestionMachine asks only for a value and the state of the quarter's versions (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/machines.ts lines 804 to 860). The keys written on the Draft carry the id, basis, value, period, document, page, run, each input and each other source's value, not the result of either check (suggestionDraftKeys, controls-suggestion.ts lines 1161 to 1181), so the two approvers of the valuation see a failed check only by opening the suggestion.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: hold the value back until someone explains it. Others seen: suggest it, and ask for a reason at accept when a check fails; write both check results into the Draft's keys so the lock hash covers them and both approvers read them on the version screen; treat the two checks differently (lines that do not add up point at a reading or a document; contributions far from our record point at the wrong position, a whole vehicle read as ours or a wrong unit); a second, larger threshold above which the value is held back; open a data exception when a check fails.
- **context:** Thresholds: valuationSuggestions.tieToleranceByUnit and contributionsTolerancePct in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/definitions.json (see VS-21). The statement schema reads one net_gain_loss line (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/extraction_schema.json), so a real statement that prints fees, expenses and carried interest as separate lines may fail the five-line tie without being wrong. A gate at accept is a state change: it needs a precondition in the transition table of docs/18 section 14 with rejection tests (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/machines.test.ts, CLAUDE.md rule 14). mon.valuation_suggestion keeps decision_reason for a dismissal only (check constraint, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/migrations/0023_valuation_suggestion.sql line 88), so an accept reason is either a migration on that new table or lives in the audit event. Rule 10 (never invent) and rule 5 (a person approves) are fixed. Pages: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/valuations/Suggestions.tsx, SuggestionWorkings.tsx, SuggestedMarkPanel.tsx, Valuations.tsx (board column), ValuationDetail.tsx. Tests: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/test/suggestions.test.ts, suggestion-commands.test.ts, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.test.ts; the preview route /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/preview/routes/valuationSuggestions.ts. See also VS-15: an accepted wrong share is shown only by these two checks.

### VS-5: When cash has moved on the position since the last Locked mark, does a report-based rule give no value, or roll the scaled mark forward by the flows?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md 'Options considered' 5 (line 26) and item 4 (line 106). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/08_CALCULATIONS_SPEC.md rule 12.2 (reason flows_since_reference_mark, line 215) and 'Which marks and flows count' (line 223).
- **builtDefault:** referenceOf in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/src/suggestion.ts (lines 336 to 352) answers flows_since_reference_mark when hasFlowBetween is true: any approved cash flow of the position dated after the reference mark's own period end and on or before the suggestion's quarter end. Every flow type counts (contribution, distribution, fee, expense, interest, principal, recallable): /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/suggestions/sources.ts passes the dates of all approved flows (lines 247 to 256 and 358). The suggestion is then Open with a reason and no value, and can only be dismissed or superseded. A statement-based suggestion is not affected.
- **alternatives:** Named in the record: roll the scaled mark forward by the flows. Others seen: scale first, then add contributions and take off distributions since the mark; count only the flows that change our stake (a contribution, a return of capital) and let fees, expenses and income pass; give no value only when the flows exceed a configured share of the mark; keep as built.
- **context:** hasFlowBetween has golden fixture /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/fixtures/31_reference_marks.json and a Python twin /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/marks.py. A roll-forward rule is new financial math: docs/08 text, a fixture, property tests and the Python cross-check come first (CLAUDE.md rule 12). navRollForward and qtdGainLoss already exist (docs/08 section 7, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/src/rollforward.ts). Flow types and sign rules: mon.cash_flow in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/migrations/0004_mon.sql lines 177 to 198. Any threshold goes in config (rule 9). The reason sentences are in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts. Related: VS-3 (the unchanged-share assumption) and VS-18 (a flow approved later does not refresh an Open suggestion). Public standards a decider may check (not consulted here): fair value at the sponsor's latest reported net asset value adjusted for cash flows since (IPEV; the net asset value practical expedient of US accounting standards).

### VS-6: Which valuation method does a draft opened from a suggestion carry: the existing 'Sponsor mark' for a statement and a new 'Indication from sponsor report' for a report, or 'Sponsor mark' for both?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0023-valuation-suggestions.md item 5 (line 107). /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/migrations/0023_valuation_suggestion.sql lines 227 to 235. /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/03_DATA_MODEL.md line 85.
- **builtDefault:** SUGGESTION_METHODS in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/src/terms.ts lines 117 to 121: capital_account_balance gives valuation_method.sponsor_mark ('Sponsor mark', an existing term); sponsor_equity_value and held_multiple both give valuation_method.report_indication ('Indication from sponsor report', added by migration 0023, sort order 7). The method is stored on the suggestion and copied to the Draft at accept or applyToDraft; operations can still change it on the Draft through PATCH /valuations/{id}. No code branches on a method code other than the weekly intake writing its own (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/intake/weekly/weekly.service.ts line 87); nothing in apps/api/src/reports, apps/api/src/packages or /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/accounting_layout.json reads it.
