### EE-43: The role work list on Home has links and no counts ('3 valuations wait for you'): what is counted for each line, and where do the counts come from?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md role focus entry (line 494: 'The work list has links, not counts; counts need one small read per list'); /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0021-role-focus.md consequences; /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/09_ROADMAP_AND_BACKLOG.md line 85 ('Counts on the work list are the next step').
- **builtDefault:** config/role_focus.json holds for each role a work list of { to, label, what } (four to seven lines a role); apps/web/src/lib/role-focus.ts reads it and pages/Home.tsx shows 'Your work' as links with one line each. No count is read. Note: config/role_focus.json is being edited in the working tree by the Reporting, Packages work right now.
- **alternatives:** One API read that answers every count for the caller in a single call (each count a query under the caller's row-level security: valuations in the state the role acts on, tickets waiting for a second person, open suggestions, flagged staged rows, exceptions open, packages in review), with the definition of each count written beside its line in config or in code. One small read per list from the page (the record's wording), reusing list endpoints with limit 0 or a count field. Counts from stored tasks once a task hub exists (EE-19). No counts.
- **context:** decision 0021 (the file orders and describes, never grants or hides); a count must be 'what waits for this person', which depends on the transition tables (packages/workflows: which role acts from which state, and segregation of duties: a valuation I prepared does not wait for me) and on walls; CLAUDE.md rule 10 (a count that cannot be read is a placeholder, not 0); docs/17 performance and no-store reads; contract and OpenAPI for a new route; preview recordings per user and the simulated state (a count must follow simulated writes); tests apps/web/src/lib/role-focus.test.ts and Home tests.

### EE-44: Is the arrangement in the role focus file (each role's areas, tab order, home sections, work lines, and which role wins for a person with several) right as a standing default?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0021-role-focus.md (status: 'Accepted for the prototype, as a first arrangement for the owner to change'; 'The arrangement is a first draft made without the firm's people in the room').
- **builtDefault:** config/role_focus.json: version 1, precedence approver, operations, deal_team, investor_relations, auditor, platform_admin, viewer (a person with several roles gets the focus of the first they hold in that list), and per role the area name, a sentence, the ordered areas, tab orders, home sections and work list. apps/web/src/lib/role-focus.ts and apps/web/src/app/nav.ts apply it; nothing is hidden by it. A test checks every path exists, every role is covered and no navigation link is dropped.
- **alternatives:** Keep as built. Let a person with several roles choose their focus (a per-user setting, which needs somewhere to store it). Order by what waits for the person (once counts exist, EE-43) in place of a fixed order. Change single lines in the file.
- **context:** docs/06 section 2 ('Focus never hides a tab; access does'); CLAUDE.md rule 9; the lines were written from the transition tables and contract role lists so none names an action the role cannot take; how the firm's real teams divide work is a fact about the outside world that the file is built to absorb without code; tests apps/web/src/lib/role-focus.test.ts; the site's tour recording shows the older home page (another reader's area).

### EE-45: The synthetic generator does not produce the required scenarios qtd_ytd_basis, sign_flip and units_millions: what should each look like in data, given a reporting package builds its own lines from the record?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md Phase 4 entries (lines 428 and 471); /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/09_ROADMAP_AND_BACKLOG.md line 46 (unchecked); /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/14_SYNTHETIC_DATA_SPEC.md section 3 (the three tags are listed as required); /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/tools/synthetic/src/profiles.ts comment at lines 77 to 82 ('units_millions, qtd_ytd_basis and sign_flip ... are not produced yet and are not listed').
- **builtDefault:** DATA_SCENARIOS and DOCUMENT_SCENARIOS in tools/synthetic/src/profiles.ts list neither tag. The QA engine's tests build both cases by hand (packages/report-engine/src/qa.test.ts lines 159 and 240; snapshot.test.ts line 87). buildPackageSnapshot computes begin NAV from the Locked mark at the prior quarter end and the gain or loss by arithmetic, so a package built by the product cannot carry a wrong quarter basis or a flipped sign; those defects can only enter through figures typed or imported from outside (a sponsor's statement, an accounting or ops-drop file, a hand-made draft). docs/14 words them as 'Data that produces wrong QTD if computed from beginning of year' and 'Gain reported as loss in a draft'. For units, the accounting defects export has a revenue-in-millions row and documents state units, but no item carries the units_millions tag.
- **alternatives:** qtd_ytd_basis as a data property: tag a position whose prior year-end mark differs from its prior quarter-end mark with flows in between, so a year-to-date start would give a visibly wrong quarter, and assert the builder and QA-QTD-01 on it. sign_flip as a document or file defect: a position-level capital account statement whose net gain or loss line has the wrong sign (caught by the statement's own tie check), or an ops-drop row. units_millions as a tag on the existing documents that state US$ millions and US$ thousands. Or amend docs/14 to say which scenarios are engine-level only and need no seeded data. Each new scenario goes in a stream of its own so seed 42 moves only where intended.
- **context:** docs/09 Phase 4 exit ('QA catches every reporting scenario tag in docs/14'); docs/08 section 7 rule table (QA-QTD-01, QA-SIGN-01) and fixtures 25 and 26; docs/12 (golden files); CLAUDE.md rules 1, 10 and 12; tools/synthetic digest tests, verify.ts (a listed tag that is not produced fails the check), the preview recordings and the document truth sidecars (84 documents in the small profile; adding documents changes counts asserted in tests and evals); packages/report-engine is being worked on by another agent (decision 0024).

### EE-46: The static preview ships every mock user's recorded answers and every investment's figures, walled ones included, in files anyone holding the link can open: is that acceptable as a standing design, and what stops it ever being built over real data?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/SESSION_LOG.md Session 5 (line 148), Session 5 close-out (line 174), Session 6 (line 206); /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0008-shared-read-models-and-preview-engine.md consequences ('demonstrates row-level security ... and does not enforce them ... acceptable only because every figure is synthetic; a preview over real data would need a server and real authentication').
- **builtDefault:** apps/web/scripts/build-preview.mjs records each request family for eleven mock users into fixtures.json and writes preview/book.json (the investment directory with company names, aliases, guards, approved quarters, Locked marks, documents and truths) and sample files; apps/web/src/preview/shim.ts answers from them and switches user in the browser. The build runs the employer-data guard over the output, stops if a clear bank detail of the dataset appears, and the production bundle is checked for simulation code. Nothing ties the preview build to a synthetic-only data source other than the recorder running against the seeded in-memory database.
- **alternatives:** Keep as built and add a hard check that the recorder refuses any database that is not a seeded synthetic one (for example a marker row the seed writes, or a refusal when mock identity is off). Record per-user bundles and load only the signed-in user's (still not enforcement). Retire the role switcher from the published demo. Where a link may be shared is the owner's act and belongs to the hosting reader.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md rule 1 and rule 13; docs/10 (data tiers; real data is only ever handled by the deployed app); docs/16 section 5 (no deployments from the prototype); SEC-5.1, SEC-5.3, SEC-17.6; decision 0006 and 0008; decision 0015 (public site and hosting, another reader); tests apps/web/src/preview plan and shim tests, probe-preview.mjs.

### EE-49: Decisions 0001 and 0002 leave the stack and the database engine to be confirmed with IT at merge, and docs/13 still names parts the build did not use: what should the record say now?

- **source:** /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0001-platform-stack.md (status: 'Accepted for the prototype; to be re-confirmed with IT at merge'); /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0002-database-engine.md (status: Proposed; 'Revisit: at merge, with IT'); /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/13_BUILD_APPROACH.md lines 26 to 29; /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0005-phase0-toolchain.md ('ECharts arrives with analytics').
- **builtDefault:** PostgreSQL 16 semantics through PGlite, with row-level security, triggers, enums, tsvector search, advisory locks and security definer functions kept inside packages/db; the firm may prefer Azure SQL. Workflow and jobs: docs/13 names Temporal or Azure Durable Functions; the build runs state machines in packages/workflows inside API transactions and drives extraction batches inline (no engine, no durable timers). Document and Office workers: docs/13 names Python with python-pptx, python-docx and openpyxl; the build parses PDFs and workbooks in TypeScript (packages/pdf, packages/xlsx) and apps/worker-py holds only the calculation cross-check. Charts: docs/13 and decision 0005 name ECharts; the build draws its own SVG chart kit (apps/web/src/components/charts) and ECharts is not installed. AG Grid Community is installed and used on the portfolio grid.
- **alternatives:** Amend docs/13 and decision 0005 to what is built and mark the engine, the workflow engine and the Office workers as production choices behind adapters. Add a decision record for 'no workflow engine in the prototype' and for 'own SVG charts'. Mark decision 0002 Accepted for the prototype with the list of Postgres-only features and what an Azure SQL move would cost. Which engine and which hosted services the firm will approve is a fact only the owner and IT have; the build can only keep the choice open.
- **context:** CLAUDE.md section 1 (stack in docs/13), rule 7 ('record new components in docs/decisions'), rule 8; docs/02 architecture; docs/10 section 4 checklist (IT approval of the stack, gate G0); docs/decisions/README.md (records required for stack and engine choices); the reporting template engine of Phase 4 must write pptx, docx and xlsx and will force the Office worker question (EE-9); durable timers are assumed by docs/18 section 10 for reminders (EE-19).

### EE-50: Decision 0003 asks that Anthropic's current documentation on data retention and session limits be checked again before gate G0 and the date recorded: has it been, and what does it say?

The observed Python is 3.13 where the stack doc said 3.12, handled as '3.12 or later'.

### EE-51: Which standing 'owner' items are acts only the owner can perform outside the repository?

## practice:base-case

PRACTICE LENS. Deal performance against the sponsor's underwritten base case, BC-1 to BC-27.

HOW THIS WAS CHECKED
- Read only. Nothing in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach was created, edited or run. No file was written anywhere.
- Read: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/decisions/0022-underwritten-base-case.md and 0023-valuation-suggestions.md, docs/08 sections 1 to 11, docs/18 (all), docs/14 section 12, docs/04 M9, docs/SESSION_LOG.md lines 495 to 653, packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts and credit.ts and pooled.ts, packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts, apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts and comparison.ts, apps/api/src/performance/performance.service.ts, apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts and UnderwritingCard.tsx, packages/workflows/src/machines.ts, packages/db/migrations/0001, 0006, 0021, tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts, verify.ts, settings.ts, config/definitions.json, config/closing_checklist.json.
- Seeded figures were recomputed from /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/tools/synthetic/.synthetic/dataset.json (small profile, seed 42, as of 2025-06-30) with a read-only script.
- Public sources: web search only. ilpa.org and www.privateequityvaluation.com could not be opened from this session (they are outside the session's allowed network domains and would have to be allowed in the network settings). So what is said below about the wording of ILPA and IPEV texts rests on search summaries and professional memory. A decision record should name the standard and not quote it until the text has been opened.
- Standards relied on: IPEV Valuation Guidelines (the December 2025 edition, in effect for reporting periods beginning on or after 1 April 2026; the December 2022 edition before that): calibration of inputs to the entry price, and earnings and multiples on a like basis. ILPA quarterly reporting (Reporting Template and Performance Template, January 2025; the portfolio company reporting template, now being refreshed), under which a sponsor reports each company's twelve-month revenue, EBITDA and net debt and how it stands against plan, and gross and net returns are kept apart. The four-eyes (maker and checker) control for reference data. Private credit reporting practice of naming a yield by its basis (at cost, at fair value).

SUMMARY
- Confirmed as built, record changes from open to decided: BC-3, BC-14, BC-15, BC-16, BC-21, BC-22, BC-23, BC-27. BC-10 and BC-11 are confirmed with a change of words only.
- To build: BC-1, BC-2 (words), BC-4, BC-5, BC-6 (net debt only), BC-7 (realized positions only), BC-8 with BC-20 as one dataset change, BC-9, BC-12, BC-13, BC-17, BC-18, BC-19, BC-26.
- None is an owner-only question. Two acts are the owner's: approving and merging pull requests (BC-24) and approving the publish (BC-25).
- Suggested order: BC-26, then the rules (BC-6, BC-7, BC-8 tie check, BC-13, BC-19, BC-9), then BC-1, BC-2, BC-4, BC-5, BC-12, then BC-17 and BC-18, then the dataset (BC-8, BC-20), then BC-25.
- What a careful team never automates, and none of the choices below does: confirming a base case; changing a recorded plan figure so that it ties or matches a basis; deciding that the original plan has stopped being a fair yardstick; the overall rating of a position (the committee's judgment); putting a mark, a cash flow or a client figure on record.

Every new threshold named below is a key of `underwriting` (or `watchlist`) in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/definitions.json with no default in code. Every value given is a starting value the firm tunes; the system is correct whatever the value.

----------------------------------------------------------------
BC-1. Which case is compared by default
Choice: both, with one judged. The entry case stays the default and is the only case whose status travels (card header, watchlist, roll-up). When a later Confirmed re-underwriting exists the card shows the position against it as well, beside the entry case. The reader's pick goes into the address. No config key for the default: it is a rule, not a threshold.
Why: the case approved at investment committee is the yardstick a limited partner or co-investor holds a deal to. Re-basing to the latest plan is the usual way underperformance leaves a report, so a well-run team never lets a revision replace the original. It reads "against original underwriting" and "against current plan" side by side at the quarterly portfolio review. This is also the logic of IPEV calibration: the entry price and the entry case are the fixed point, and later inputs are carried forward from it. The latest plan is the better forward view and the one a valuation leans on, which is why it is shown and not hidden.
Build:
1. The read answers a second comparison when a Confirmed case dated later than the entry case exists (the same `underwritingComparison`, the latest Confirmed case): a field such as `latest: { version, comparison } | null` in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts, and one more line on each tile. No new math.
2. The pick lives in the address (`?case=N`) in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingCard.tsx.
3. An honest label. `underwritingKindOf` says "entry" only when the case date is within `underwriting.entryCaseMaxDaysFromEntry` (start at 120) of the position's entry date. Otherwise the label is "Earliest case on record, dated ..." and the card adds "No case dated at entry is on record."
4. Let an original case be recorded late. `isCaseDateAllowed` should also accept a date earlier than every Confirmed case, from version 2 with its reason. Today the only route is to retire the later case, record the earlier one and record the later one again. docs/18 section 13, the machine tests and the guards move with it. On confirmation the default moves to the new case and the audit event says so.
5. When a Confirmed case with an earlier date was retired, the card says so: "The entry case was withdrawn on <date>: <reason>. Compared with the case dated <date>."
6. No silent switch. When the entry plan has ended and a later plan still runs, the entry tiles say "After the plan ends" and the later case's line carries the operating figures.
At run time: the system picks the judged case, labels it truthfully, shows both and says when the entry case is missing or withdrawn. A person records and confirms a re-underwriting, and a person alone judges that the original plan is no longer a fair operating yardstick (a large add-on, a merger, a restructuring). That judgment is why both are on the card.
Context check: correct. One addition: the seeded `underwriting_reunderwritten` example sits on a realized position (entered 2016-11-06, exited 2020-07-06; entry case 3.17x and 26.54% to 2021-09-30; re-underwriting dated 2019-06-30 at 3.31x and 16.92% to 2024-06-30). The return yardstick differs by 9.6 points between the two cases, which is the argument for showing both. For the demo it should sit on a held position (BC-8).

BC-2. The plan between two plan dates
Choice: as built. A straight line by days, with the status kept on it. Change the words only: say "straight line" on the tile itself, and carry the flag wherever the status goes.
Why: revenue and EBITDA are twelve-month figures. A straight line between two year-end twelve-month figures is the same as spreading the year's change evenly over its quarters, which is how an annual budget is pro-rated when the sponsor gives no quarterly phasing. Monitoring is quarterly; a comparison on plan dates only leaves three quarters in four with no reading, and drift between annual points is what monitoring is for. Where the sponsor's model has quarterly points the case already takes them and the line is not used. A constant growth line changes almost nothing: in worked example 1 of docs/08 the plan EBITDA at 2024-06-30 is 43,640,000 on the straight line and about 43,486,000 on a growth line, so the variance is -7.2% or -6.9% and the status is the same. The straight line can be checked by hand. Net debt is a balance and can be seasonal, so its line is weaker; it carries the same flag.
Build: `metricTile` in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts reads `row.interpolated` and adds "on a straight line between plan dates, not a sponsor figure" to the hint. The watchlist reason (BC-17) and the roll-up row (BC-18) carry the same flag. docs/08 rule 1 stands.
At run time: the system works out the figure, judges it and says what it is. Nothing goes to a person. Never automate: stating an interpolated figure as the sponsor's in anything that leaves the firm.
Context check: correct.

BC-3. Second person on the record, or staging and approval
Choice: as built, decided. A second person confirms on the record; no staging table; the confirmer is a deal team member or an approver and never the person who last changed the figures. Do not narrow the confirmer to approvers.
Why: this is the four-eyes control every well-run operations team uses for reference data. Staging, approval and a lock are for the firm's own figures: marks, cash and what a client is sent. A base case is a yardstick, not a figure of record: it goes to no accounting system, administrator or client statement, and no valuation committee approves a sponsor's plan. The right checker is a second member of the deal team, who holds the model and the committee memo; operations usually cannot check a sponsor's model, and making approvers do data checking slows entry for no added control. SEC-9.1 is met by Draft (staging), the checks, the second person (approval) and Confirmed (the only state compared). Its weakest leg today is validation, which is only the revenue bounds: BC-8 and BC-13 make that leg real.
If a base case figure ever reaches a client package, the control over what the client sees is the package's own gate (QA, review, approval, release), not a staging table for the base case.
Build: nothing for the control. The checks of BC-4, BC-8 and BC-13 are shown on the Draft before the Confirm button, and the confirm audit event records which flags were showing, so the trail shows what the second person saw.
At run time: the system refuses the same person, runs the checks and shows them. A person confirms. Never automate the confirmation, whatever the source and however well the case ties.
Context check: correct.

BC-4. When a base case is entered, and what a missing one does
Choice: entry stays after the position exists and blocks nothing. Add the chase: the system lists a held equity, continuation vehicle or credit position with no Confirmed case `underwriting.missingAfterDays` (start at 45) after entry, and a Draft that has waited `underwriting.draftWaitingDays` (start at 10) for its second person.
Why: the base case is fixed at investment committee and often restated at closing for the final price, capital structure and our allocation. The closing case is the one to track, and loading it is a post-closing onboarding task due before the first quarterly review. A closing is a legal and funding event; nobody holds one for a monitoring record. What a well-run team does not accept is a position that sits for a year with no yardstick and nobody told.
Build: no job and no stored exception. The list is a cohort of the roll-up of BC-18 ("no base case", "draft only"), worked out on read under the reader's own access, and one line in the deal team's attention list on the home page. The first case's date is compared with the entry date (BC-1, item 3) and the gap is shown to the confirmer. Optional and config only: an item "Sponsor base case model" with `required: false` in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/closing_checklist.json, taken only if the dataset is being regenerated anyway (it adds an item to every seeded closing). Not now: recording the case on the opportunity, and reading a Draft out of a model. If a model is ever read by extraction, the result fills the form for a person who becomes `entered_by`, and a second person confirms: two people, never the system and one person.
At run time: the system finds what is missing or waiting and lists it. A person records and confirms.
Context check: correct.

BC-5. What counts as the source
Choice: either form stays, but the system decides which is enough. When the position holds a document that is the sponsor's model or the underwriting memo, and the person confirming can open it, the case must name a document, with a page or a sheet and cell. Otherwise a note is accepted, the card says the source is a note, and the confirmer is told there is no document to open.
Why: evidence for reference data is a pointer a second person can follow to the figures. A note that a model sits in a deal folder is acceptable when nothing is on the platform, because the second person attests that they opened it. Once the document is on the platform a note is no longer good enough, and SEC-9.4 asks for document and page.
Build: the rule must key on an exact document type or it will make people cite the wrong document. None of the present types fits (CIM, management presentation, closing set, valuation support). So: one taxonomy term set by a person in the document hub (the classifier need not learn it), listed in `underwriting.sourceDocumentTypes`; the `confirm` precondition in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/machines.ts and the service refuse a note-only case when such a document of the position is visible to the caller, and ask for a page or locator when a document is named. Small; low urgency while the seed has no documents. If the build would rather not add a type now, what is built is acceptable and the rule above is recorded as the rule from the day real documents exist.
At run time: the system decides which source is required from the documents on hand. A person names the place and a second person opens it.
Context check: correct. Every seeded case uses a note, so only tests exercise the document path.

BC-6. The tolerances and their shape
Choice: 5% for revenue and EBITDA and 2 points for the IRR stand as the decided starting values. One share for revenue and EBITDA is right. A share of plan net debt is the wrong shape for net debt: judge net debt by its distance from plan in turns of plan EBITDA.
Why: no public standard sets a number; ILPA's reporting asks for an assessment against plan and leaves the band to the firm. Bands of 5% to 10% on EBITDA and 2 to 3 points on IRR are common, so the values are sound and the firm tunes them. Net debt is different because everyone who lends to or owns these companies reads debt in turns of EBITDA. A percent of plan net debt gives false alarms on a small base: plan net debt 20,000,000 against plan EBITDA 50,000,000, actual net debt 24,000,000, reads 20% over plan and "behind", for a difference of 0.08 turns. In worked example 1 of docs/08 the same rule gives 3,640,000 / 43,640,000 = 0.08 turns, on plan, as today. It also gives a plan in net cash a status, where today there is none.
Build (rule 12 order: docs/08 rule 3, fixture 27, the Python cross-check, then code): net debt favourable figure = -(actual - plan) / plan EBITDA at the same date; on plan within `underwriting.netDebtToleranceTurns` (start at 0.25, the step lenders use). No status when plan EBITDA at the date is missing or not positive, with the reason. The percent of plan net debt stays on the tile as information. Not taken: a band that narrows with time, an uneven band, tying to `watchlist.ebitdaYoYDeclinePct` (a different measure). Remove "a draft" from the config note.
One coupling to break: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts judges the seeded scenarios with the config tolerances while the generator's shifts are constants (8 points of growth, 5 points of IRR). A tolerance edit can therefore fail the dataset check. The generator should take its shifts from the tolerances (tolerance plus a margin), or the dataset should record the tolerances it was built for.
At run time: the system judges every figure by the config values and returns the values it used. No person is asked. The values are the firm's to edit.
Context check: correct.

BC-7. Gross MOIC before the exit
Choice: as built for a held position: the path at the underwritten IRR over our own flows, with no status. One change: a realized position's MOIC gap is judged.
Why: for a held position the MOIC gap and the IRR gap always have the same sign (the gross MOIC is above the path MOIC exactly when the IRR is above the underwritten IRR), so a second badge would add nothing, and setting a young position against the exit multiple would read every one as behind. For a realized position the two can disagree, and practice looks at both: an early exit can beat the IRR and return less money than planned. Example: underwritten 2.07x and 20% over four years; realized after two years at 1.60x. The IRR is 26.5%, 6.5 points ahead, and the card badges "Ahead of plan"; the MOIC is 0.47x short, 23% under, and carries no badge. A reader who skims badges is told the wrong story.
Build (rule 12 order): docs/08 rule 5, fixture 28 and the Python `returns`: for a realized position, MOIC status by `planStatus` over (gross MOIC - underwritten MOIC) / underwritten MOIC and `underwriting.moicTolerance` (start at 0.05). Contract `moicStatus`, null while held. `moicTile` shows it for a realized position only.
At run time: the system judges. No person.
Context check: correct. The missing agreement check between the underwritten MOIC, the IRR and the dates is BC-8.

BC-8. The seeded examples, and whether a case's own figures tie
Choice: yes to both. Rebuild the three status scenarios once, in the same dataset change as BC-20. Add a product check that shows where a case does not tie, as a flag and never a block.
Why: a practitioner opens the "ahead" card and sees a base case whose EBITDA falls. No sponsor underwrites that, and the demo loses the reader. Verified in the seed 42 small dataset: the "ahead" plan runs 16.43m to 14.76m (-2.65% a year) with 1.70x and 14.65% underwritten, while its own exit pays 1.09x; "on plan" is underwritten at 5.76% (nobody underwrites a buyout co-investment at 6% gross) and 1.47x against a plan exit of 1.64x; "behind" 1.61x against 2.15x. And the check the second person does by hand is exactly arithmetic the system can do.
What a plausible base case looks like (the generator's rule): revenue growing, margin flat or up, net debt falling, exit multiple within about a turn of entry, MOIC = (exit multiple x exit EBITDA - exit net debt) / entry equity value, IRR = the rate that MOIC stands for over the dates, as the ordinary cases already do. For the status scenarios: floors on plan growth (3% a year) and on the underwritten IRR (12%), hosts chosen because their actuals sit ahead of, on or behind such a plan, and the returns derived from the plan, not written over it. The tag is judged on the EBITDA headline; the IRR reads as it reads ("EBITDA ahead, IRR behind" is common and a good demo). If no host can be ahead of a plausible plan, give the three scenarios positions of their own, drawn last, as BC-20 does. Put the re-underwritten example on a held position.
The product check (new rule 7 of docs/08 section 11, fixture, Python cross-check first; answered on each version by the read):
- Plan against MOIC (equity, needs the entry snapshot and the exit point): plan MOIC as above against the MOIC entered, flagged beyond `underwriting.tie.moicTolerance` (start at 0.15; management equity, fees and interim cash make real cases differ by up to that).
- MOIC against IRR and dates: equity, the rate MOIC^(365/days) - 1 from our entry to the planned exit against the IRR entered, flagged beyond `underwriting.tie.irrPoints` (start at 0.02), with words by direction (lower cannot be right; higher implies cash back before the exit). A loan: the MOIC should lie between 1 + IRR x years (all cash pay) and (1 + IRR)^years (all PIK).
Proof that it works: the seeded `underwriting_corrected` mistake (exit multiple typed three turns high) gives a plan exit of 3.94x against 2.85x entered, 38% apart, flagged; the corrected version gives 2.85x and ties.
At run time: the system computes and shows the flags to the person confirming and on the card. A person decides whether the case is right. Never automate: changing a figure so the case ties, or refusing a case that does not (a real one may not).
Context check: figures confirmed. Additions: the forward-dated snapshot position carries a case dated 2019-04-17 whose plan runs 2025-12-31 to 2031-12-31 (a 12.7 year hold at 9.17%), an artefact of that scenario that the date check of BC-1 would flag; and the re-underwritten example is on a realized position.

BC-9. Which value the yield to maturity is priced on
Choice: keep the present figure, named for what it is (at the reported fair value), and add a second figure, at our own mark, when a Locked mark of ours for the same period end differs from the reported fair value. Both come from the one function, both show on all three pages, neither is judged.
Why: private credit reporting names a yield by its basis (at cost, at fair value) because one loan has several. Our Locked mark is the value our committee approved and the one our IRR, MOIC and NAV use, so the yield beside them should be on that basis; the manager's reported value is the manager's and is the cross-check. Showing only one hides either our view or the source. Not judging is right: the yield rises as value falls. What credit teams judge is the IRR against underwriting, the mark as a share of par, payment and covenant status, which the card and the credit watchlist already do.
Build: additive, so no existing figure changes and the objection in the session log falls away. `creditYieldToMaturity` in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/performance/performance.service.ts answers the reported yield as now and `atOurMark`: the same `yieldToMaturity` with our latest Locked mark in place of the fair value, only when the mark's period end is the credit row's (so par is of that date, never another period's, rule 10) and the two values differ. Contract: one more nullable field on the credit terms detail and on the comparison. docs/08 section 10 and rule 6 first. Example for the fixture: par 10,000,000, 10% paid yearly, three years to maturity; reported at par the yield is 10.00%; at our mark of 9,000,000 it is about 14.3%, while the IRR to date is 5.12% against 10%, behind.
At run time: the system shows the second yield whenever the two values differ. No person.
Context check: correct. Additions: in the seed the Locked marks of both loans equal the reported fair values to the cent, so nothing shows the difference today (BC-20). One seeded loan is marked at 18,837,699.89 under the method "par plus accrued" while par plus accrued is 18,200,513.56, so the method label does not describe the number.

BC-10. Primary funds
Choice: none, as built, and the page says so in one line.
Why: a fund commitment is a blind pool. There is no sponsor plan for one company to track. What a limited partner tracks a fund against is its own underwriting (target net IRR and net multiple), its pacing plan for calls and distributions, and its vintage peers. That is a different feature under a different name, and it waits on two things the platform does not have: a mark on a commitment (decision 0023, question 1) and net returns of a position (BC-15).
Build: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/deal/UnderwritingCard.tsx renders one line for `form: null` in place of nothing, for example "A primary fund holds no sponsor base case: it is a blind pool with no company plan." Decision 0022 records the two preconditions of a fund form.
At run time: the system decides from the deal type. No person.
Context check: correct in effect. `underwritingFormOf` reaches the list through `coversDealType` and `QUARTERLY_REPORTING_DEAL_TYPES`, which is `EQUITY_DEAL_TYPES`: the form follows which positions report quarterly company figures, which is the right dependency. A multi-asset continuation vehicle is sound on the equity form only while its quarterly figures are the vehicle's aggregate; the small seed has no such position.

BC-11. The outbox sentence
Choice: one decision for every machine: correct the text now to say what is true, and build the writer in one place when the first consumer needs it.
Why: the control an auditor tests is that no state changes without an audit event in the same transaction, and that the people who must act are told. Both hold: every transition is audited, and the alert to prior approvers on a reopen goes through `ops.task`. An event row nobody reads is not a control. A procedures document that says something the system does not do is a finding.
Build: docs/18 line 3, packages/workflows/README.md, docs/17 section 5 and docs/02: a transition writes `audit.event` in its transaction; outbox events are not written yet. Alternative set aside: writing rows with no reader. Trigger: the first consumer a read cannot serve (a pushed alert through the Microsoft 365 integration, for instance).
At run time: nothing changes.
Context check: correct. No file under apps or packages/workflows writes `ops.outbox`.

BC-12. One overall verdict
Choice: no blended score and no merged verdict. Two named readings, kept apart: operating (EBITDA against plan) and returns (gross IRR against underwriting, and for a realized position the gross MOIC). `headline` is the one judged figure of the form: EBITDA for equity, and it should also be the IRR status for a loan. Show the headline as a named badge in the card header so the field is used.
Why: weights would be invented. "The weakest decides" hides which thing is wrong: EBITDA ahead with the IRR behind (a lower multiple in our mark) is a different matter from EBITDA behind. The single rating of a position is the deal lead's judgment at the portfolio review, approved by the committee; the sponsor's assessment against plan under ILPA reporting is likewise a judgment. The system supplies the readings.
Build: card header badge ("EBITDA: behind plan"). For a loan, `headline` = the IRR status (docs/08 rules 3 and 6, fixture 28, Python `headline`). The watchlist and the roll-up read the named readings.
At run time: the system gives readings. A person rates the position; never automate that.
Context check: correct. No page reads `headline`; only the dataset check does.

BC-13. A difference of basis between the model and the reports
Choice: detect and show. Never adjust.
Why: a sponsor's model usually runs on adjusted or pro forma EBITDA (add-backs, run-rate savings from combining businesses) and the quarterly report on a narrower figure. IPEV asks for earnings and multiples on a like basis, and the same holds for plan against actual. A gap of basis reads as "behind plan" from the first quarter and nobody learns why. The gap at entry can be established from data on hand.
Build: when the plan holds a point on the entry snapshot's date, compare its EBITDA, revenue and net debt with the snapshot on record; beyond `underwriting.tie.entryFigureTolerance` (start at 0.02) show it to the confirmer and on the card: "The plan starts from EBITDA of 45.0m; the entry snapshot on record is 40.0m. The plan and the reported figures may be on different bases." With no point on that date: "Not checked: the plan has no figure on the entry snapshot date." It is part of the tie rule of BC-8 (same spec text, fixture and cross-check). The progress table carries the note, because the planned bridge starts from the snapshot: with entry EBITDA 40, EV 400 and a planned exit EBITDA of 80, planned EBITDA growth is 400m from the snapshot and 311m on the plan's own start of 45, so progress of 250m reads 62.5% or 80.4%. A basis label on the case is not needed now.
At run time: the system finds and shows the difference. A person decides what it means. Never automate: rescaling the plan (rule 10) or choosing the right basis.
Context check: correct.

BC-14. Other currencies
Choice: as built, decided. Refuse loudly.
Why: docs/03 keeps the prototype in US dollars. The rule for later, as practice has it: a plan is compared with actuals in the currency the company reports in, never converted (a rate move would read as an operating variance), and returns are in the currency the position is held in.
Build: none. Context check: correct.

BC-15. Net returns
Choice: as built, decided. Gross only.
Why: a sponsor's base case for a co-investment is gross at the deal level, and gross against gross is the like comparison. ILPA's Performance Template keeps gross and net apart for the same reason. Net depends on our vehicle's fee and carry terms and is a feature of its own. Do not add net fields that nothing computes or checks.
Build: none. Context check: correct.

BC-16. A downside case
Choice: as built, decided. One base case.
Why: the owner asked for the base case, and monitoring against the base case is the norm. If a downside is added later it is a labelled scenario drawn beside the base case, and the base case stays the one judged.
Build: none. Context check: correct.

BC-17. A watchlist flag
Choice: yes, by rule, for equity and continuation vehicle positions. None for loans.
Why: EBITDA well under plan is a standard watchlist trigger for sponsors, lenders and co-investors. A system that knows a position is 20% under plan and leaves it off the watchlist is not doing the work. The threshold must be wider than "behind": most positions sit under a sponsor's base case, so the 5% band would flood the list. Loans are already covered by the credit rules (coverage, payment status, maturity, markdown), and a loan's IRR gap moves with our own mark, which the markdown rule already catches.
Build: `watchlist.ebitdaBehindPlanPct` (start at 0.15): flagged when the EBITDA variance of the current quarter against the entry case (BC-1) is below minus that share. Example from docs/08: 40.5m against 43.64m is -7.2%, behind and not flagged; 36.0m would be -17.5%, flagged. The reason text says when the plan figure is a straight line (BC-2). No flag without a status (no case, draft only, plan ended, missing figure): those are cohorts of BC-18, never "on plan". Worked out in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/analytics/analytics.service.ts with the same `underwritingComparison`, over rows loaded in bulk, under the reader's access so a wall holds.
At run time: the system flags. A person decides what to do about the position. It writes nothing to a financial record.
Context check: correct.

BC-18. A roll-up across positions
Choice: yes, in Analytics, for internal use. Not in a client package.
Why: "performance against underwriting" is a standing page of a portfolio review: each position with its case, EBITDA against plan and returns against underwriting, and how much of the book is ahead of, on and behind plan.
Build: one row per position (case compared, EBITDA variance and status, straight-line flag, IRR gap and status, for a realized position the MOIC status), counts and NAV by each reading, and explicit cohorts: no base case, draft only, entry plan ended, not calculable by reason, loans (IRR reading), realized. A position without a status is counted in its cohort, never as on plan. The roll-up is a function in `@pb/calc` with its fixture and the property that it adds up over any split, as the value creation roll-up does. Each reader's roll-up is over what they can see. A client line would be a separate decision, under the package gate.
At run time: the system counts. No person.
Context check: correct.

BC-19. Why a loan's yield is missing
Choice: yes, a reason from the one function, on both cards.
Why: a held loan past its maturity is a credit event, and "Not calculable this quarter" hides it; the words are also untrue, since the quarter is not the cause. The seed has the case: a held loan with maturity 2024-10-22 as of 2025-06-30.
Build: `creditYieldToMaturity` answers a reason: `past_maturity`, `no_priced_quarter`, `non_positive_fair_value`, `unknown_payment_frequency`, `no_single_rate`. Contract, docs/08 section 10, the credit fixture and the Python check move with it. Nearby gap: `irrReasonText` words every IRR reason other than a short hold and no valuation as "The cash flows give no single IRR", which is the wrong sentence for too few flows.
At run time: the system says why. No person.
Context check: nearly correct. "Every other missing figure has a reason code" is not quite true: a missing gross MOIC borrows the IRR flag and has no reason of its own.

BC-20. A loan marked below par in the data
Choice: yes, one, as a scenario position of its own drawn last, in the same dataset change as BC-8.
Why: a credit book with every loan at or above the reported value is not a real book, and this is the one card that shows what BC-9 and the IRR rule are for: our mark at about 90% of par, the reported value near par, the IRR behind, two yields. The fixture and the API test prove the rule; the demo needs the card.
Build: the new position moves book totals and the dataset digests once; BC-8 moves them too, so do both together and then BC-25.
Context check: correct.

BC-21. Revenue bounds in the form
Choice: as built, decided. The API is the one place.
Why: the common entry error (millions typed as dollars) is caught twice already: the review step shows every figure in full, and the refusal reads in the form's terms. If wanted later, return the bounds with the read beside `thresholds`; the web app must never hold its own copy.
Build: none. Context check: correct.

BC-22. `entered_at` in the database
Choice: as built, decided.
Why: the control rests on `entered_by`, which the database enforces. The history table and the audit event already carry the database's time for every change. The clock is injected by rule.
Build: none. Context check: correct.

BC-23. Seed 14 on the small profile
Choice: as built, decided. Refusing is right.
Why: a dataset that cannot carry its scenarios should fail its check, not bend one. Nothing depends on that seed.
Build: none. Context check: correct.

BC-24. Opening the work for review
Choice: the build opens stacked pull requests in commit order, one feature at a time, each split by layer with the rules first (docs/08 text, calc, fixtures, Python check), then record and machine, API, pages and preview, data and documents. Approving and merging is the owner's act.
Why: the reviewer's financial judgment is needed on the rules, so they should be readable apart from the pages.
Context check: correct.

BC-25. Republishing the preview and the media
Choice: once, after this theme's changes (BC-8 and BC-20 change what the cards show), with a walkthrough for the base case added to the preview guide. The build prepares the preview build, the probe and the media run; the publish runs in GitHub Actions on the owner's approval, which is the owner's act.
Context check: correct.

BC-26. History tables and the wall
Choice: yes. One migration gives the history table of every walled record the visibility of its base table, with the access matrix to match. The build may take it: it narrows who reads and adds no role.
Why: an information barrier covers every copy of the data, history included. Operations, approvers and auditors outside a wall reading a walled position's past row images is a finding in any compliance review, route or no route. When audit needs to see behind a wall, a named person is added to it and the addition is logged.
Build: for the two base case tables the clause reads the row image, not the live row (plan points are deleted when a Draft is edited): `pb.investment_visible((row_image->>'investment_id')::uuid)` and `pb.underwriting_case_visible((row_image->>'case_id')::uuid)`, with the role test kept. Decision 0022's SEC note is corrected to say what was true and what is true now.
At run time: the database decides. No person.
Context check: correct. The matrix also shows the reverse oddity: a wall member who is not in one of the three roles reads no history at all.

BC-27. The recorded differences, and the word "draft"
Choice: all five stand as built, and the record changes from draft to decided in the places listed (decision 0022 status and its open list, docs/08 section 11, the config note, the decisions index, docs/09 and docs/11).
Why, on the one that could be argued: retiring a Confirmed case with one approver and a reason is acceptable because it creates no figure and is audited, provided the removal is visible. BC-1 item 5 makes the card say a case was withdrawn, and BC-18 counts the position as having no standing case, so a retirement cannot quietly remove a "behind plan" reading. Rates typed in percent are how people type them; the review step and the tie check of BC-8 catch 0.2 typed for 20.
Build: record only.
Context check: correct.

Sources (web search results used; the two standards sites could not be opened from this session):
- [KPMG: IPEV 2025](https://kpmg.com/dp/en/insights/2026/01/ipev-2025.html)

- [Houlihan Capital: 2025 IPEV Valuation Guidelines](https://www.houlihancapital.com/2025-ipev-valuation-guidelines/)

- [ILPA: combined overview of the templates](https://ilpa.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/Combined-Overview-ILPA-Templates.pdf)
- [Mondaq: ILPA publishes updated Reporting Template and new Performance Template](https://www.mondaq.com/uk/corporate-and-company-law/1587188/ilpa-publishes-updated-reporting-template-and-new-performance-template)

## control:base-case

CONTROL REVIEW: deal performance against the sponsor's underwritten base case (BC-1 to BC-27)

Read only. Nothing in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach was created, changed or run. Every path below is under /home/user/Portfolio-Beach.

FINDINGS THAT ARE NOT IN THE COLLECTED CONTEXT (each is used in the sections below)

F1. The database lets one deal team member do what docs/18 section 13 gives to an approver or to the system. Policy `move_case` (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/migrations/0021_underwriting_case.sql) admits deal_team and approver for every update, and trigger `mon.guard_underwriting_case` checks states and figures but never the role per move. So at the database line: a deal team member can retire a Confirmed case (the access matrix says so: "withdraw the confirmed base case of the open investment", allowed deal_team, approver, wall_member, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts line 649); a deal team member or approver can set a Confirmed case to Superseded with no correction behind it (nothing in the trigger asks for one); an approver can edit the terms of a Draft (MOIC, IRR, exit multiple) as long as they name themselves `entered_by`. The API and the machine refuse all three. SEC-5.1 asks for the same rule at the data layer, and the valuation suggestion table already does it ("two update policies judge the row as it will be", migration 0023).
F2. Nothing in the base case create or read asks the currency of the position. `UnderwritingService.create` selects id, number and deal type only; the case is USD by constraint, but `core.investment.currency` is never read, while the suggestions bar reads it (`not_usd`, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts line 221). A USD plan can be recorded on a position held in another currency and compared with its quarters. docs/03 section 1 says mixed-currency math must fail loudly.
F3. `ops.outbox` has no row in the access matrix at all (a search of /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/db/src finds it only in schema/ops.ts and classification.ts), its payload is classified restricted_mnpi, and policy `insert_any` lets every signed-in user insert any row. The collected context for BC-11 says a matrix row exists. It does not.
F4. `audit.event` is read by role alone (auditor, approver, operations, platform_admin; migration 0006 line 214). The audit route (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/audit/audit.service.ts) answers action, entity, entity id, actor name and reason for every event, walled records included, so those four roles outside a wall learn that a walled position's base case was created, retired or opened, by whom, with the free-text reason. At the database the `details` column also holds the terms (`termsOf`: MOIC, IRR, exit multiple, yield, investment number). This is the same gap as BC-26, one table over, and it reaches an API route, which the history tables do not.
F5. Decision 0022 line 61 says the straight line "is flagged wherever it is used". It is not: `metricTile` (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts) never reads `row.interpolated`, and the badge is given the same way on a line figure as on a sponsor figure.
F6. `underwritingKindOf` (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts) counts only Confirmed and Superseded versions as having stood. Once an approver retires the entry case, the next earliest Confirmed case has no earlier case that "stood", so it becomes kind `entry` and the card labels a re-underwriting "Entry case". One person's act changes the plan a position is judged by, and the label hides it.

OWNER ONLY, after applying rule (e): two acts and one fact. Approving and merging pull requests, and naming the second human reviewer SEC-17.3 asks for on the row-level security and audit layers (BC-24). Approving the publish workflow run (BC-25). Nothing else in this theme is owner only.

----------------------------------------------------------------
BC-1. Which case is compared by default
Choice: the entry case stays the default and the only case that is judged. Decided, as built, with two fixes to what the card says.
Rules: docs/08 section 11 takes exactly one case, so "both judged" is not open. Rule 10 forbids borrowing a figure, so the default must be stated, not implied. SEC-9.2 keeps every version.
Reasons: a default of "latest" lets a re-underwriting reset the bar, and a revision takes only two deal team members. The first underwriting is the fixed yardstick; this is the same idea as calibration and backtesting in the IPEV Valuation Guidelines (later judgements are tested against the entry position). A later case stays one pick away.
Change to build:
1. Label honesty (F6). In /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts let a retired case count as having stood: a Withdrawn version with `confirmedBy` set was confirmed and then retired (the trigger keeps `confirmed_by` on a retirement). `UnderwritingVersionRef` gains `wasConfirmed`. Then a re-underwriting stays "Re-underwritten <date>" after the entry case is retired, and the card adds one line: "The case dated <date> was retired on <date>: <reason>. Compared with the earliest case that stands."
2. Entry date check. The system holds `core.investment.entry_date`. When the earliest standing case is dated more than `underwriting.entryCaseMaxDaysAfterEntry` (new config key, no default in code) after the entry date, the label is "Earliest case on record, dated <date>", not "Entry case". A flag, never a refusal.
3. Keep `isCaseDateAllowed` as it is. It stops an earlier dated case being slipped under a standing one, which would silently become the default.
4. Put the pick in the address (`?version=`), so a link opens what its sender saw. The read already audits `comparedVersion`.
Run time: the system picks the case and says which and why. A person only chooses to look at another version. Any flag or roll-up (BC-17, BC-18) judges the entry case and names it.
Tests: contracts test for the retired-then-relabelled case and the entry date threshold on its edge; card test for the retirement line; API test that a read after a retirement answers the next case with the new kind.
Context: correct as collected.

BC-2. Straight line between plan dates
Choice: keep the straight line by days, keep the status on it, and say "straight line" on the tile itself. Decided, as built plus the tile wording.
Rules: rule 10 forbids inventing a missing input and taking the nearest period. `planAt` does neither: it never reaches past a neighbour, never extrapolates, gives a reason at every gap, and the figure is derived by a written, tested rule (fixture 27, the Python cross-check). What rule 10 does require is that a reader can tell a derived plan figure from a sponsor figure, and today the tile cannot (F5).
Reasons: comparing on plan dates only gives a comparison about once a year, so the product would decide nothing in most quarters. Twelve-month revenue and EBITDA move smoothly between two year ends, so a line by days is the usual pro-rata reading of an annual budget. Where the sponsor gave quarterly points the record takes them (120 points) and the system then uses the sponsor's own figure, flagged as such. A constant-growth line is a second rule for no gain.
Change to build: page only. `metricTile` adds to the hint, when `row.interpolated` is true, "plan read on a straight line between <d0> and <d1>" (the two dates are in the series already). No contract change. Anything that carries a status off the card (BC-17, BC-18) carries `interpolated` with it.
Worked example (docs/08 example 1): 2024-06-30 is day 182 of 366; plan EBITDA 43,640,000.00 on the line, actual 40,500,000.00, minus 7.20%, behind. The tile must say the 43,640,000.00 is ours.
Run time: the system decides sponsor figure or line and labels it. Nothing goes to a person.
Tests: card test (hint present when interpolated, absent on a plan date); the property test "between its two neighbours" already holds.
Context: correct. Decision 0022 line 61 overstates what is flagged.

BC-3. Second person on the record, or staging
Choice: the record-level confirmation stands. No staging table. The confirmer stays deal team or approver, never the person who last changed the figures. One migration closes F1.
Rules: rule 5 names valuations, quarterly performance and positions; the base case is none of them. I checked the premise: only /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/app.module.ts mentions underwriting outside its own module; nothing under apps/api/src/reports, apps/api/src/packages or the intake adapters reads it. SEC-9.1 is met in substance: Draft is the staged state, the contract and bounds are the validation, the second person is the approval, Confirmed is the only state compared. docs/03 "every intake writes to stg.*" is about files and extraction runs; a record typed by a person and verified by a second on the same table is the house pattern already (wire instruction Proposed to Verified, trade ticket, legal term, closing). SEC-5.6 is held in the constraint, the machine and the API, and for the points by migration 0022.
Where one person can do two people's work today: F1, at the database only.
Change to build (a narrowing, so the build's to take): one new migration that replaces `mon.guard_underwriting_case` so that, inside a user transaction, Draft to Draft and Draft to Withdrawn need `pb.has_role('deal_team')`; Confirmed to Withdrawn needs `pb.has_role('approver')`; and a deferred constraint trigger (as migration 0023 uses for its replacement key) refuses a commit that leaves a Superseded case with no Confirmed version of the same position and case date and a higher number. The owner connection (seed loader) stays exempt, as now.
Not chosen: requiring the approver role to confirm. It would narrow, but the approver heads the same team, so it adds a queue and no independence. Operations cannot confirm, which is right: they do not hold the model.
Trip-wire to record in decision 0022: the day a base case figure or status is printed by a report, a client package or an accounting export, rule 5, SEC-9.3 and SEC-9.4 apply and this decision is void. Make it a test, not a sentence.
Run time: the system runs the checks of BC-8 and BC-13 and shows them to the confirmer. A person enters and a second person confirms; the system never confirms.
Tests: guards.test.ts (deal team cannot retire, nobody supersedes without a correction, approver cannot edit a Draft); matrix row "withdraw the confirmed base case" becomes approver only, plus two new refused rows; an architecture test that fails if any file under apps/api/src/reports, apps/api/src/packages or the accounting intake imports the underwriting module or `schema.underwritingCase`.
Context: "the rule is held three times" is true for confirm only. The roles of retire, supersede and edit are held twice (machine and API), not at the database.

BC-4. When a case is entered, and whether a missing one blocks or flags
Choice: entered once the position exists, as built. A missing case never blocks a closing. The system flags a missing one by itself.
Rules: docs/18 sections 4 and 12 gate cash, documents and allocations. A closing must not fail for want of a monitoring input, so no required checklist item. Rule 9 for the threshold.
Change to build: a derived status, worked out on read and never stored (the pattern of `doc.expected_document`): a held equity, continuation vehicle or credit position with no Confirmed case more than `underwriting.expectedWithinDays` (new config key) after its entry date reads "base case missing, N days". Shown on the card in place of the bare "No base case recorded.", and as a row state in the roll-up of BC-18. No job, no new write path, no exception row.
Not chosen: a case on the opportunity at investment committee (a second anchor on the table and a carry-over at conversion, for no control gain); a model reading the sponsor's workbook into a Draft (possible later under SEC-8.4 and SEC-9.1, and then the reviewer of the extraction must be barred from confirming, as source reviewers are in decision 0023).
Run time: the system establishes "missing" and "late" from the entry date and the clock. Only the entering and the confirming go to people.
Tests: a pure function test on the edge day; an API test on a seeded position with no case (one in five has none).
Context: correct.

BC-5. What counts as the source
Choice: a document or a note stays allowed. When a document is named, a page or a sheet and cell is required at confirmation. A note-only case is marked as such wherever it is shown.
Rules: SEC-9.4 reads "source document, page". The decision record cites SEC-9.4 as met by a note; a note is not click-to-source, and it is acceptable only because no reported number rests on the case (BC-3's premise). The second person's confirmation is only a control if they can open what they confirm; a document of the position is always readable by whoever reads the position (policy `read_visible` on doc.document, migration 0011), a note proves nothing.
Change to build: precondition `hasSourcePlace` on `confirm` in `underwritingCaseMachine` and the API (a document needs `source_page` or `source_locator`); the card shows "Source held outside the platform" beside a note; the `confirm` audit event carries the kind of source. No schema change.
Not chosen: a document always. Sponsors often keep the model in a data room the firm cannot copy, so the system must work with either; it does.
Run time: the system checks that a named document is the position's and has a place. A person supplies the source.
Tests: machine rejection test (document with no place), API test, guards test unchanged.
Context: correct. The seed uses notes only, so the document path is proven by tests alone.

BC-6. The two tolerances and their shape
Choice: 0.05 and 0.02 stand as decided values; one share for the three company figures stands. Not owner only: the system is correct for any value, and a change is a reviewed config edit.
Rules: rule 9 (config, no default in code: held, `tolerancesOf` answers 500 naming the key). Rule 7: do not tie the band to `watchlist.ebitdaYoYDeclinePct`; a fall against last year and a gap to plan are two different rules.
Control points: the read returns the thresholds and the calc version it judged by, so a status can be checked from the answer. A tolerance change re-judges every position at once with no audit event; the record of it is the config commit, which is enough under SEC-16.2.
Change to build: only the note in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/config/definitions.json (`underwriting._note` says "a draft"). One fragility to fix in the generator: `UNDERWRITING_GROWTH_SHIFT` (8 points) and `UNDERWRITING_IRR_SHIFT` (5 points) are constants, so a tolerance at or above them breaks `verify.ts`. Derive the shifts from the config tolerances or refuse with a message that names both keys.
Run time: the system judges. Nothing goes to a person.
Context: correct.

BC-7. Gross MOIC before the exit
Choice: as built. Path MOIC at the underwritten IRR over our own flows, the exit target beside it, no status on the MOIC gap.
Rules: rule 12 is met (fixture 28, property tests, cross-check). One judged return figure is the right control: two badges on the same fact (IRR and MOIC both rest on our Locked mark) would count one shortfall twice. A comparison with the exit MOIC alone would read every young position as behind, a known-wrong verdict.
Change to build: none here. The missing agreement check between the entered MOIC, IRR and dates is BC-8.
Run time: system only.
Context: correct.

BC-8. Seeded scenarios, and a tie check in the product
Choice: both. The product checks that a case's own figures tie and shows the result to the confirmer and on the card. The three status scenarios are rebuilt so every seeded Confirmed case passes that check, with one new scenario that fails it on purpose.
Rules: SEC-9.1 lists validation between staging and approval; today validation is shape and bounds only, so the second person confirms figures the system has not looked at. Rule 12: the tie check is a calculation, so docs/08 text, a golden fixture (next free number) and the Python cross-check come first. Rule 1: synthetic only, which holds.
The rule (flags, never a block, tolerances in config):
(a) MOIC the plan's own exit pays = (exit multiple x exit EBITDA less exit net debt) / (entry EV less entry net debt), against the MOIC entered.
(b) IRR the entered MOIC stands for from the entry date to the planned exit, MOIC^(365/days) less 1, against the IRR entered.
Worked example from the seed 42 small dataset, the `underwriting_ahead` case: entry equity 157,255,022.55 less 70,764,760.15 = 86,490,262.40; plan exit 10.03 x 14,758,994.16 = 148,032,711.42, less 53,524,599.16 = 94,508,112.26; that is 1.09x, against 1.7015x entered: flagged. Check (b): 1.7015^(365/1,419) less 1 = 14.65%, equal to the 0.1465 entered: ties. A sponsor's MOIC can differ from the company equity multiple for good reasons (dividends, add-on equity, dilution), which is why it is a flag with a wide band and words, not a refusal.
Seeds: build the scenario plans as ordinary plans with growth above zero and derive MOIC and IRR from the plan's exit; choose hosts that then read ahead, on and behind; a scenario no host fits is left out and reported, as now. Do it in one dataset change, re-pin the digests, then BC-25.
Run time: the system works out both checks and shows them. The confirmer decides whether to confirm with a flag showing.
Tests: fixture and cross-check for (a) and (b) with each missing input giving a reason; `verify.ts` asserts every seeded Confirmed equity case ties except the one tagged not to.
Context: figures verified against /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/tools/synthetic/.synthetic/dataset.json: correct (1.09x, 1.64x against 1.4743x, 2.155x against 1.6066x).

BC-9. Loan yield priced on the reported fair value or on our mark
Choice: priced on the latest approved credit quarter, as built, in the one function. Forbidden: pricing on the mark in the base case card alone (two different figures called yield to maturity on one page, rule 7). Added: when our Locked mark for the same quarter end differs from that quarter's reported fair value, the same function also answers the yield at our mark, labelled, on all three pages.
Rules: rule 10. The credit row gives par, accrued interest and fair value for one date from one approved record; a Locked mark of another date over that row's par would mix periods. docs/08 section 10 and rule 6 state the built rule. Neither yield is judged, so no verdict depends on the choice; the two-person mark already drives the judged figure (the IRR gap).
Why add the second figure: two approved values exist for one loan and one date, and today the page prices off one and stays silent about the other except in a note. The system can establish the difference itself. Illustration on docs/08 example 4 with two annual coupons left: 10.00% at the reported 10,000,000.00, about 16.25% at our mark of 9,000,000.00 (9x squared less x less 11 = 0).
Change to build: docs/08 section 10 one sentence, a case in the credit fixture and the cross-check first; `creditYieldToMaturity` answers `atMark` only when the mark's quarter end equals the credit row's; contract fields in the credit terms detail and the comparison. Lower priority than F1.
Run time: the system decides whether one figure or two is shown. Nothing goes to a person.
Observation, not asked: the function prices the quarter's fair value as if it were paid on the as-of date (flows are generated from the as-of date). docs/08 section 10 says so, so it is to spec; it is worth a line on the card when the quarter is older than the as-of date.
Context: correct.

BC-10. Primary funds
Choice: none, as built, and the page says so.
Rules: a fund form is new financial math (rule 12) on inputs that do not exist: no net return of a position is worked out anywhere (BC-15), and a fund commitment has no valuation record (decision 0023 question 1, another theme). It would also be our own underwriting, not a sponsor's plan for one company, so it is a different record, not a third value of `form`.
Change to build: `UnderwritingCard` renders nothing when `form` is null, so a reader is not told why. Render one line: "A primary fund position holds no sponsor base case." A position the reader cannot see still renders nothing (404).
Run time: the system decides the form from the deal type.
Context: correct.

BC-11. Outbox events
Choice: change the text to say what is true; do not write outbox rows now. One decision for every machine, recorded once, not in each feature's "consequences".
Rules: rule 14 is about transitions going through the tables, which holds. SEC-11.1 is met by `audit.event` on every transition. The outbox is a delivery pattern for a queue; a queue is a new external service the build may not add, and nothing consumes events.
Why not write rows now: (1) F3. Any signed-in user can insert any outbox row, so a relay built later would publish forged events; a writer needs that policy closed first. (2) Rows with no reader pile up, and the day a relay starts it would send months of stale alerts. (3) Where a transition must tell a person today, the build already does it in the same transaction with `ops.task` (the valuation reopen alerts prior approvers that way, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/valuations/workflow.service.ts).
Change to build: /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/18_WORKFLOWS_AND_STATE_MACHINES.md line 3, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/workflows/README.md line 5, /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/17_ENGINEERING_STANDARDS.md section 5 and /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/docs/02_ARCHITECTURE.md line 103: a transition writes its audit event, and a task where a person must be told; the outbox is written by one writer inside `DbService.run` when the first consumer is built, with a payload of ids, machine, command and states only (SEC-11.4). Narrowing to take now: drop `insert_any` on ops.outbox (nothing uses it) and add the table to the access matrix.
Tests: matrix rows for ops.outbox (nobody inserts, service reads); the workflow tests already assert an audit event per transition.
Context: the matrix row for ops.outbox does not exist (F3).

BC-12. One overall verdict
Choice: no blended score. EBITDA against plan is the headline for an equity position and the IRR gap for a loan. Decided, as built for the card.
Rules: weights would be config nobody can justify (rule 9), and a verdict rule is docs/08 text, fixture and cross-check first (rule 12). Fewer judged figures leave less room to pick the flattering one.
Change to build, only with BC-17 or BC-18: one function in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts answers the verdict a list uses (`headline` for equity; for a loan `returns.irrStatus`, today `headline` is null for a loan), so no page or list derives its own. Until then the unused `headline` field stays; it is harmless.
Context: correct.

BC-13. Different basis in the sponsor's model and its reports
Choice: the system detects it where it can and does not judge a metric it knows is on another basis.
Rules: rule 10 rules out rescaling the plan. SEC-9.1 validation. A badge the system knows rests on two bases is a misleading figure, the same class as an IRR with a flag, which already gets no status.
The rule (docs/08 first, fixture, cross-check): when the plan holds a point dated the entry snapshot, compare its EBITDA, revenue and net debt with the snapshot on record. Beyond `underwriting.basisTolerance` (new config key) the metric gets reason `basis_differs`: amounts and percent still shown, status withheld, and the planned bridge says its start is the record, not the plan. With no plan point on that date the card says the basis was not checked. Example on docs/08 example 2: record at entry 40,000,000.00, a plan point of 44,000,000.00 on 2021-06-30 is 10% apart, so every later EBITDA reading would be "behind" by about that much for no operating reason.
Run time: the system establishes the mismatch and withholds the badge. A person fixes it through the existing paths: a corrected case (new version) or a correction of the snapshot through its own workflow.
Tests: fixture cases on and beyond the tolerance and with no point on the date; card test for the wording; `verify.ts` needs one seeded mismatch scenario.
Context: correct.

BC-14. Other currencies
Choice: refuse, as built, and close F2.
Rules: docs/03 section 1. A rate source is a new connector, not the build's to add.
Change to build: `create` answers 422 for a position whose `core.investment.currency` is not USD, and the read answers no comparison with reason `not_usd`, using the same test the suggestions use. No schema change.
Tests: API test with a position held in another currency (create refused, read gives the reason, nothing compared).

BC-15. Net returns
Choice: gross only, as built. A co-investment's sponsor case is stated gross at the deal; no net return of a position exists to compare with, and showing underwritten net figures with nothing beside them invites a reader to compare them with gross. Every figure already says "gross". No change.

BC-16. Downside case
Choice: one kind of case, as built. More cases are more yardsticks to choose from. If it is ever asked for, the base case stays the only judged one and the others are lines on the chart; that changes the machine's preconditions and its rejection tests. No change.

BC-17. Watchlist flag
Choice: build it as one more watchlist rule, with conditions. A flag that lives only on each deal's own card works only when somebody opens the page.
Conditions (each is a control, not a preference):
1. Its own threshold, `watchlist.ebitdaBehindPlanPct`, no default in code. At the card's 5% most of the book would be flagged (docs/14 says most ordinary positions read behind plan), and a list that flags everything is no control.
2. The same function and the entry case only (rule 7); the row names the case, says when the plan figure is a straight line (BC-2), and gives no flag where the status is withheld (no case, Draft only, not calculable, `basis_differs`). Those are never counted as clear.
3. Worked out per reader inside `watchlist` (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/apps/api/src/analytics/analytics.service.ts), which already runs under the reader's row-level security, so a walled position's flag stays inside the wall (SEC-5.3). Never as the system actor.
4. No report or package prints it. I checked: nothing under apps/api/src/reports or apps/api/src/packages reads the watchlist today; only the web pages and the assistant do, under the reader's session (SEC-8.5).
Tests: analytics API test per condition; a wall test (outside the wall the counts do not move); the architecture test of BC-3 extended to the watchlist.
Context: the weekly report does not read the watchlist in the API. Only web pages do.

BC-18. Roll-up across positions
Choice: build one Analytics table after BC-17, from the same loader: one row per position (case compared, EBITDA status, variance, IRR gap, straight line or not) and counts and NAV by status. Not a line in any weekly or client report.
Rules: SEC-5.3: totals are over what the reader can see, never read as the system actor (the allocation totals pattern would leak here). SEC-11.1: one audited read. Rule 12: counts and sums only; the existing roll-up rule adds up over any split, a weighted score of statuses would be new math. A client report line would bring rule 5, rule 6, SEC-9.3, SEC-9.4 and Compliance review of client output (SEC-16.2), and would void BC-3.
Must show as their own rows: no case, Draft only, missing and late (BC-4), not calculable, basis differs.
Tests: counts by actor on the fixture with the walled position; a position with no case is never in "on plan".

BC-19. Reason for a missing yield
Choice: add the reason in the one function. Rule 10 is met by the placeholder, but decision 0022 says a missing figure "says why", and every other figure of the comparison does.
Change to build: `yieldToMaturity` (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/packages/calc/src/credit.ts) returns null for a past maturity and for a fair value of zero or less without telling them apart. Reasons: `past_maturity`, `non_positive_fair_value`, `no_priced_quarter`, `unknown_payment_frequency`, `no_single_rate`. docs/08 section 10, the credit fixture and the cross-check first; then `yieldNotCalculable` in the credit terms detail and the comparison, so both cards say the same thing.
Tests: one fixture case per reason; API test on the held loan past maturity in the small profile.

The API test locks the mark through the real valuation workflow, which is better proof than a row the loader writes; a seeded mark would move every recorded total for a demo effect. If BC-9's second yield is built, show it in the preview by marking a loan down in the session.

The fixes decided here follow as further requests. Branch names follow `pb/<issue#>-<short-name>` (the working branch `claude/gifted-tesla-eg7s85` does not).

BC-25. Preview and media
Choice: one publish and one media run, after the builds of this theme (BC-8 changes what the seeded cards show). The build prepares it (`build:preview`, the probe, `check:employer-data`); the publish is a workflow run the owner approves (CLAUDE.md 3d). Add a base case walkthrough to the guide, as the suggestions did.
