BC-26. History tables and the wall
Choice: build it. History follows the visibility of its base table, for every history table of a walled record, in one migration. It narrows access and adds no role, so under rule (b) it is the build's to take; decision 0023 question 12 need not wait.
Rules: SEC-5.1 makes the database a line of its own. SEC-5.3 says only named users see a walled record and nobody else learns it exists. Today operations, approvers and auditors outside a wall read the full row image of a walled base case.
Change to build: `history_read` keeps its three roles and adds a clause over the row image, because a base row can be gone (plan points are deleted and replaced): for `mon.underwriting_case_history`, `pb.investment_visible((row_image->>'investment_id')::uuid)`; for the point history, `pb.underwriting_case_visible((row_image->>'case_id')::uuid)`; the same shape for every table in the list of `audit.add_history` calls that carries a wall, and for the history of `core.walled_record` and `core.wall_member`, which today tells those roles which records are walled and who is inside.
The same decision must cover F4: an audit event about a record is read by the audit roles only when the reader can see that record (events with no record id stay by role). An auditor who must audit a walled deal is named a member of its wall, which works whatever the firm's arrangement is. Decide this once in the security theme.
Tests: matrix rows become, by my count from the fixture comment (five case images: two on the open position, three on the walled one; six point images: two and four), `mon.underwriting_case_history` 2 for operations, approver and auditor and `mon.underwriting_case_point_history` 2; a positive test with an approver inside the wall who reads all five; an audit route test that a walled position's events are absent for an auditor outside the wall.
Context: decision 0022's SEC note ("invisible to everyone outside the wall") is not true at the database until this is built; it should then be corrected to say what holds.

BC-27. Remaining differences, and the "draft" wording
Choice: four stand as built; the fifth stands with BC-1's fix; the first needs F1.
(1) The database enforces more than designed: stands, and must enforce the roles too (BC-3).
(2) `no_invested_capital`: stands; docs/08 rule 5 already says it.
(3) No single ETag on the read: stands; docs/17 section 3 says so, and every write is guarded by the version's own row version.

(5) Withdrawing a Draft with one person: stands (a Draft is not yet a plan). Retiring with one approver and a reason: stands, on the precedent of a valuation reopen, but only with BC-1's label and retirement line, since a retirement can change the case a position is judged by.
The record: once these are decided, "a first draft for the owner to change" goes from decision 0022 (Status, line 62, and "Open for the owner" becomes "Decided", each with its reason and the choice set aside), docs/08 section 11, `underwriting._note`, the decisions README, docs/09 line 86 and docs/11 row E14. BC-11 and BC-26 are not the base case's alone and belong in one cross-cutting record with the next free number.
One point no question asks: every signed-in role that can open a position reads its base case (viewer, investor relations and platform admin included), the same as the quarterly figures beside it. That is no wider than the actuals, so I do not require a change. Narrowing it to the four roles of the valuation suggestions is open to the build under rule (b) and becomes necessary if a base case status is ever shown outside the deal page to those roles.

ORDER I WOULD BUILD IN
1. F1 migration and its tests (BC-3). 2. BC-26 with F4. 3. F2 (BC-14). 4. Page wording: BC-2, BC-1, BC-10. 5. Calculation rules, tests first: BC-8 tie check, BC-13 basis check, BC-19 reasons, then BC-9. 6. BC-5 precondition, BC-4 derived status. 7. BC-17, BC-12 verdict function, BC-18. 8. Seeds (BC-8), then BC-25. 9. Text: BC-11, BC-27.

Public standards named: IPEV Valuation Guidelines (December 2022 edition; a secondary source reports a newer edition published December 2025), for calibration against the entry position and backtesting; ILPA Portfolio Company Metrics Template (2019, a refresh in progress), for company figures compared as the sponsor reports them. Both were checked through secondary pages only, not the primary texts.

Sources:
- [ILPA Portfolio Company Metrics Template](https://ilpa.org/resources-tools/resource-library/portfolio-company-metrics-template/)
- [ILPA: refresh of the Portfolio Company Metrics Template](https://ilpa.org/news/portfolio-company-metrics-template-refresh/)
- [Deloitte: IPEV guidelines 2022](https://www.deloitte.com/uk/en/services/audit/research/uk-ipev-guidelines-2022.html)
- [PwC: IPEV 2022 update summary](https://www.pwc.com/jg/en/events/document/ipev-2022-update-summary-26-january-2023.pdf)

## product:base-case

THEME: Deal performance against the sponsor's underwritten base case. Lens: product. 27 questions, one section each.

HOW I READ THE JOB

At quarter end nobody should open forty Performance tabs to learn where the book stands against plan. Once a quarter's figures are approved the comparison already works itself out on read, so the quarterly work on base cases should be close to nothing. Three things are missing today:
1. The system never says, for the whole book, which positions are behind plan and which need a person (BC-17, BC-18, BC-4).
2. The system checks nothing about a case before a second person confirms it (BC-3, BC-8, BC-13).
3. In several places the card leaves a person to settle something the figures on hand already settle (BC-1, BC-6, BC-9, BC-14, BC-19). I mark these "data already settles it".

What the system cannot establish, and must put in front of a person, is a short list: a position with no base case; a Draft waiting for its second person; a position held past the end of every confirmed plan (a new plan has to come from the sponsor or the deal team); a case whose own figures do not tie; a loan whose Locked mark differs from the approved credit quarter's fair value. Everything else it decides and shows why.

Nothing below widens access, adds a role, a service or a licence, deletes data, or touches a table the accounting import or export reads. No question in this theme is owner only except two acts: approving and merging pull requests (BC-24) and approving the publish run (BC-25).

Facts I checked in the generated small profile, seed 42 (/home/user/Portfolio-Beach/tools/synthetic/.synthetic/dataset.json, read only, arithmetic by hand, to be confirmed by the generator's own check): 20 positions, deal types co_invest_equity, cv_single_asset and private_credit only (no primary fund position, no multi-asset vehicle). 13 equity positions have a Confirmed entry case that is compared. 8 of them get an EBITDA status, all 8 on a straight-line plan figure and none on a sponsor figure; 5 get none (4 past the plan's end, 1 before it starts). The 8 read: 1 ahead, 2 on plan, 5 behind. Three held positions (INV-0001, INV-0002, INV-0004) are past their planned exit with no later case.

BUILD ORDER THE DECISIONS IMPLY
1. Calculation first (docs/08 text, golden fixtures, Python cross-check, then code): net debt judged in turns (BC-6), the case checks (BC-8, BC-13), the yield's reasons and the yield at our mark (BC-9, BC-19), the headline of a loan (BC-12), the roll-up (BC-18).
2. Contracts and the read: the latest case beside the entry case (BC-1), the checks, the revenue bounds (BC-21), the currency refusal (BC-14).
3. Workflow table of docs/18 section 13 with its rejection tests: a place in the document for a document source (BC-5), any case date for a Draft (BC-4).
4. Pages: the card, the Analytics tab "Against plan", the watchlist flags, the deal team work list line.
5. Seed: the three status scenarios rebuilt (cases only), then one preview rebuild, probe and media run (BC-8, BC-25).
6. The record: decision 0022 "Open for the owner" becomes "Decided" (BC-27).

New config keys proposed, all in config/definitions.json with no default in code, values are drafts: underwriting.netDebtToleranceTurns 0.25; underwriting.checks.moicTolerance 0.15; underwriting.checks.irrTolerance 0.03; underwriting.checks.entryTolerance 0.02; underwriting.expectedWithinDays 45; underwriting.entryCaseWindowDays 90; watchlist.ebitdaBehindPlanPct 0.15; watchlist.irrBehindPlanPoints 0.05.

==================================================
BC-1. Which case is compared by default
==================================================
Choice: both at once, with the entry case the one judged. No config key for it: one rule.

Why: the two cases answer two different questions a deal team asks every quarter. Against the entry case: did the deal do what we underwrote. Against the latest re-underwriting: is it doing what we now expect. As built the reader sees the first and has to find a dropdown for the second, and the dropdown forgets itself on reload. A worked example from the seed: INV-0018, quarter ended 30 Jun 2020, EBITDA 10,631,973.42. Against the entry case the plan is 14,674,999.60 on a straight line: 27.6% under, behind. Against the case re-underwritten 30 Jun 2019, on a plan date, the plan is 11,047,388.98: 3.8% under, on plan. Both sentences are true and a reader needs both. Judging against the entry case follows practice: the entry price and the entry assumptions are the anchor later performance is read against (the calibration idea of the IPEV Valuation Guidelines, section 3.10 of the December 2022 edition), and it keeps a revision from hiding the gap.

Build:
- packages/contracts/src/underwriting.ts: underwritingRead gains latestVersion and latestComparison, answered when a Confirmed case with a later case date than the entry case exists; the same underwritingComparison over that version. apps/api/src/underwriting/underwriting.service.ts and apps/web/src/preview/routes/underwriting.ts both call it.
- UnderwritingCard.tsx: each tile gets a second line ("Against the case re-underwritten 30 Jun 2019: -3.8%, on plan"), the chart a third line for the later plan. The picker stays for the full view of one version, and its pick goes in the address (a search parameter on the Performance tab, as apps/web/src/pages/valuations/Suggestions.tsx keeps its filter), so a link opens the same view.
- Data already settles it: where the entry case has no plan figure at the quarter because its plan has ended (past_plan_end) and a later Confirmed case covers the date, the company tiles lead with the later case and one line says why ("The entry case's plan ended 30 Sep 2023; company figures are set against the case re-underwritten ..."). The gross IRR is always judged against the entry case first, since the IRR gap does not need the plan to cover the date. One pure function in the contracts file decides which case leads, used by the card, the watchlist and the roll-up.
- Honest labels, from the data: the earliest standing case is called "Entry case" only when its case date is within underwriting.entryCaseWindowDays of the position's entry date; otherwise "Earliest case, dated N months after entry". When a Withdrawn version with an earlier case date was once Confirmed, the card says "The case dated X was retired on D by P: reason. The position is now compared with the case dated Y."

Run time: the system decides which case leads and shows both. A person is needed only when the position is held past the end of every confirmed plan: the card and the roll-up say "Past the planned exit of ...: record the re-underwriting".

Context check: the three unstated facts are right as collected. Each pick is also an audited read (action investment.underwriting.read with comparedVersion), which is fine. Fact 2 (a case cannot be dated before a Confirmed one) is decided under BC-4.

==================================================
BC-2. Straight line between plan dates
==================================================
Choice: as built is right. Keep the straight line by days, keep the status badge on a straight-line figure, and say it on the tile itself.

Why: the judged figures are twelve-month figures, so seasonality is already inside each one, and a straight line between two year-end LTM figures is the ordinary way to phase an annual plan when the sponsor gave no quarters. The other choice gives a comparison once a year: in the seed it would leave all 8 judged positions with no status in this quarter. Rule 10 holds: the line runs only between two sponsor figures, is flagged, never extrapolates and never reaches for a further point. Where the sponsor gave quarterly points the rule already uses them (a case takes 120 points).

Build (page only, no rule change):
- metricTile in apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting.ts reads the interpolated flag it already receives: "US$ 8.4m against a plan of US$ 11.7m, straight line between Dec 2024 and Dec 2025".
- A second line from the series the read already carries: the last plan date on which an approved quarter ends, sponsor figure against actual. Seed example, INV-0016: on 31 Dec 2024 the sponsor figure is 11,083,801.17 and the actual 7,841,026.97, 29.3% under; at 30 Jun 2025 the straight line gives 11,746,063.78 (181 of 365 days) against 8,406,286.87, 28.4% under. The reader sees that the line and the sponsor's own figure tell the same story, without a click.

Run time: the system decides; nothing goes to a person.

Context check: right as collected. I add the count: in the small profile every one of the 8 statuses rests on a straight-line figure, so the tile, not only the note under the chart, has to say so.

==================================================
BC-3. Second person on the record, or staging and approval
==================================================
Choice: as built is right. Draft then Confirmed on the record, confirmed by a deal team member or an approver who did not last change the figures. No staging table. The confirmer is not narrowed to approvers.

Why: rule 5 names valuations, quarterly performance and positions. A base case changes none of them, the accounting import and export do not read it and no client report prints it. SEC-9.1 is met: Draft is the staging, the checks below are the validation, the confirmation is the approval, and only a Confirmed case is compared. A staging queue for a record typed once per deal would add a screen and remove nothing. The right second reader of a sponsor's model is another person on the deal, so narrowing to approvers would create a queue at the head of the team without a better check.

Build, so the second person checks something and the premise stays true:
- The case checks of BC-8 and BC-13 are worked out for the Draft and shown above the Confirm button and in the audit event of the confirmation (which checks differed). Today the confirmer is shown the typed figures and nothing else.
- A test that fails if any file under packages/report-engine, apps/api/src/reports or apps/api/src/packages imports the underwriting module or names mon.underwriting_case. The reason for "no staging" is then enforced, not remembered. If a base case figure is ever wanted in a client output, that test fails first and the staging question is taken then.

Run time: the system refuses the last editor (three layers, built), runs the checks and shows them. A person confirms. The watchlist flag and the roll-up (BC-17, BC-18) are internal pages read under each reader's own access, so they do not disturb the premise.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-4. When a case is entered, and whether a missing one blocks or flags
==================================================
Choice: entered on the position, after closing, as built. A missing case never blocks a closing. It is flagged by the system, which it is not today. Two changes to how a case gets in.

Why: a closing is a legal and cash event; holding it for an internal comparison input would be wrong, and it would change the gates of docs/18 sections 4 and 12. But "No base case recorded." on one card nobody opened is not a control. The data settles which positions lack a case and for how long.

Build:
- Each eligible position gets a state the system works out on read, never stored (the pattern of the expected-document tracker in apps/api/src/documents/expected.ts): expected (entered less than underwriting.expectedWithinDays ago, no Confirmed case), missing (longer), draft waiting (with its age and who entered it), confirmed, no standing case (all withdrawn), past the planned exit. They appear on the roll-up of BC-18, and the deal team's work list in config/role_focus.json gains one line that opens it filtered to "needs a person".
- docs/18 section 13, first row: let a Draft carry any case date other than a Confirmed case's (that one is still a correction). Today isCaseDateAllowed refuses a date earlier than a Confirmed case, so a team that records this quarter's re-forecast first can never add the original case behind it, except by having an approver retire a correct case as "confirmed in error", which puts a false statement on the audit trail. With the change, the kind of a version (entry, correction, re-underwriting) is read from the case dates of the standing versions, not from the order of entry (underwritingKindOf and versionLabels change; the rejection tests of the machine are rewritten). The two-person rule, the reason from version 2, and the label of BC-1 ("recorded N months after entry") keep a back-dated case visible for what it is.
- The form (UnderwritingForm.tsx) takes plan rows pasted from a spreadsheet (date, revenue, EBITDA, net debt in US$ millions) and shows them back in full on the review step it already has. A book of forty positions is roughly 700 typed figures otherwise.
- Not taken now: recording a case on the opportunity at committee (needs an opportunity link and a carry-over at conversion, for a figure the pipeline does not use), and reading a Draft out of a model by extraction (worth doing after the checks exist; only the prototype extractor has been measured, and a sponsor model is a spreadsheet in many shapes). An optional closing checklist item is not taken either: the generator reads config/closing_checklist.json, so it would move every seeded closing and the pinned dataset for an item that blocks nothing.

Run time: the system decides the state of every position. A person is sent the positions that are missing a case, the Drafts waiting, and the positions past their plan.

Context check: right as collected. In the small profile three eligible positions hold no case at all (INV-0005, INV-0011, INV-0017) and nothing in the product tells anyone.

==================================================
BC-5. What counts as the source
==================================================
Choice: a document of the position or a note, as built, with two tightenings.

Why: a sponsor's model is often a spreadsheet that never enters the document store. Whether the firm's models will be filed on the platform is a fact about the outside world; the system behaves correctly either way if the note stays valid. But SEC-9.4 is click to source, and a document cited with no place in it does not let a reader click to anything in a 200 page file.

Build:
- A document source carries a page or a sheet and cell before a case is confirmed (the hasSource precondition of confirm in packages/workflows/src/machines.ts, the API's hasSource, and one check constraint on mon.underwriting_case, this feature's own table). Every seeded case uses a note, so no seeded row changes; the tests that confirm with a bare document gain a page.
- The system tells the person entering and the person confirming when a note was used although documents are filed for the position: "Source is a note. 3 documents are filed for this position." A flag, not a block: the filed documents may not hold the base case, and only a person knows.
- The card and the roll-up say plainly which kind of source a case has ("Note, not a document on the platform"), and the roll-up counts them, so the team can see what share of its base cases can be opened at their source.

Run time: the system checks the document is the position's and readable by the caller (built), that it has a place, and whether documents exist. A person chooses the document and the place.

Context check: right as collected. I add: the document type list (migration 0011) has no type for a sponsor model or a committee memo, so "a fitting type" cannot be decided by the system today; that is why the note-only flag counts every document of the position.

==================================================
BC-6. The two tolerances and their shape
==================================================
Choice: keep 0.05 and 0.02 as the shipped values. Keep one share for revenue and EBITDA. Judge net debt in turns of plan EBITDA, with its own key. Show the thresholds on the card.

Why:
- The values. What the owner's team calls "on plan" is a fact only the owner has, but the system is correct whatever the values: they are configuration, the API refuses to answer without them, and the read returns them. So this is decided, not open. The card never shows the thresholds it receives (no file under apps/web/src/pages/deal reads `thresholds`); one line fixes that: "On plan: within 5% of the plan figure; gross IRR within 2.0 points."
- Net debt. Data already settles it, and the card says nothing. Seed example, INV-0008 (underwriting_net_cash), 30 Jun 2025: the plan is net cash of 33,323,568.36 and the company carries net debt of 45,721,545.69. That is 79,045,114.05 worse than plan, and the tile reads "The plan figure is zero or less." with no status. A percent of net debt also misleads on a small base. Second example, INV-0006: net debt 61,687,405.17 against a plan of 58,105,071.85 is 6.2% over, so "behind", while EBITDA is 21.8% ahead and leverage is better than planned (3.30x against 3.78x).
- The rule: net debt variance divided by the plan EBITDA at the same date, in turns; on plan within underwriting.netDebtToleranceTurns (draft 0.25), sign turned as now. INV-0008: 79,045,114.05 over plan EBITDA of 18,331,756.28 is 4.31 turns over: behind. INV-0006: 3,582,333.32 over 15,368,526.37 is 0.23 turns: on plan. INV-0016: 3,878,313.13 over 11,746,063.78 is 0.33 turns: behind, as today. No status where the plan EBITDA at the date is missing or not above zero, with a reason. The percent stays on the tile where the plan figure is above zero.

Build: docs/08 section 11 rule 3 first, fixture 27 (net debt under and over plan, a plan in net cash, a plan EBITDA of zero), apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/underwriting.py, then planStatus for net debt in packages/calc/src/underwriting.ts. The property "a higher net debt never raises its own status" stays. Not taken: a band by deal type or one that narrows toward exit (no evidence it is needed; one more thing for a reader to learn).

Run time: the system judges all three figures; nothing goes to a person.

Context check: right as collected. The seeded net cash scenario is exactly the case the present shape cannot judge.

==================================================
BC-7. Gross MOIC before the exit
==================================================
Choice: as built is right. The gross MOIC against the MOIC on the underwritten path, the exit target beside it, no status.

Why: against the exit target alone every young position reads behind. The path uses only the case's own IRR and our own dated flows, so nothing is invented. A status on the MOIC gap would be a second badge saying what the IRR badge says, and the two could differ only by an accident of two tolerances. A path read from the plan would need a multiple at every date that no sponsor gave.

Build: nothing in the rule. The one weakness, that nothing checks the underwritten MOIC against the underwritten IRR and dates, is closed by the checks of BC-8.

Run time: the system decides. Nothing goes to a person.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-8. The seeded status examples, and whether a case's own figures must tie
==================================================
Choice: yes to both. The product checks every case against itself and against the record at entry, as flags and never blocks. The three status scenarios are rebuilt so each is a case a sponsor could have underwritten and each passes those checks.

Why: this is the most useful thing the system can do for the second person. The seed proves it. In scenario underwriting_corrected (INV-0014), version 1 was confirmed with an exit multiple typed three turns too high, 12.97x, and an underwritten MOIC of 2.8529x. Its own plan's exit at 12.97x pays 3.94x on the entry equity value. A check would have shown "the plan's exit pays 3.94x; the case says 2.85x" before anyone confirmed. Today a person has to notice.

The checks (new rule 7 of docs/08 section 11, one pure function in packages/calc, fixture and Python cross-check before any use):
- 7.1 Exit MOIC, equity: (exit multiple x exit EBITDA - exit net debt) / (entry EV - entry net debt) from the entry snapshot on record, against the underwritten gross MOIC. Differs beyond underwriting.checks.moicTolerance (draft 0.15, wide on purpose: dividends, dilution and fees make a sponsor's gross MOIC differ honestly).
- 7.2 IRR of the MOIC, equity: MOIC^(365 / days(entry date, planned exit)) - 1 against the underwritten gross IRR. Differs beyond underwriting.checks.irrTolerance (draft 0.03).
- 7.3 A loan: underwritten yield less the annual loss rate against the underwritten gross IRR, same tolerance. The single-flow formula of 7.2 does not fit a loan that pays interest as it goes.
- 7.4 The plan against the record at entry: BC-13.
- Each check answers ties, differs with both figures, or not checked with the reason (no entry snapshot, entry equity not above zero, no plan point there). Never a zero.

Where they show: the form's review step, above the Confirm button, in the confirmation's audit event, and on the card of any Confirmed case that does not tie ("1 of 3 checks differs").

The seed (tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.ts, docs/14 section 12): the ahead, on plan and behind cases become ordinary cases in every respect but growth. EBITDA grows at the larger of 2% a year and the position's own annualised rate less, at or plus the shift; the MOIC is the plan's own exit over the entry equity value and the IRR the rate that MOIC stands for, as every ordinary case already has them; a candidate is taken only if it then reads as tagged on EBITDA and on IRR under the config tolerances. The IRR shift constant goes. By hand on seed 42: INV-0006 would hold a plan growing 2% a year (16.43m to about 17.79m), read about 8.4% ahead on EBITDA, and be underwritten at about 1.44x and 9.9% against its own 19.65%; INV-0012 about 1.64x and 7.4%, 1.7 points over its own, on plan; INV-0016 about 2.15x and 17.0%, well over its own, behind. All three then tie on 7.1 and 7.2, and the superseded version of INV-0014 is the one seeded card where a check differs.

Run time: the system works out the checks. A person decides whether a difference is a typing error or a real feature of the sponsor's case.

Context check: the figures are right (ahead: EBITDA 16,432,081.77 falling to 14,758,994.16, MOIC 1.7015 against a plan exit of 1.09; on plan 1.4743 against 1.64; behind 1.6066 against 2.15). One correction for BC-20 and BC-25: rebuilding the scenarios moves only the base case rows. The digest of the dataset without the base cases (first pin in tools/synthetic/src/underwriting.test.ts) holds; the pin on the equity cases, the recordings and the tests that read those three positions move.

==================================================
BC-9. A loan's yield to maturity: credit quarter or Locked mark
==================================================
Choice: priced on the fair value of the latest approved credit quarter, in the one function, as built. The system works out whether our Locked mark differs from that fair value, and shows a second yield at our mark only when it does.

Why: data already settles it. The card tells every reader of every loan that the yield is priced on a fair value "which our own mark does not change". In the seed the two values are the same to the cent on both loans (INV-0009: 18,837,699.89 and 18,837,699.89; INV-0013: 9,859,053.48 and 9,859,053.48). The caveat describes a difference no seeded loan has, and makes a reader wonder about one. When they do differ, the reader will want the yield at our mark, because the IRR and MOIC on the same card use it, and will otherwise work it out by hand.

Build: docs/08 section 10 and section 11 rule 6 first, then creditYieldToMaturity in apps/api/src/performance/performance.service.ts (still the one place):
- It also reads the latest Locked mark for the credit row's quarter. When the mark equals the row's fair value within rollForwardToleranceUsd, the answer says so and the hint reads "On the fair value of the quarter ended ..., which is also our mark." The YIELD_NOTE caveat goes.
- When they differ, the answer carries both values, the difference, and a second figure, the same yieldToMaturity over the same par and contractual flows priced on our mark, each labelled ("at the reported fair value", "at our mark"). The Credit terms card, the Analytics credit tab and the base case card read the same answer, so two figures called yield never appear on one page unlabelled.
- Neither yield is judged, as now.
- Golden fixture 28 worked example 4 gains the second yield for the loan marked at 9,000,000.00; the Python credit check follows.

Run time: the system decides which case applies. A person is shown a loan whose Locked mark and reported fair value differ: that is a valuation difference worth a look in its own right.

Context check: right as collected, plus the seed fact above. A side finding outside this theme: the seeded loans' marks carry the method "par plus accrued", but INV-0009's par plus accrued interest is 18,200,513.56, not 18,837,699.89.

==================================================
BC-10. A base case for a primary fund
==================================================
Choice: none, as built. The page says so in one line instead of showing nothing.

Why: what a fund commitment is underwritten on is our own view of the fund (net IRR, net TVPI, the pace of calls and distributions), not a sponsor's plan for one company. It belongs with the pacing plan and net returns, neither of which exists (BC-15), and a commitment carries no mark yet (decision 0023 question 1, decided in its own theme). Practice follows a fund interest on the sponsor's reported net asset value rolled forward for cash flows since, and on reported net figures. When it is built it should be a record of its own beside the pacing plan, not a third value of `form` on this table.

Build: UnderwritingCard returns null when form is null. Replace with one line: "A primary fund position holds no sponsor base case. It is followed on its reported net figures." A reader then knows the card did not fail to load. Record in decision 0022 that a multi-asset continuation vehicle takes the equity form on purpose: it follows the basis its quarterly figures use, as the value creation card does.

Run time: the system decides from the deal type. Nothing goes to a person.

Context check: two points. underwritingFormOf does not read EQUITY_DEAL_TYPES directly; it calls coversDealType in apps/api/src/analytics/value-creation.ts, which reaches the same list through QUARTERLY_REPORTING_DEAL_TYPES. And the seed holds no primary fund position and no multi-asset vehicle, so today the question is about a card no seeded page shows; a primary fund position would be drawn by the equity branch of PerformanceTab.tsx with the base case card silently absent.

==================================================
BC-11. Outbox events
==================================================
Choice: the text says what is true. The audit event is the record of a transition. The outbox row is written when the first consumer is built, in one place for every machine, with its relay.

Why: one decision for all machines. Nothing reads ops.outbox and nothing relays it. Writing rows nobody reads grows an unpublished table, fixes a payload before any consumer has said what it needs, and gives a false sense that something is delivered. The audit event is written in the same transaction with the state before and after, so nothing is lost for a later writer. A relay is an integration and ships with a kill switch, a health check and a probe (section 3c); a real queue is a new service, which is not the build's to add.

Build: change docs/18 line 3, packages/workflows/README.md line 5, docs/17 section 5 and docs/02 line 103 to say: every transition writes audit.event with the state before and after; the outbox row and its relay are added with the first consumer, in the audit callback of DbService.run. Remove the "deviation" lines from decisions 0022 and 0023. One test across the API workflow suites: every command of every machine writes one audit event with both states.

A product consequence to record with it: docs/18 section 1 says a reopen "alerts prior approvers" and docs/04 M9 says "alerts to the deal team". No file in apps/api/src sends a notification. Until there is a notification adapter (a tenant integration, which waits on decision 0019), these are met in the app on read: the watchlist, the work list and the roll-up. The two sentences should say that.

Run time: not a run-time decision.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-12. One overall verdict
==================================================
Choice: no blended score. Use the headline that already travels, and define it for a loan.

Why: weights nobody chose would be an invented number. But the watchlist and the roll-up need one status of record per position, and `headline` is in the contract and read by no page (only tools/synthetic/src/verify.ts reads it). Two plain statuses answer two plain questions and should never be merged: is the company on plan (EBITDA against plan) and is our return on plan (gross IRR against underwriting). "The weakest decides" would let a conservative mark of ours paint a company that is beating its plan as behind.

Build:
- docs/08 section 11 rules 3 and 6, fixture 28, the Python check: the headline is the EBITDA status for an equity case and the IRR status for a loan (null today for a loan).
- The card shows the headline as one badge in its header, beside the quarter, so the answer is there at a glance; the tiles keep their own badges.
- The roll-up shows two counts, company against plan and return against plan, never a sum of the two.

Run time: the system decides. Nothing goes to a person.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-13. A difference in basis between the sponsor's model and its reports
==================================================
Choice: detect and show. Never rescale.

Why: data already settles part of it. Where the plan holds a figure at the entry snapshot's date, the plan's own starting figures can be set against the snapshot on record. A sponsor's "adjusted" or "pro forma" EBITDA shows up there as a gap at day one. Rescaling the plan would invent numbers (rule 10). A basis label typed by a person cannot be compared with anything, since the actuals carry no such label.

Build: check 7.4 of BC-8. At the entry snapshot's date, the plan figure by rule 1 (the point's own, or a straight line between two plan dates, flagged) against the snapshot's EBITDA, revenue and net debt; differs beyond underwriting.checks.entryTolerance (draft 0.02), each figure on its own. Not checked, with the reason, when the plan starts after the snapshot. Where it shows: the review step, above Confirm, on the card beside the source, and under the drivers table when it differs, because the planned bridge starts from the snapshot on record and not from the plan's first point: "The planned bridge starts from the entry snapshot on record, 3.0% under the plan's own starting EBITDA." The bridge rule itself does not change: both bridges start from the same snapshot, which is what makes progress comparable. A plan is not required to start on the snapshot date; many sponsor plans are dated to fiscal year ends.

Run time: the system finds and sizes the gap. A person decides what it means, and may record it in the case's note.

Context check: right as collected. Ordinary seeded plans start from the snapshot itself, so the seed needs one case with a gap (a test case, or one more scenario built from the base case stream only).

==================================================
BC-14. Other currencies
==================================================
Choice: refuse, as built, and close a gap on the other side of the comparison.

Why: docs/03 says US dollars only and that mixed-currency math fails loudly. The case side does. The position side does not: core.investment.currency has no US dollar check (packages/db/migrations/0002_core.sql), the base case read never loads it (loadInvestmentsWithMetrics carries no currency), and the comparison never reads the currency of the quarters. A position held in another currency could today be given a base case in dollars and be compared silently with actuals in its own currency. The valuation suggestions bar exactly this (reason not_usd in packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts).

Build: the read answers no comparison with reason not_usd (one more value of UNDERWRITING_NOT_CALCULABLE) when the position, or an approved quarter it would compare, is not in US dollars; a create is refused 422 in words for such a position. The card says "This position is not held in US dollars, so it is not compared with a base case yet." API test with a non-dollar position.

Run time: the system refuses and says why. Nothing goes to a person. Conversion needs a rate source, a new connector, which is not the build's to add.

Context check: "Nothing is converted anywhere" is true, but nothing checks that the two sides share a currency either.

==================================================
BC-15. Net returns
==================================================
Choice: gross only, as built. Decided, not open.

Why: a sponsor's base case for a co-investment is stated gross at the deal level, and that is what the owner asked to track. No net return of a position is worked out anywhere, and there are no fee and carry inputs to work one out from. Holding underwritten net figures with nothing to compare them to would be a field with no use. Every tile already says "Gross".

Build: nothing. The record says: optional underwritten net figures are added to the case when a net return of a position exists (new math under rule 12), not before.

Run time: nothing to decide.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-16. A downside case
==================================================
Choice: the base case only, as built. Decided, not open.

Why: the owner asked for the base case. Three scenarios would triple what the deal team types for every position, and nothing in the product, the reports or the seed reads a downside. If one is wanted later, the light form is two more figures on the base case (downside MOIC and IRR shown beside the returns), not a second and third plan with their own versions; that keeps the correction rule of docs/18 section 13 keyed on the case date alone.

Build: nothing.

Run time: nothing to decide.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-17. A watchlist flag
==================================================
Choice: build it. Two rules in config, each reading the comparison the card shows.

Why: data already settles it. Which positions are well behind plan is known the moment a quarter is approved, and today a person has to open each deal to find out. A watchlist band should be wider than the "on plan" band, or most of the book sits on the list: in the seed 5 of 8 judged positions are more than 5% behind.

Build (evaluateFlags in apps/api/src/analytics/analytics.service.ts, watchFlagCode in packages/contracts/src/schemas.ts, a batched loader of the standing cases beside loadOperatingRows):
- behind_plan, severity watch, equity and continuation vehicles: EBITDA of the current quarter under the plan of the leading case (the BC-1 rule) by more than watchlist.ebitdaBehindPlanPct (draft 0.15). The message carries everything needed to trust it: "EBITDA of US$ 8.4m for 30 Jun 2025 is 28.4% under the entry case's plan of US$ 11.7m (straight line between plan dates; limit 15%)".
- return_behind_plan, severity watch, loans: gross IRR under the underwritten IRR by more than watchlist.irrBehindPlanPoints (draft 0.05), only when the IRR carries no flag. Equity is not flagged on its IRR gap: early marks are conservative and the markdown rule already covers a falling mark.
- Judged against the entry case, so lowering the plan does not take a position off the list. Never raised on a figure that is not calculable, on a Draft, or with no case; those are the roll-up's business, not a risk flag.
- On seed 42 by hand: INV-0008 (47.7% under), INV-0016 (28.4%) and INV-0010 (18.0%, the walled position, in the list of wall members only) would be flagged; INV-0014 (10.5%) would not.

Run time: the system decides and says why in the flag. A person reads the list.

Context check: one correction. No report reads the watchlist: there is no reference to it in packages/report-engine, apps/api/src/reports or apps/api/src/packages. It is read by three pages (Home, the Watchlist, the assistants page through apps/web/src/lib/assistant.ts), each under the reader's own access. So the flag does not make the base case a figure a client could see, and BC-3 stands. The watchlist counts, the Home page and the preview recordings do move.

==================================================
BC-18. A roll-up across positions
==================================================
Choice: build it, as an Analytics tab "Against plan". It is also the work list for everything in this theme that needs a person.

Why: this is the page that replaces opening forty tabs. It costs little: the value creation view already loads every position with its metrics and quarters in batches (loadInvestmentsWithMetrics, loadOperatingRows), and the comparison is a pure function over those rows.

Build:
- Route GET /api/v1/analytics/underwriting?asOf=, the roles of the value creation route, one audit event for the read. One row per equity, continuation vehicle and credit position the reader can see: the case that leads (entry, or re-underwritten on a date) and its source kind; company status with the EBITDA variance; revenue and net debt statuses; the IRR gap with its status; time passed beside total progress; whether its checks tie; and for a position with no comparison its state in words: no case (expected or missing, BC-4), draft waiting N days, no standing case, plan ended, not in US dollars, or the reason a figure is not calculable. A position without a comparison is counted in its own bucket, never as on plan.
- Counts by company status and by return status, each with invested capital and with NAV where every held position in the bucket has a Locked mark (the pooled rule of docs/08 section 8; otherwise the NAV is missing with its reason). A pure function in packages/calc with a fixture and the Python cross-check, and the property that the roll-up adds up over any split, so a filtered view is exact.
- A column "last quarter" with the status as of the previous quarter end, by the same function with that date, so a reader sees what moved this quarter.
- A filter "needs a person". The deal team's work list line of BC-4 opens the tab with it set.
- Internal only. Not in any reporting package (the test of BC-3 guards it). Each reader's counts are over what that reader can see, so a walled position is never in an outsider's count.

Run time: the system decides every row. A person gets the filtered list: record a case, confirm a Draft, record a re-underwriting, look at a case that does not tie.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-19. Why a loan's yield to maturity is missing
==================================================
Choice: say which reason applies, in the one function.

Why: data already settles it. Every other missing figure of the comparison says why. The seed has the case on a card: INV-0013 matured on 22 Oct 2024 and is still held, and its tile reads "Not calculable this quarter." The comment above yieldTiles even says "the tile says so"; it does not.

Build: docs/08 section 10 first. yieldToMaturity in packages/calc/src/credit.ts and creditYieldToMaturity answer a reason beside a null: past_maturity, no_priced_quarter (no approved credit row with a fair value and par on or before the date), non_positive_fair_value, unknown_payment_frequency. Fixture 14 and fixture 28, apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/credit.py. The contract carries it on the credit terms detail and on the comparison (packages/contracts/src/schemas.ts and underwriting.ts, apps/api/openapi.json). Both cards read it: "The loan is past its maturity of 22 Oct 2024."

Run time: the system decides. Nothing goes to a person; the past maturity flag on the watchlist already sends the loan to one.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-20. A seeded loan marked below par
==================================================
Choice: not seeded, as built. Prove it in the browser journey instead.

Why: a marked-down loan cannot be seeded without moving recorded totals: a lowered mark or a new position changes NAV and counts that are pinned across the suite, and the digest of the dataset without the base cases. That is a different and larger cost than BC-8, which moves base case rows only. The behaviour is not in doubt (fixture 28 worked example 4 and the API test cover it); what is missing is the owner seeing it in a running app.

Build: apps/web/e2e/underwriting.spec.ts gains a step: lock one seeded loan at 90% of par through the valuation workflow, open its card, and read the IRR behind plan, the line "Our mark differs from the reported fair value by ..." and the two labelled yields of BC-9. If the static preview's one-pager answer moves with a simulated lock, the same steps become a walkthrough in components/PreviewGuide.tsx; if it does not, the preview does not claim it.

Run time: not a run-time decision.

Context check: the note that BC-8 "moves the same pins" is not right, as said under BC-8.

==================================================
BC-21. Checking revenue bounds in the form
==================================================
Choice: return the bounds with the read and check in the form with the shared function.

Why: one rule in one place is kept, since the web app still holds no copy of a threshold: it receives it. underwritingBodyIssues is already in packages/contracts and already takes the bounds. The person then sees "Plan date 3, revenue: ..." on the review step, before "Save draft", not after a refusal.

Build: underwritingRead gains `bounds` (revenueMin, revenueMax, decimal strings) beside `thresholds`; UnderwritingService.read fills them from revenueBounds(); the preview route already imports the config. bodyOf in apps/web/src/pages/deal/underwriting-form.ts calls underwritingBodyIssues with the bounds the card passes down. The API check stays as the line that counts.

Run time: the system refuses a figure typed in the wrong unit, in the form and again in the API, and never guesses the unit. The person retypes it.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================

Why: docs/17 section 4 gives the clock to the application, so a database rule that moved entered_at would disagree with a fixed clock in tests. The two-person rule rests on entered_by, which the database does enforce. The history table and the audit trail already record the database's own time of every change.

Build: nothing.

Run time: nothing to decide.

==================================================

Why: a scenario that does not fit is left out and the check says which, which is the honest behaviour. Letting scenarios share a position would make one card demonstrate two things at once and make the scenario tests ambiguous. Nothing in the product depends on a seed other than 42.

Build: nothing, beyond making sure the refusal names the scenarios that did not fit and the number of positions available.

Run time: nothing to decide.

==================================================
BC-24. How the work is opened for review
==================================================
Choice: one pull request per feature, stacked in commit order, each to be read commit by commit. Opening and splitting is the build's to do. Approving and merging is the owner's act and the only owner-only step here.

Why: the slices of one feature do not stand alone, and later features on the branch share files with the base case (packages/contracts, packages/workflows/src/machines.ts, docs/08, docs/18), so the branch cuts cleanly only at feature boundaries. One pull request per slice would hand a single owner thirty or more to merge in strict order. The target of about 800 changed lines cannot be met per feature; each pull request says so, lists its slices in commit order with their changed lines, and uses .github/pull_request_template.md with its SEC ids. The decisions of this theme then go in as their own smaller pull requests, in the build order at the top, each of which can stay near the target.

Run time: not a run-time decision.

Context check: right as collected.

BC-25. Rebuilding the published preview and the media
==================================================
Choice: once, after the decisions of this theme are built. The build prepares everything; approving the publish run in GitHub Actions is the owner's act.

Why: BC-1, BC-2, BC-6, BC-8 and BC-9 change what the seeded cards show. Recording before them means recording twice.

Build, in order: the decisions; `build:preview` and the probe; a walkthrough for the base case in components/PreviewGuide.tsx (none is recorded as written for this feature): a position ahead of plan, the re-underwritten one with both cases on its tiles, the corrected one with the check that would have caught the error, a loan, then the Analytics tab; then the media run. Nothing is deployed from a session.

Run time: not a run-time decision.

Context check: right as collected.

==================================================
BC-26. History tables and the wall
==================================================
Choice: history follows the wall of its record. One migration for every history table of a walled record, decided once (it is the same decision as question 12 of decision 0023).

Why: it narrows who can read and adds no role, so it is the build's to take. SEC-5.1 makes the database a line of its own, and SEC-5.3 says a walled record is not revealed to others. Today operations, approvers and auditors outside a wall can read the full row image of a walled position's base case, figures and people included, at the database. Decision 0022 says a walled base case is invisible outside the wall; that sentence is not true of its history.

Build: a migration that replaces history_read on each such table with the role test and the visibility test of the base record, read from the row image: for mon.underwriting_case_history, pb.investment_visible on the image's investment_id; for mon.underwriting_case_point_history, pb.underwriting_case_visible on the image's case_id (a case is never deleted, so the join holds). The rows of packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts change from 5 and 6 for an outsider to what an outsider sees of the base tables. An auditor who must read a walled record's history is a member of the wall, as for the record itself. Still no API route reads a history table.

Run time: the database decides. Nothing goes to a person.

Context check: right as collected.

The five:
1. The database enforcing more than the design asked: right; it is the pattern that makes the two-person rule real.
2. The reason no_invested_capital: right; it is a reason in place of a division by zero.
3. No single ETag on a read that lists versions: right, and docs/17 section 3 already says so.
4. Percent in the form and the last plan row as the planned exit: right; the date cannot then be typed twice and disagree.
5. Withdrawing a Draft with no second person: right, a Draft is never compared. Retiring a Confirmed case with one approver and a reason: right, on one condition, which BC-1 builds: retiring the entry case moves the comparison to a later and possibly softer case, so the card and the roll-up must say that it was retired, by whom and why, and which case now leads. With that shown, a second person adds nothing the audit trail and the page do not already give.

Build (the record only): decision 0022 status line and consequences line 62; its "Open for the owner" section becomes "Decided", each item with the reason and the choice set aside; "Left out for now" becomes what is decided for each (BC-14 to BC-18); docs/08 section 11 first paragraph; the `_note` of `underwriting` in config/definitions.json; the index line in docs/decisions/README.md; docs/09 line 86; docs/11 row E14; docs/SESSION_LOG.md. Where a decision above changes a rule, the same pull request changes docs/08, docs/18 or docs/14 first.
