## practice:valuation-suggestions

PRACTICE LENS: valuation suggestions from capital account statements and sponsor reports (VS-1 to VS-28)

Read only. Nothing in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach was created, changed or run. No file was written.

NOTE ON PUBLIC SOURCES
I searched public professional sources with generic queries. The primary texts could not be opened from this session: the hosts www.privateequityvaluation.com, ilpa.org and www.dirittobancario.it are outside the session's allowed network domains and have to be allowed in the session's network settings before they can be fetched. So the standards below are cited from public summaries and from working knowledge, and the decision record should check section numbers against the primary text before it quotes one. What the searches confirmed:
- IPEV Valuation Guidelines, the part on valuing fund interests: an investor starts from its share of the fund's reported net asset value and adjusts it for capital calls and distributions since the NAV date and for other known changes. The reported NAV is a starting point, not a default conclusion. Secondary sources say a December 2025 edition replaced the December 2022 one for quarterly periods starting on or after 1 April 2026: the record should cite the edition in force and check this on the IPEV site.
- US accounting standard ASC 820-10-35-59 (the net asset value practical expedient): an investee's NAV may be used without adjustment only when it is as of the investor's measurement date and measured on investment company principles. An adjusted NAV is a fair value estimate. The SEC staff statement of 28 September 2026 on private asset valuation repeats this and asks for written valuation policies and for a fresh look when new information arrives.
- ILPA Reporting Template (updated release of January 2025, in use from the first quarter of 2026): the capital account statement is one roll per investor from beginning NAV to ending NAV through contributions, distributions, fees, expenses, income, realised and unrealised gains and the change in accrued carried interest, with the commitment and unfunded lines beside it. The NAV lines are stated net of the incentive allocation (carried interest).
Links are listed at the end.

WHAT A CAREFUL TEAM NEVER AUTOMATES (applies to every section)
Writing, approving or locking a mark. Reopening a Locked mark. Choosing an exchange rate or converting at a rate nobody approved. Deciding, when the document is unclear, whether a statement's lines are our own account or the whole vehicle's. Deciding which account a statement is for when it does not come to exactly one. Adjusting a sponsor's NAV for a known event. The fair value of a loan. Setting a source aside (a dismissal). Any fuzzy match of a name.

==================================================
VS-1. How a fund commitment carries a mark
==================================================
Choice: option (a), one position per commitment. Each core.commitment has one core.investment of deal type deal_type.primary_fund, linked one to one. The mark is an ordinary mon.valuation on that position. A fund statement is linked to that position, and rule 12.1 then applies as written. This is not an owner-only question.

Practice and reasons:
- A limited partner carries each fund interest as one holding with a commitment, called, distributed, unfunded and a NAV each quarter. The NAV is the ending balance of its capital account as the sponsor reports it (IPEV, valuing fund interests; ASC 820 practical expedient). Every limited partner book of record is built this way.
- A sponsor issues one capital account statement per investor of record. Each of our vehicles that committed is its own limited partner. So the unit that carries the mark is the commitment (vehicle, sponsor fund, optional client), and a statement belongs to exactly one commitment.
- The repository already says so. docs/03 line 48 and decision 0004 list primary_fund among the investment deal types ("fund-level rows for primaries"). docs/04 line 148 (M9) asks for "Primary fund monitoring: fund NAV, calls, distributions, unfunded". apps/api/src/monitoring/monitoring.service.ts loadExpected (lines 152 to 220) already answers a primary fund position from its sponsor fund's statement. createDraft in apps/api/src/valuations/workflow.service.ts checks only that the position is active, so the valuation workflow already takes such a position with no change.
- (b) changes mon.valuation, which config/accounting_layout.json reads: not the build's to take, and avoidable. (c) is a second valuation workflow (CLAUDE.md rule 7) and would give reports two places to read NAV. (d) leaves the fund of funds NAV not calculable for ever, against M9. (e) has nowhere to put the mark.

What must be built (tests first where a rule moves):
1. The link. A nullable, unique column on core.commitment naming its position (for example position_id references core.investment). core.commitment is not read by the accounting import or export. core.investment and mon.valuation keep their schema.
2. The company row. core.investment.portfolio_company_id is NOT NULL, so each sponsor fund with a commitment gets one core.portfolio_company row that stands for the fund, as apps/api/src/pipeline/convert.ts already makes from a primary fund deal's company name. Record this plainly in the decision as a stand-in, and keep it out of company-level readers by deal type.
3. Who sees it. A client-directed commitment is under the client rule (SEC-5.2) and core.investment is under the wall only, so a position for a client-directed commitment would show its NAV to readers who cannot read the commitment. The read policy of core.investment gains one narrowing clause: a position that stands for a commitment is visible only to those who can read that commitment. Because every child table asks pb.investment_visible (packages/db/migrations/0006_history_rls.sql lines 14 to 19), valuations, suggestions, documents and runs follow with no other change. It needs a definer function (a plain subquery cannot tell an invisible commitment from none) and new rows in packages/db/src/rls/matrix.ts.
4. Creating the pair. The synthetic generator writes one position per commitment with an id derived from the commitment's id (no new random draw, so no other row moves). convert.ts must create the commitment and its position together when a primary fund deal closes: today it writes an investment and no commitment.
5. Cash flows stay keyed by commitment_id (mon.cash_flow, mon.capital_notice). One shared reader, "the approved flows of a position", returns flows where investment_id is the position or commitment_id is its commitment. apps/api/src/suggestions/sources.ts (lines 247 to 256 and 378 to 382), the portfolio metrics and apps/api/src/portfolio/commitments.ts all call it.
6. suggestionBar in packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts: deal_type.primary_fund is allowed for the statement basis. No report rule can fire for it, because a fund position has no mon.quarterly_performance row. EQUITY_DEAL_TYPES stays as the list for quarterly reports.
7. Which commitment a fund statement is for. One commitment of ours to the fund: that position. Several (scenario two_clients_one_fund): the investor named on page one must match one of our vehicles exactly, otherwise the document goes to NeedsReview for a person (see VS-9). A document that lists several investor accounts links to no position and raises nothing, with a reason of its own (several_accounts in place of fund_commitment), and the page tells operations to mark each commitment from it by hand.
8. Expected documents: one statement per commitment position per quarter, keyed on investment_id, which doc.expected_document already allows. The row per sponsor fund goes (see VS-8).
9. Synthetic documents: one fund statement per commitment that states the five balance lines. The eleven pinned fund-level statements state no balance of ours, which no real statement does; they change, or stay as the several-accounts example, and the byte pin in docs/14 line 110 is updated on purpose. Fund NAVs need a deterministic source of their own (a separate stream, as the base cases used).
10. The meaning of the ending balance. docs/08 rule 12.1 and STATEMENT_TASK in packages/ai/src/registry.ts must say: the ending capital balance net of accrued carried interest; when a statement prints a gross and a net figure, the net one. This is a prompt version change with an eval case.
11. Readers. NAV series, exposure and vehicle totals should now include fund positions (that is the requirement of M9). Company-level readers (value creation, base case, quarterly monitoring rows, the map, the accounting export of company figures) keep their deal type filter, each with a test that a fund position is left out.
12. Records: docs/03, docs/04 (M3, M9, M10), docs/08 section 12, docs/11 rows E4 and E15, docs/14, docs/18 sections 6 and 14.

Worked example (rule 12.1 on a fund interest, in dollars): beginning 9,150,000.00, contributions 1,250,000.00, distributions 400,000.00, net gain 310,000.00, ending 10,310,000.00. 9,150,000 + 1,250,000 - 400,000 + 310,000 = 10,310,000: the lines add up. S = 10,310,000.00, read as our own account. Previous quarter's mark 9,150,000.00: change 1,160,000.00, 12.68%, variance. Net of flows 310,000.00, 3.39%. Contributions to date 11,250,000.00 against 11,250,000.00 approved on the commitment: they agree.

At run time. The system decides: which commitment a statement is for when that comes to exactly one, the suggested mark, both checks against the commitment's own flows, the comparison with the last Locked mark. It sends to a person: which account when it is not exactly one, a document listing several accounts, the acceptance and both approvals.

Context check. Accurate on every line I read. Three things the collected context did not say: (1) convert.ts creates a position and no commitment, so the two halves were never joined; (2) M9 already asks for fund NAV, so a fund position in the NAV series is a requirement, not a leak; (3) a document linked to no position is readable by every authenticated user (comment in packages/db/migrations/0011_documents.sql before the policies), and the seeded fund-level statement prints one block per commitment, client-directed ones included. So today a viewer can read a client-directed commitment's figures in that document while the commitment row is restricted. Linking each statement to its commitment position closes that.

==================================================
VS-2. Order of the sources
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. One list for every deal type: capital_account_balance, then sponsor_equity_value, then held_multiple. Record as decided. No code change.

Practice and reasons: the order follows how direct the evidence is. First the sponsor's stated value of our own interest, net of the vehicle's fees and carried interest (the capital account, which is what the practical expedient and IPEV start from). Then a value we derive from the sponsor's valuation of the company. Last an estimate from an operating figure at a multiple nobody restated. That ranking is the same for a co-investment, a continuation vehicle and a fund interest, so an order by deal type adds nothing. "The newer document first" is wrong: two sources for one quarter speak for the same date, and the direct one outranks the indirect one whenever it arrived. Of two statements for one quarter the later counts, which is right: sponsors send revised and final statements. A blend hides the source and is not how a valuation file is kept. "Statement only" would drop the owner's second ask (financial statements) and leave a co-investment with no indication in the weeks before its statement arrives.

If VS-3's addition is built, it sits second: statement, sponsor's holding value, equity value, multiple held.

At run time: the system picks the source and shows the others as cross-checks. A person never chooses the source; a person may dismiss.

Context check: accurate (selectSuggestion, readSources and derivationStep read as described).

==================================================
VS-3. The two report rules, and what "equity value" is
==================================================
Choice: keep rules 12.2 and 12.3. Equity is enterprise value less net debt. The record's total_equity is never used. Record as decided. Two refinements below, the first worth building, the second small.

Practice and reasons:
- total_equity in a company's accounts is book equity. It is not a valuation, and scaling a fair value by it would be wrong. Enterprise value less net debt is the sponsor's own valuation bridge and is the right figure.
- Rule 12.2 is a calibration (IPEV uses the word): the last approved mark stood in a known relation to the sponsor's equity value then, and the same relation is applied to the equity value now. It holds while our share is unchanged and the capital structure is plain.
- Rule 12.3 is a desk estimate, the usual one between sponsor valuations: new EBITDA at the last multiple, less new net debt. It is ours, not the sponsor's, so it must rank last and say so in its label (VS-6). Dropping it would leave nothing to suggest from a financial statement with no valuation page, which is the common case.

Refinement 1 (worth building, after VS-1): the sponsor's own fair value of its holding. The report schema already reads fund_fair_value and fund_cost (config/extraction_schema.json) and nothing uses them. That figure is the sponsor's valuation of the same security we hold, after the capital structure, so it is closer evidence than enterprise value less net debt. New basis sponsor_holding_value: S = M0 x F1 / F0, where F is the accepted fund_fair_value of the approved report run for each quarter, read from the runs' final values (finalValuesOf), so mon.quarterly_performance does not change. No value when fund_cost differs between the two quarters (the sponsor's holding changed; reason sponsor_holding_changed), plus the reasons of 12.2. Worked example: M0 7,310,450.25; F0 131,500,000.00 and F1 140,875,000.00 with fund cost 80,000,000.00 in both quarters; S = 7,310,450.25 x 140,875,000 / 131,500,000 = 7,831,632.54, the same as worked example 4 when the sponsor holds half the equity. With fund cost 90,000,000.00 in the new quarter: no value. Touched: SUGGESTION_BASES and SUGGESTION_METHODS in packages/calc, the basis check of mon.valuation_suggestion (the new table), suggestionSettings (the order is then four bases), docs/08 text, a fixture, the Python cross-check, the pages.

Refinement 2 (small, a tightening): rule 12.3 only when the reference mark is the previous quarter's (the flag previousQuarter already exists). Holding a multiple for one quarter is a normal approximation; holding it for a year is not supportable, because IPEV asks for a multiple that reflects the market at the measurement date. Reason code reference_not_previous_quarter.

At run time: the system computes; a missing input gives a reason and no value. A person judges whether the indication is a fair value.

Context check: accurate. The calculation in packages/calc/src/suggestion.ts matches docs/08.

==================================================
VS-4. A statement that does not tie, or whose contributions differ
==================================================
Choice: neither "always suggest" nor "always hold back". Four parts.
(1) Lines that do not add up: the value is suggested with the flag, as built.
(2) Accepting a suggestion with a failed check needs a written reason.
(3) Both check results and that reason are written on the Draft, so the lock hash covers them and both approvers read them on the version screen.
(4) Contributions far from our record give no value.

Practice and reasons:
- The two checks are different controls. Rolling the capital account (beginning + contributions - distributions + net result = ending) tests the reading and the document. When it fails, the ending balance is still the sponsor's stated figure, and the cause is usually a line the roll does not show (a transfer, syndication costs, carried interest printed apart). An analyst books the NAV and writes down why the roll broke. So: flag, reason, visible to the approvers.
- Reconciling the statement's cash to our own ledger is the identity control. Limited partner operations teams do it before they take any NAV from a statement. A wide gap means the statement is not our account as read: the wrong position, a whole vehicle read as ours, a wrong share or a wrong unit. A balance that fails this by a wide margin is not a suggestion a careful team would show as a mark. The system can establish that from the data on hand, so it should say so and give no value.
- The check as built compares the statement's contributions with flow_type.contribution only (summarizeFlows). Under the ILPA template a statement's contributions include capital called for fees and expenses inside the commitment, while the ledger here types fees and expenses apart. A fee-bearing vehicle would be flagged every quarter. The record side should be a band: from approved contributions (R1) to contributions plus fees plus expenses (R2). Any figure inside the band agrees with the record.

What must be built (docs/08 rule 12.1 text, fixture 29 and the Python cross-check first):
- contributionsCheck: agree when T is between R1 x (1 - tolerance) and R2 x (1 + tolerance). Differ outside it. New config key valuationSuggestions.contributionsHoldPct (0.5 suggested): beyond the band by more than that share, statementMark gives no value with a new reason contributions_far_from_record, placed after share_out_of_range. The next source in the order then gives the suggestion and the statement sits beside it with its reason.
- The suggestion machine (packages/workflows/src/machines.ts, accept and applyToDraft; docs/18 section 14): a new precondition, a reason when a check failed (ties false, contributions differ) or when the contributions could not be checked because the record has none. Rejection tests in machines.test.ts. The reason is kept on the suggestion: the check constraint on decision_reason in migration 0023 line 88 is relaxed in a new migration so an acceptance may carry one (the new table, not one the accounting import reads), and it goes in the audit event.
- suggestionDraftKeys (controls-suggestion.ts lines 1161 to 1181) adds suggestion.check.ties, suggestion.check.tieDifference, suggestion.check.contributions, suggestion.check.contributionsDifferencePct and suggestion.acceptReason. suggestionFacts in apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts words them on ValuationDetail.tsx.
- The prompt: net_gain_loss is answered only when the statement prints one total for everything between the two balances other than contributions and distributions; otherwise null, so the roll has no answer and does not fail wrongly.

Worked example: approved contributions 12,400,000.00, fees and expenses 620,000.00, so the band is 12,400,000.00 to 13,020,000.00. A statement showing 13,000,000.00: as built 4.84% off and flagged; decided, inside the band, they agree. 13,400,000.00: 2.92% above the band, flagged, reason needed at accept. 30,000,000.00: 130% above, no value. 49,600,000.00 (a whole vehicle read as ours): no value. Worked example 8 of docs/08 changes: its 30.00 case now gives no value.

At run time. The system decides: whether the lines add up, whether cash agrees, and whether the gap is too wide to call the balance ours. It sends to a person: the reason for accepting past a flag, and the cause of a statement held back.

Never automate: deciding that a failed check does not matter.

Context check: accurate. statementMark gives the value whatever the checks say, accept is not gated, and the Draft's keys do not carry the check results.

==================================================
VS-5. Cash flows since the last Locked mark (report rules)
==================================================
Choice: keep "no value" and never roll the scaled mark forward by the flows. Narrow which flows count: a fee or an expense no longer stops the rule. The page shows the flows that did stop it.

Practice and reasons:
- Adding flows to a scaled mark is wrong in the common cases. Example with worked example 4: M0 7,310,450.25 on equity of 263,000,000 is a share of about 2.78%. The company raises 20,000,000 from every holder pro rata; we pay about 555,928. The new equity value already contains the new money, so M0 x E1 / E0 is right as it stands, and adding the contribution overstates the mark by the whole contribution. A dividend funded by debt works the same way in reverse. If instead we alone bought or sold, scaling is wrong in the other direction. Nothing on record says which case a flow is. So a person must look.
- A fee or an expense pays the vehicle's costs. It changes neither our share nor the company's equity. As built, a routine fee call makes rule 12.2 not calculable every quarter for a fee-bearing vehicle, which is a wrong refusal. The seeded data has no fee or expense flow, which is why no test shows it.
- Rolling forward for cash is right for the other basis, the capital account. See the note at the end of this section.

What must be built (docs/08 rule 12.2 text, fixture 31 and apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/marks.py first): hasFlowBetween takes the flow type; new config key valuationSuggestions.stakeNeutralFlowTypes with flow_type.fee and flow_type.expense (config, not code); sources.ts passes types with dates; the reason sentence in apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts lists the date, type and amount of each flow that stopped the rule.

Worked example: a fee of 18,275.00 on 2025-05-15 after a mark for 2025-03-31. As built: flows_since_reference_mark and no value. Decided: S = 7,831,632.54. A contribution of 500,000.00 on the same day: no value, and the page shows that flow.

Recommended next rule, not part of this question (12.6, the rolled-forward balance). Practice for a quarter whose statement has not arrived is the last sponsor NAV plus contributions less distributions since (IPEV; common limited partner practice; docs/14 already has the scenario stale_valuation, "NAV rolled forward, needs footnote"). It should be its own basis with its own label, raised only from a Locked mark that rests on a statement, limited in age, and never called a sponsor mark, because an adjusted NAV is an estimate. It matters most once VS-1 is built, since a fund interest has calls most quarters. It needs a trigger of its own (no document arrives), so it is a separate slice.

At run time: the system decides whether a flow could have changed our stake. A person prepares the mark when one did.

Context check: accurate. Every approved flow type counts today (sources.ts line 358 passes dates only).

==================================================
VS-6. The valuation method a draft carries
==================================================
Choice: three labels. "Sponsor mark" for a statement (12.1). "Indication from sponsor report" for 12.2 (and for VS-3's addition). A new term for 12.3, for example valuation_method.held_multiple_estimate, "Estimate at the multiple held".

Practice and reasons: the method column is what a valuation committee and a report reader see. It should say how close the figure is to something the sponsor stated. A capital account balance is the sponsor's value of our interest. Rule 12.2 rests on the sponsor's valuation of the company. Rule 12.3 rests on a sponsor's EBITDA and our own held multiple; calling that an indication from the sponsor's report overstates what the sponsor said. "Sponsor mark" for all three would hide the difference that matters most. A separate label for a statement against a sponsor mark typed by hand is not needed: the sealed keys already say which document and page the value came from.

What must be built: one taxonomy row in a new migration (0023 is the last on disk and decision 0024 is in progress, so take the next free number after checking), SUGGESTION_METHODS in packages/calc/src/terms.ts and its Python twin, the API test and e2e test that name labels. A Locked version keeps the code it was locked with, so nothing on record changes.

Context check: accurate as far as read (migration 0023 lines 227 to 235). I did not open terms.ts.

==================================================
VS-7. Source reviewers and the two approvals
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. Source reviewers are refused both approvals. Add two things: whoever approved the record a figure is cited to is named and barred too, and the system says before accept when the bar would leave nobody able to approve.

Practice and reasons, stated plainly: common practice is four eyes on a mark, meaning the preparer is not the approver (SEC-5.6), and the final approval sits with someone independent of the work. The deal lead who checked the sponsor's report normally also gives the deal team's sign-off, so the bar as built is stricter than common practice and stricter than SEC-5.6. The owner's guidance of 2026-11-11 (no safeguards beyond those the build guide names) points the same way. But loosening it lets more people take an approval, which is a permission change and not the build's to take, and an option exists that avoids it: keep the bar. It is workable: when operations reviews the run and prepares the valuation, three people are needed, the same as for any valuation. The record should say exactly this, so the owner can drop notSourceReviewer from dealTeamApprove with one line if he wants common practice.

What must be built:
- The gap. A figure cited to the record names nobody (recordFigureOf, controls-suggestion.ts lines 585 to 609). mon.quarterly_performance.approved_by holds who approved the promotion that wrote the quarter, an import included. Name that person as the reviewer of a record-cited figure. This narrows, so it may be taken. It rarely bites, since import approvers are in operations and take neither approval.
- The dead end. At accept and applyToDraft, count as the system actor the people inside the position's wall who could still take the deal team approval and the lock. When either count is zero, the confirmation says so before anything is written, with counts and no names. The remedy stays a person's: an approver adds a wall member, or another person reviews the document.

At run time: the system works out who is barred, refuses them, and warns of a dead end. Never automate: an override of the bar.

Context check: accurate where read (machines.ts lines 14 to 25 and 61 to 79; the table checks in 0004_mon.sql). I did not open ValuationWorkflowService.sourceReviewers.

==================================================
VS-8. Expected documents for position-level statements
==================================================
Choice: track them, by a rule the system can establish. A statement is expected every quarter for every commitment position and every continuation vehicle position. For a co-investment it is expected from the quarter after the first one arrives.

Practice and reasons: an operations team tracks deliverables per holding. Wherever we are a limited partner of record (a fund, a continuation fund, a co-investment vehicle) the partnership agreement obliges the sponsor to send a capital account statement each quarter. A co-investment held directly in the company has none. The repository does not hold which co-investments go through a vehicle, and it does not need to: a position that has sent a statement is one whose sponsor issues them. That avoids a standing "missing" row for a sponsor who never sends one and still catches a statement that stops coming.

What must be built: generate in apps/api/src/documents/expected.service.ts adds capital_account_statement rows keyed on investment_id (the unique index exists). New config under documents: the deal types always expected (primary_fund, cv_single_asset, cv_multi_asset) and those expected after a first statement (co_invest_equity). expectedParentOf in packages/validation/src/documents.ts answers for the position; the preview twin; the test in statement-guards.test.ts that pins "fills no fund row" is replaced; matrix counts; docs/04 M3 and docs/18 section 6. With VS-1 the row per sponsor fund goes.

At run time: the system decides what is expected and whether it is late or missing. A person chases the sponsor.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-9. What tells a company-specific statement from a fund statement
==================================================
Choice: the two names on page one, the entity that issues the statement and the investor it is for, matched exactly. A statement that does not come to exactly one position goes to a person. It is never taken to be a fund statement by default. A person's choice is remembered as an exact alias.

Practice and reasons: a sponsor's statement does not carry our internal reference, and a real file name follows no convention. What every capital account statement does carry is the name of the partnership whose capital it reports (the fund, or the sponsor's co-investment vehicle) and the name of the partner it is prepared for (our vehicle). Operations teams keep exactly that map: issuing entity to holding, investor of record to our vehicle, set up at closing from the subscription documents. The dangerous case in the build today is the silent one: a statement that names only the sponsor's co-investment vehicle is classified as a fund statement, fills the fund's row and raises nothing. The rule has to be right whichever way real documents name a position, and "exactly one, or a person" is right in every case.

What must be built:
- classifierOutputSchema (packages/ai/src/schema.ts) gains issuerName and investorName; CLASSIFIER_TASK asks for them; prompt version 2; the prototype keywordClassifier reads them from the synthetic layout; evals.
- A small new table, for example core.investment_alias (investment_id, alias, unique after normalising, history), read under the position's own visibility. core.company_alias names companies and core.fund_alias names funds; this names the sponsor's vehicle that issues our statement for one position, so it is not a second table for the same job. Record it in the decision.
- statementHold: issuer equals a position's alias, that position. Issuer equals a sponsor fund's name or alias, that fund's commitment position, chosen by the investor name when we have several. Anything else, NeedsReview. When the reviewer chooses the position, the issuer name read is stored as that position's alias in the same transaction, audited, so the next quarter links itself.
- A narrowing to consider with it: a statement held for review with no position link is readable by every authenticated user today (0011_documents.sql). Until it is linked it should be read by operations only, since it may belong to a walled position.

At run time: the system decides the link when the names come to exactly one and learns each new name once. A person decides the first time and every time it is not exactly one.

Never automate: a fuzzy match, or a default to "fund".

Context check: accurate. One practice remark: the classifier asks for our reference on page one, which a real sponsor document will not normally print; it exists in the synthetic layout only.

==================================================
VS-10. Putting a classified document on the right position
==================================================
Choice: build the relink, allowed after approval too. Operations send a Classified document back to NeedsReview with a reason. Its approved runs are withdrawn, and everything that rested on them is refreshed or flagged. No mark and no record figure is changed by the command.

Practice and reasons: misfiling is routine and is usually found late, through the figure it produced. The option named in the record (refused once a run is approved) fails in exactly that case. A correction must be possible, must be audited, and must cascade: derived items are withdrawn or replaced, never edited. It must also never wait on a valuation: a document sitting behind the wrong wall is a barrier breach while it stays there.

What must be built:
- docs/18 section 6: a new row, Classified to NeedsReview, operations, reason required, with rejection tests (documentClassificationMachine, machines.ts lines 410 to 447). Classified stops being terminal.
- doc.extraction_run gains withdrawn_by, withdrawn_at and withdrawn_reason (not a table the accounting import reads). A withdrawn run is not on hand: sources.ts filters it, so the wrongly linked statement stops being the statement on hand and stops sitting beside later suggestions as a cross-check.
- In the same transaction: refreshPosition for the old position (Open suggestions are superseded, figures_changed); the expected row's received_document_id is cleared and matched again; duplicates follow the original.
- What a person had already approved on top of the document is flagged, not undone: an Accepted suggestion on a version in progress or Locked, and a quarter of mon.quarterly_performance promoted from the run, each open an ops.data_exception for operations (walled by its entity, as migration 0014 does for other entities). The remedy is a normal command: edit or send back the Draft, reopen the Locked mark, approve the right report.
- Whoever picks the new position must be able to see it. Audit with before and after. The line RELINK_NOTE in apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts and the note in ExtractionRun.tsx go.

At run time: the system withdraws, refreshes and flags. A person decides the relink and every correction of an approved figure.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-11. Private credit
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. No suggestion for a private credit position. Record as decided, with the reason below.

Practice and reasons: the platform's credit mark is a fair value to which accrued interest is added (docs/08 section 10: MOIC and IRR use fair value plus accrued interest as the terminal value). A capital account balance for a credit vehicle already includes accrued income. Putting that balance on a credit position by rule 12.1 would count accrued interest twice in every return figure. And no rule on hand can build a loan's fair value: par plus accrued is right only for a performing loan, and deciding that a loan is performing is a credit judgment a careful team does not automate. So the system should read and review a credit statement, raise nothing, and say why, which is what it does.

What would change it later: docs/08 section 10 first says whether the credit mark is with or without accrued interest; then a rule could give the balance less the quarter's approved accrued interest, with a reason when that figure is missing.

At run time: the reason private_credit on the run page. A person prepares a credit mark.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-12. Positions not in US dollars
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. No suggestion, nothing converted. Record as decided.

Practice and reasons: practice is to value the interest in its own currency and translate at the period-end rate from one named, approved rate source. The platform is US dollar only (docs/03 line 9), has no rate table, and a rate nobody approved is an invented number. Converting would need a rate source (possibly paid) and a mark that can carry another currency, which touches mon.valuation. Neither is the build's to take. The system is correct whatever the owner later decides, because it refuses loudly with the reason not_usd.

Never automate: the choice of rate source.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-13. Who reads the keys a suggestion leaves on a valuation
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. Every role that reads the valuation reads the keys. Record as decided.

Practice and reasons: the workings behind an approved mark are part of the valuation file, and whoever is trusted with the mark may see what it rests on. Investor relations answer clients' questions about valuations from that file. What is kept to the valuation group in practice is the work in progress: open, dismissed and not calculable indications, which is the suggestion list and stays with four roles. Narrowing here would be an API rule only, because mon.valuation.inputs is one column under one policy, so the layers would disagree (SEC-5.1), and it would either leave the lock text showing the same keys or take away the check of the hash against its text. The keys hold nothing of a higher class than those roles already read on the same position.

Context check: not re-read beyond detail() in workflow.service.ts, which serves the inputs in every state and the lock text once Locked, as described.

==================================================
VS-14. History tables and the wall
==================================================
Choice: wall them now, for every record and not only suggestions. One rule: a history row is read by whoever the base table's policy would admit for that row, and still only by operations, approvers and auditors. Build's to take, since it only narrows.

Practice and reasons: an information barrier has to hold on every copy of the data. A wall that hides a valuation and shows its history image to the same role outside the wall is not a wall, and "no route reads it" is one layer where SEC-5.1 asks for two. Auditors outside a wall already do not read the walled base rows, so they lose nothing they had by design.

What must be built: one migration that changes audit.add_history (packages/db/migrations/0001_baseline.sql lines 113 to 132) and the policies of the tables already created; the wall key read from row_image (investment_id for most, row_id for core.investment, the parent for child tables); the client rule the same way for the client-restricted tables, so the rule is in one place even though the three roles read every client today; the *_history rows of READ_MATRIX and pnpm test:rls.

Two more places with the same fault, to record with it:
- audit.event details hold fair values and suggested values and are read by role alone. Same principle. It has no uniform key, so do it as the next step and keep the test that no route serves the details.
- ops.task. A reopen writes a task titled "Valuation reopened: <investment number> <period>" (apps/api/src/valuations/workflow.service.ts lines 697 to 708) and ops.task is readable by every authenticated user (0006_history_rls.sql line 188). For a walled position that names the position to everyone. The title should carry no identifier, or the task should be walled by the record it regards.

Context check: accurate. The ops.task point is in the collected context as a fact and is a defect.

==================================================
VS-15. A share read from a statement that is in fact our own account
==================================================
Choice: a new review decision, "not stated in the document", made by a named person. After it the share is absent and the lines are read as our own account.

Practice and reasons: the reviewer's true statement is "the document gives no share". Today the only ways through are to reject the share and lose the statement, or to type 1.0000, which records a stated share of 100% that the document does not contain and names the reviewer as the source of a figure that is not there. A valuation file must not hold a figure the source does not show (SEC-9.4). The decision is also the honest answer for any field a model answers where the document is silent.

What must be built: the decision check of stg.extracted_value (0011_documents.sql around line 171, a staging table) gains not_stated; the review command and its contract (extractionRunCommandBody); shareLeftOpen (controls-suggestion.ts lines 502 to 507) is false after not_stated, so statementOnHandOf reads the lines as our own account, and the reviewer who said so is a source reviewer; the workings line reads "Read as our own account: the reviewer marked the share as not stated"; docs/08 rule 12.1, fixture 29, docs/18 sections 7 and 14; the preview twin and the review pages. A typed share of exactly 1 stays allowed (a single-investor vehicle does state 100%), and the page says which choice is which.

The other direction (a wrong share accepted) is covered by VS-4: a wide gap in contributions now gives no value, and a smaller one needs a reason that both approvers read.

Never automate: deciding own account against whole vehicle when the document is unclear.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-16. Who may act on a suggestion
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. Operations alone accept, apply and dismiss. Record as decided.

Practice and reasons: preparing a valuation belongs to the function that is independent of the investment decision; the deal team gives input and the first approval. A dismissal is remembered and silences that value from that source, so letting the investment side dismiss would let it set aside an indication it does not like. Widening is a permission change in any case.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-17. An accounting import: refresh only, or also raise
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. An import refreshes Open suggestions and raises none. Record as decided.

Practice and reasons: a suggestion should be raised by a sponsor document that a person reviewed, which is what the owner asked for. An import restates the record from the accounting side. In the control flow the accounting record follows the Locked mark, so an import for a quarter with no mark is something to look at, not a reason to propose one. Nothing is lost: figures an import brings refresh every Open suggestion at once and are used the moment any statement or report for the position and quarter is approved. Raising from an import would also need a suggestion that names an intake file in place of a run, for little gain.

One related change is in VS-7: the approver of the import becomes the named reviewer of a figure cited to the record.

Context check: accurate (promote.ts line 240; derivationStep answers none_open on a refresh; the two columns are NOT NULL and the guard trigger checks the run).

==================================================
VS-18. A cash flow approved later, or a position that closes
==================================================
Choice: refresh at once. The transaction that approves a cash flow refreshes the Open suggestions of its position, in a way that can never refuse the funding command.

Practice and reasons: the list is a work queue. A value that accept would refuse should not sit there as if it were good, and with VS-5 the flow decides whether a report rule gives a value at all.

What must be built: writeCashFlow in apps/api/src/capital/capital.service.ts (lines 715 to 760) calls refreshPosition for the position, and with VS-1 for the position of the commitment. The refresh runs behind a savepoint: if the advisory lock is not free within the wait, the refresh is skipped, logged with a code, and the funding goes through. Accept still derives again, so nothing wrong can be accepted. docs/18 sections 3 and 14, an API test in suggestions.test.ts, and the preview hook.

At run time: the system keeps the queue true.

Context check: one correction. The context says a position that is exited or made inactive does not refresh its Open suggestions. I found no API command that exits a position or sets it inactive at all (no write of exit_date or is_active under apps/api/src other than the insert in convert.ts). So there is nothing to hook today. The rule should be written in docs/18 section 14 now, so that whichever command first exits a position calls the refresh.

==================================================
VS-19. applyToDraft and the Draft's date
==================================================
Choice: keep as built. The Draft keeps its date. Add one sentence where the two dates differ. Record as decided.

Practice and reasons: a few days between a sponsor's book date and the calendar quarter end is immaterial, and the platform already treats both as the same quarter everywhere (alignedQuarterEnd). Refusing would send a person to discard a Draft that cannot be deleted. Moving the date is not an edit the workflow has, and versions follow the exact date. What matters is that both dates are on record, and they are: the Draft's own date and the key suggestion.periodEnd. The confirmation and the version screen should say "This Draft is dated <date>; the statement is for <date>. The Draft keeps its date." when they differ.

Context check: accurate (editDraft never writes period_end).

==================================================
VS-20. One variance flag for a quarter's change and for two sources
==================================================
Choice: keep one flag (valuationVarianceFlag, 0.1). Record as decided.

Practice and reasons: ten percent is a normal review trigger for a quarter's move. Two sources for one date could be held to a tighter test, but the report indications ignore the vehicle's fees and carried interest and the capital structure, so they sit a few percent from a capital account balance for structural reasons. A tighter flag would fire most quarters and teach people to ignore it. The cross-check blocks nothing and the statement is the stronger source. Revisit when real spreads have been seen. One case does need its own tighter figure, and it is a different question: a later statement against a Locked mark (VS-28).

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-21. The tie tolerances and the contributions tolerance
==================================================
Choice: the values are right for what they assume. The form should change: work the tie tolerance out from the decimal places the lines were printed with. Keep 2% for contributions, against the band of VS-4.

Practice and reasons: an analyst rolling a statement accepts a difference of half a unit of the last printed place per line, no more. The fixed table assumes whole thousands and two places in millions and billions. Many statements in millions print one place. Example: true lines 18.44, 2.04, 0.36, 0.84 and 20.96 print as 18.4, 2.0, 0.4, 0.8 and 21.0; the printed lines give 20.8, a gap of 200,000.00 on a correct statement, which fails the 25,000 test. With VS-4 that would cost a written reason every quarter for nothing. The system can establish the print precision from the figures on hand, so it should.

What must be built (docs/08 rule 12.1 text, fixture 29 worked examples 2 and 8, the Python cross-check first): statementTieTolerance = the number of lines in the roll x half of one unit of the last decimal place printed, at the coarsest line (five lines: 2,500 for whole thousands, 25,000 for two places in millions, 250,000 for one place), never under rollForwardToleranceUsd. When that tolerance is more than a configured share of the ending balance (a new key, 1% suggested), the roll is "not checked: printed too coarsely" and not a pass. The staged value must keep its trailing zeros as printed: add that to EXTRACTION_VALUE_RULES with an eval case. valuationSuggestions.tieToleranceByUnit is then no longer read and is removed.

Contributions: 2% matches the platform's other "these two should agree" settings (intake.lookthroughNavTolerancePct). Keep it.

Priority: below VS-1, VS-4, VS-15 and VS-28. If it is not built now, record that the table is right only for statements printed as its note assumes.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-22. The five second wait
==================================================
Choice: keep it a constant in code. Record as decided.

Reasons: it is how long one database lock is waited for, not a business threshold. Rule 9 lists fund lists, report specs, tiers, thresholds, phrases and colours. No practice view bears on it. docs/17 can name it among the timeouts if a line is wanted.

Context check: accurate.

==================================================
VS-23. Outbox events and telling someone
==================================================
Choice: no outbox writer and no task of its own for suggestions. The Suggestions tab, the board column and the work list are the queue. The outbox is built once for every workflow, as its own slice.

Practice and reasons: valuation work is run from a queue. An Open suggestion is already the item of work; a task per suggestion would be the same thing twice, and ops.task is readable by every authenticated user, so a task about a walled position would name it (see VS-14). docs/18 line 3 asks every transition to emit an outbox event and nothing in apps/api/src writes ops.outbox: that is a gap of the whole platform. Fixing it for one feature would put one rule in two places. Every transition already writes an audit event in its own transaction, so that one writer is where the outbox row belongs, with codes only and no figures (SEC-11.4), for all machines at once.

Worth adding for the queue itself: the work list shows how many suggestions are open and how old the oldest is, so none sits unseen.

Context check: accurate. The only task insert in apps/api/src is the one a reopen writes.

VS-24. Measuring the statement extraction
==================================================
Choice: the measure stays the prototype's until an endpoint is approved, and the record says so in those words. Whether a real endpoint is approved is the one fact here that only the owner has. Everything else is decided and can be built now.

Practice and reasons: a model whose output feeds a valuation is measured on a gold set before anyone relies on it and after every prompt change. The system is safe whatever the answer, because no figure is on hand until a person accepts it and no mark exists until two more people approve it.

What to build now, with synthetic documents only: widen the gold set of statements from one page in one layout to the cases the decisions above turn on: a roll with fees, expenses, income, gains and carried interest on separate lines; gross and net ending balances both printed; thousands, and millions to one place; a whole vehicle with and without our share; several investor accounts; another currency; the issuer and investor names of VS-9. tools/synthetic/src/statement-examples.ts already holds some. Then pnpm test:evals against an approved development endpoint is a one-line change when one exists.

==================================================
VS-27. How the feature goes to review
==================================================
Choice: one pull request per slice, stacked in the order of the slices, with each review fix folded into the slice it corrects. The decisions built now follow as their own pull requests. Merging is the owner's act.

Practice and reasons: a change to a valuation control is reviewed rule first. The calculation with docs/08 section 12, its fixtures and the Python cross-check should be readable alone, before the machine, the table, the guards, the routes and the pages. One pull request for the whole feature cannot be reviewed by a person. The Reporting, Packages commits on the same branch go in their own stack.

==================================================
VS-28. A statement that arrives for a quarter already Locked
==================================================
Choice: do not stay silent. Always compare the statement's value with the Locked mark and show the result. When they differ by a configured share or more, open a data exception for operations. A reopen, which stays a person's command, then opens a suggestion from the statement on hand. Nothing reopens by itself.

Practice and reasons: sponsors send final and audited statements after a first mark is locked. Comparing the final figure with the booked mark and truing up when the difference is material is a standard control, and auditors ask for it. A match is evidence too: "the final statement agrees with the Locked mark" belongs in the file. Raising nothing, as built, means the most direct evidence about a mark arrives and nobody is told.

What must be built:
- A pure check beside suggestionBar: when the bar is mark_locked for the statement's own quarter and the statement gives a value, sourceCrossCheck of that value against the Locked mark, with a new config key (valuationSuggestions.lockedMarkDifferencePct, 0.02 suggested, the figure the platform uses elsewhere for two numbers that should agree). The board's 10% flag is too loose here: both figures are for one date.
- The run page (runOutcomeText in apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts) says either "Agrees with the Locked mark of <value> within 2%" or "The mark for this quarter is Locked at <X>. This statement gives <Y>, <Z>% away."
- At or above the setting: one ops.data_exception against the Locked valuation, opened as the system actor, with its own rule id, readable inside the position's wall only (a new entity clause as in migration 0014). Operations resolve it with a reason, by reopening or by recording why not (docs/18 section 9, unchanged).
- The refresh after a reopen runs in opening mode for that quarter, so the statement already approved raises its suggestion and applyToDraft puts its value on the new Draft with its keys and its source reviewers. Today a reopen refreshes only, so the value would be typed by hand with no source on it. docs/18 section 14, tests in suggestions.test.ts and suggestion-commands.test.ts.
- A report approved for a Locked quarter: shown on the run page, no exception. It is an indication, not the sponsor's value of our interest.

Worked example: Locked mark 7,831,632.54 for the quarter, taken from a report indication. The statement arrives with an ending balance of 7,810,000.00: 21,632.54 lower, 0.28%, under 2%: the run page says it agrees and nothing else happens. With 8,300,000.00: 468,367.46 higher, 5.98%: an exception opens; operations reopen with a reason; the suggestion opens at 8,300,000.00; it is applied to the Draft and goes through both approvals.

At run time: the system compares, records the result and tells operations when it matters. Never automate: the reopen, and the judgment that a difference is immaterial.

Context check: accurate (suggestionBar line 224; a reopen calls the derivation in refresh mode).
