## control:valuation-suggestions

CONTROL REVIEW: valuation suggestions (decision 0023), VS-1 to VS-28

Method. Read only. Nothing was run, built or written. Every statement about behaviour comes from reading the files named; where I rely on a pure function and saw no test that pins the case, I say "as read". Rules used: CLAUDE.md rules 5, 7, 9, 10, 12, 14 and section 4; docs/05 SEC ids; docs/18 sections 1, 6, 7, 9, 14; docs/03; docs/08 section 12. Public standards named where relied on (two searches, no repository words in a query): the IPEV Valuation Guidelines, part on valuing fund interests (reported net asset value, adjusted for calls and distributions since); the ILPA Reporting Template (capital account roll-forward from beginning to ending NAV, fees and expenses itemized).

FINDINGS THAT CUT ACROSS THE QUESTIONS (new, not in the collected context)

F1. Fund-level statements cross the client rule today (SEC-5.2, SEC-5.4). doc.document admits every signed-in user to a document linked to no position (packages/db/migrations/0011_documents.sql lines 202 to 203). GET /api/v1/documents/{id}/pages has roles [] (packages/contracts/src/routes.ts lines 511 to 516) and answers the page text (apps/api/src/documents/documents.service.ts, pages, lines 659 to 681). The generator prints one block per commitment, "Account <vehicle>" with Commitment, Contributions, Distributions and Unfunded, the client-directed commitment included (tools/synthetic/src/documents.ts buildStatement lines 978 to 999; generate.ts lines 379 to 388). core.commitment refuses that row to a viewer, a platform admin and investor relations of another client (matrix row core.commitment, 1 of 2). So those roles read in a document what the table refuses them. It predates decision 0023.
F2. A dismissed statement shadows the report rules for its quarter. derivationStep (packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts lines 1098 to 1134) is handed one selection, the first source with a value (selectSuggestion, lines 881 to 892), and only then asks whether it was dismissed. While the dismissed statement's run stays the statement on hand (apps/api/src/suggestions/sources.ts lines 301 to 326), every later derivation selects it again and answers none, because dismissed. No report-based suggestion is raised for that quarter (as read; the unit test at controls-suggestion.test.ts lines 1452 to 1470 pins the single-selection behaviour).
F3. The source reviewer bar can be walked around with an accounting import. recordFigureOf returns no reviewers for a figure cited to the record (controls-suggestion.ts lines 585 to 609), although mon.quarterly_performance.approved_by names who approved the write (apps/api/src/intake/promote.ts lines 196 to 199).
F4. "Not in US dollars" rests on one sentence of a prompt (packages/ai/src/registry.ts line 106). No field carries the statement's currency and the unit list has dollar units only, so no deterministic rule can refuse a euro balance read as USD_M.
F5. Two more places show walled records by role alone. GET /api/v1/audit/events (routes.ts lines 279 to 283, roles operations, approver, auditor, platform_admin) answers entity, entityId and the free-text reason of every event (apps/api/src/audit/audit.service.ts lines 49 to 58) with no wall clause (policy read_audit, 0006_history_rls.sql line 214). ops.task is read by every signed-in user (0006 line 188) and a reopen writes "Valuation reopened: <investment number> <period>" (apps/api/src/valuations/workflow.service.ts lines 697 to 711); no route reads tasks yet.
F6. A fund commitment already has two unconnected forms. Seeded commitments are core.commitment rows with no position. Closing a primary fund deal writes a core.investment of deal_type.primary_fund and a portfolio company row named from the deal (apps/api/src/pipeline/convert.ts lines 112 to 166) and no core.commitment row (no insert into core.commitment exists under apps/api/src).
F7. A wrong accept has no exit. Accepted is terminal, the suggestion keys cannot be dropped from a Draft (keepSuggestionKeys), and a valuation Draft cannot be withdrawn or deleted. A statement of position B accepted onto position A leaves B's balance sealed in A's valuation inputs for good.
F8. No API path writes core.investment.exit_date or is_active after conversion (grep of apps/api/src), so "a position that closes" (VS-18) has no command to hook today.

VS-1. How a fund commitment carries a mark
Choice: option (a), one position per commitment (core.investment, deal_type.primary_fund), the mark in mon.valuation through valuationMachine unchanged. Built now for pooled commitments (client_id null). A client-directed commitment keeps "read, reviewed, no suggestion", with a reason of its own, until the system actor may read it.
Rules. Rule 5 and SEC-9.1: a mark exists only as a mon.valuation row moved by the transition table of docs/18 section 1. Rule 7 forbids a second valuation record or workflow, so (c) is out. mon.valuation.investment_id is NOT NULL (0004_mon.sql line 85) and the accounting file is compared with that table (config/accounting_layout.json, FairValue), so (b) changes the schema of an accounting table and is not the build's to take. (e) gives a suggestion nowhere to be accepted. (d) leaves the fund of funds book with no mark, against practice: IPEV values a fund interest from the reported net asset value of our account. That leaves (a), which docs/03 line 48 already describes ("fund-level rows for primaries") and convert.ts already produces.
Where (a) would leak, and how to avoid it. A position is read by every signed-in user outside a wall (0006 line 124); a client-directed commitment is read under the client rule (0006 lines 134 to 135). A position for a client-directed commitment would show its existence and its NAV to a viewer, a platform admin and investor relations of other clients (SEC-5.2, SEC-5.4). Closing that needs a client clause on core.investment and, because every derivation runs as the system actor, it needs the service role to read client-directed commitments and their flows. Today it does not (pb.sees_all_clients, 0017 line 20, has no service; matrix core.commitment service = 1 of 2). That widens what a role reads (CLAUDE.md section 4). The option that avoids it is to mark pooled commitments only.
Build (tests first where a rule moves).
1. Migration: core.commitment.position_id uuid null unique references core.investment, with a trigger that the position is deal_type.primary_fund on the same vehicle and sponsor fund. core.commitment is not read by the accounting layout. No change to core.investment or mon.valuation.
2. One function opens the position of a commitment; convert.ts calls it and also writes the core.commitment row for a closed primary fund deal, so F6 ends: one commitment, one position, always together. The portfolio company row standing for the fund is what convert.ts does today; making portfolio_company_id nullable would change an accounting table, so it stays, and is recorded as the weak point.
3. suggestionBar: a primary fund position passes for the statement basis only; fund_commitment stays for a statement linked to no position; new reason client_directed_commitment. EQUITY_DEAL_TYPES is untouched (tracker, board, value creation).
4. sources.ts reads a fund position's flows by its commitment (commitment_id), through one helper every reader of a position's flows uses, or the contributions check silently answers no_recorded_contributions and the position shows a NAV with no cost.
5. docs/18 section 6 and expectedParentOf: the kind of a statement becomes the deal type of the position it links to, not "linked or not"; a statement linked to a fund position still fills the fund's row.
6. No new math: rule 12.1 as written. Worked example: our account in US$ millions, beginning 18.42, contributions 2.00, distributions 0.35, net gain 0.87, ending 20.94, gives 20,940,000.00; a whole-fund 83.76 at a 25.00% share gives the same; contributions to date 12.40 against 12,400,000.00 of approved commitment flows agree.
7. F1 fixed in the same slice: a capital account statement of a sponsor fund that has a client-directed commitment is read under the client rule (a narrowing of doc.document and of its runs and staged values).
Run time. The system links a fund statement when it comes to exactly one account, works out the value, shows why, and bars the run's reviewers. A person gets: a statement that names no single account (two commitments, one fund), every accept, both approvals. Still open and narrow: whether the service role may read client-directed commitments; until then the system answers the reason and no value, which is correct either way.
Proof. API test: fund statement approved, suggestion 20,940,000.00, accepted, three people to Locked as a sponsor mark. Matrix: counts of core.investment and mon.valuation for viewer, platform admin and investor relations do not rise for a client-directed commitment. A viewer gets 404 on the pages of a statement that lists a client account. A schema test that mon.valuation is unchanged.
Context check. Item 3 treats primary fund positions in NAV readers as a hazard; they belong in NAV, only company metrics must show the missing placeholder. Not in the context: F6, and that the system actor does not see client-directed commitments, so the tracker (expected.service.ts lines 141 to 143, run as the system actor) expects statements for pooled commitments only.

VS-2. Order of sources
Choice: keep as built: statement, then enterprise value, then multiple held; one list in config; never a blend. Decided, no change to the order.
Rules. SEC-9.4: a blend has no one source to cite. Rule 9: the order is config and suggestionSettings refuses anything but the three, once each. Practice: the sponsor's stated value of our own interest ranks above an estimate built from company figures (IPEV). An order by deal type has no rule behind it and adds a config shape.
Build: fix F2. A source whose basis, value and inputs were dismissed steps aside: the suggestion is the first source with a value that was not dismissed in that form, and the dismissed one stays beside it as a cross-check, marked dismissed. Pure change in selectSuggestion and derivationStep, shared with the preview; docs/18 section 14 text.
Run time. The system picks the source and says which; a person decides nothing here.
Proof. Unit and API test: statement dismissed, then the quarter's report approved, opens a sponsor_equity_value suggestion (7,831,632.54 on the figures of worked example 4) with the dismissed 7,810,000.00 beside it; dismissing that too leaves none.
Context check. VS-10's context says the dismissed statement "stays beside any later report-based suggestion as a cross-check". As read, no later report-based suggestion is raised at all (F2).

VS-3. The two report rules and what equity is
Choice: keep rules 12.2 and 12.3 and equity as enterprise value less net debt. Decided.
Rules. Rule 10: total_equity may be book equity; calling it fair value would give a figure a meaning its source does not state. SEC-9.4 holds: every input is cited to a page, a typing reviewer, the record or a Locked mark. Rule 12: any other rule needs docs/08 text, fixtures 30 and 31, properties and the Python check first. 12.3 is an estimate, last in the order, and only ever a suggestion that three people then judge; its basis held_multiple is sealed in the Draft's keys.
Not required by any rule, allowed later: scaling by the sponsor fund's own fair value of its holding, read from the approved run's final values (never a new column on mon.quarterly_performance). It would be a fourth basis: a check constraint on the new table, a longer sourceOrder, tests first.
Run time. System: value or one reason in the fixed order. Person: nothing beyond accept and the approvals.
Proof. Existing fixtures 30 and 31 and the cross-check; add nothing.
Context check. Correct. Add F3: a figure "of the record" has an approver the rule ignores (see VS-7).

VS-4. A statement that does not tie, or whose contributions differ
Choice: suggest with a flag (as built), plus two changes so the flag cannot be lost on the way to a Locked mark.
Rules. Rule 10 is kept: the value is the statement's own. Rule 5 is kept: nothing is approved by the system. SEC-9.5 says a failed reconciliation (it names the NAV roll-forward) passes only with a stated reason. SEC-9.3: the lock covers what the mark rests on. Today the two approvers of the valuation see a failed check only if they open the suggestion: suggestionDraftKeys (controls-suggestion.ts lines 1161 to 1181) writes no check result, and the checks are recomputed at each read from a record that can move. Holding the value back is not required and would block a correct statement whenever our own record is the one that is late.
Build.
1. Seal the checks as accept saw them: keys suggestion.check.ties (true, false, not_checked), .tieDifference, .tieToleranceUsd, .contributions (agree, differ, not_checked), .contributionsDifferencePct. The version screen words them for every reader of the valuation.
2. A reason at accept and applyToDraft when ties is false or contributions differ: a precondition of valuationSuggestionMachine on a record key checkFailed, a new line in the table of docs/18 section 14, rejection tests (rule 14). The reason goes on mon.valuation_suggestion.decision_reason (relax the check at 0023 line 88 to "Dismissed has one, Accepted may") and in the audit event. Not in the Draft keys: free text does not belong in the lock text.
Worked example: net gain printed 0.92 gives tieDifference -50,000.00 against 25,000: S stays 20,940,000.00, accept without a reason is 422, with one it opens the Draft, and the Locked text carries check.ties false. A whole vehicle read as ours: contributions to date 49.60 against 12,400,000.00 on record, 300%: same path.
Run time. System: both checks, the badges, the sealed keys. Person: the reason, and the two approvals with the failed check in front of them.
Proof. machines.test.ts (accept refused without a reason when checkFailed); API test that the lock text holds the check keys; page test of the version screen.
Context check. Correct that accept is not gated. Add: R is contributions only; fees and expenses are summed apart (packages/calc/src/cashflows.ts summarizeFlows), which matters for VS-21.

VS-5. Cash moved since the last Locked mark
Choice: no value, with the reason. No roll-forward. One narrowing: only flows that can change our stake count.
Rules. Rule 10: a roll-forward has to guess. A follow-on raises E1 through lower net debt, so adding the contribution counts it twice; a dividend recap already lowers E1, so subtracting the distribution counts it twice; a sell-down needs the opposite. The data on hand cannot tell which. A position-level statement (12.1) is the source that is right after a flow, and it is unaffected.
Build (tests first: docs/08 12.2, fixture 31, marks.py). hasFlowBetween is fed the flows whose type is not in valuationSuggestions.flowTypesThatLeaveTheStake (config, default fee and expense). Contribution, distribution, recallable (equalization maps to recallable, capital.service.ts line 129), interest and principal all count.
Worked example: M0 7,310,450.25 at 2025-03-31. An expense call of 12,000.00 on 2025-05-15: today no value; decided: 7,831,632.54 stands. A contribution of 500,000.00 on the same day: no value, flows_since_reference_mark.
Run time. System decides from the flow types. Person: nothing; a suggestion with a reason can only be dismissed.
Proof. Fixture 31 cases on each flow type at both edges of the window; Python twin.
Context check. Correct.

VS-6. Method labels
Choice: keep: "Sponsor mark" for a statement, "Indication from sponsor report" for both report bases. Decided, no code.
Rules. The method is in the lock text, so a new code changes nothing already Locked. The basis (capital_account_balance, sponsor_equity_value, held_multiple) is sealed in the keys, so a Locked mark can always be told apart whatever the label. No client report and no accounting column reads the method. "Sponsor mark" for both would call an estimate of ours a sponsor's mark: refused.
Condition recorded: the day a method label is printed on anything a client reads, the multiple held gets a label of its own first.
Proof. Existing tests of the two labels.

VS-7. Source reviewers
Choice: keep the bar at both approvals, with no override, and close two gaps.
Rules. SEC-5.6 names the final approval. docs/18 section 1 already bars the preparer at both steps and mon.valuation checks both (0004 lines 107 to 108). The platform's own precedent treats whoever stood behind a source figure as the preparer: the weekly intake records the reviewer of the staged value as the Draft's preparer (apps/api/src/intake/weekly/weekly.service.ts lines 506 to 514). So the bar at both steps is the existing rule applied to substance, not a new safeguard. Lifting it from the first approval would widen who may approve: not the build's to take. "Only named" is no control. An override with a reason is one person doing two people's work.
Build.
1. F3: a figure cited to the record names the quarter's approver. Add approvedBy to QuarterOnRecord and return it from recordFigureOf. Example: deal.two approves an import that restates enterprise value to 430,000,000; the refresh writes 7,310,450.25 x 283,700,000 / 263,000,000 = 7,885,835.50 with no reviewer for that input; today deal.two may then take dealTeamApprove of that mark. After the fix deal.two is refused (422).
2. Say who is barred before anyone is refused: valuationDetail gains the barred people with the reason (preparer, source reviewer), read as the system actor like sourceReviewers. Roles come from the sign-in token, not the database (core.app_user has no role), so the system cannot work out that nobody is left; it can name who is barred, and a person adds a wall member.
Not built on purpose: a database trigger for the bar. It would need the quarter tolerance, which is config, so the rule would live twice.
Run time. System: computes the list on every approval. Person: the approvals; an approver adds a wall member when a wall is too small.
Proof. suggestion-commands.test.ts: the import approver refused at both approvals and on the version a reopen starts; machines.test.ts unchanged rows. Check the seed first: if seeded quarters carry a deal team approver, the browser journey changes.
Context check. Correct. Add: a hand-started Draft after a dismissal carries no keys and bars nobody; that is the workflow as it was before the feature, three people still apply.

VS-8. Position-level statements as expected documents
Choice: expect one for a position from the quarter after the first one is classified for it, while the position is held. Not one per held position.
Rules. No rule requires it; it is a completeness check. Whether a sponsor sends one is a fact the repository cannot hold; expecting it from everyone leaves standing "missing" rows that teach people to ignore the tracker. The system can establish from its own data that this position gets them. doc.expected_document already keys on (investment_id, period_end, doc_type) and carries the wall (0011 lines 219 to 220). No schema change.
Build. generate adds the row for a position with an earlier Classified position-level statement; expectedParentOf answers the position's row for a position-level statement and still never a fund's row; preview twin; matrix counts.
Run time. System: expects, matches, marks late and missing. Person: nothing.
Proof. statement-guards.test.ts: fills its own position's row, never the fund's; a walled position's row is unseen outside the wall.

VS-9. What tells a position statement from a fund statement
Choice: both of these: the classifier reads more from page one, and the system classifies a statement by itself only when it comes to exactly one account.
Rules. Classified is terminal. A wrong "fund statement" has three effects: it fills the fund's expected row, it raises nothing, and, for a walled position, the document sits outside the wall where every signed-in user reads its pages (0011 lines 202 to 203). SEC-5.3 is a must, so the rule has to fail to a person, and has to be right whichever way real statements name a position.
Build.
1. classifierOutputSchema gains optional companyName and accountHolderName (the vehicle after "Prepared for"); prompt document_classifier version 2; keywordClassifier; evals. Linking stays exact; a sponsor's co-investment vehicle name is an alias in core.company_alias (no new table).
2. statementHold: a statement that names no position is classified as a fund statement only when its sponsor fund has no held position linked to it. A fund with both a commitment and positions sends the statement to NeedsReview unless a reference or an exact name picks one. With VS-1: and unless exactly one commitment account is named.
Run time. System: links when exactly one. Person: chooses the account for the rest. Residual stated plainly: an unlinked document about a walled company that names it inexactly is still read outside the wall until someone inside links it; no rule can detect that.
Proof. documents tests: a statement named only by the sponsor's vehicle, on a fund with a commitment and two positions, is held; with an alias it links; evidence names no company or number.
Context check. Correct. Add F1.

VS-10. Putting a classified document on the right position
Choice: build the relink, allowed after approval too, with the system working out the consequences and never touching a mark.
Rules. SEC-5.3: today a document on the wrong side of a wall cannot be moved at all, so this is required, and "refused once a run is approved" would leave out the usual case (the mistake is noticed through the suggestion). Rule 14: a new row, Classified to NeedsReview, reason required, by the deal team or operations, with rejection tests. Rule 5: nothing here changes a mark or a promoted figure.
Build.
1. Command sendBackToReview. The classify command accepts an explicit "no position" (today a link left out is kept). Whoever relinks must see the document now and the target, as checkLinks already demands.
2. In the same transaction, as the system actor: the expected row is released and matched again; duplicates follow; each approved run of the document is marked withdrawn and moves with the document (its wall is the document's); a withdrawn statement run is no longer on hand; Open suggestions from it are superseded (figures_changed).
3. Refused (409, saying what to do first) while a suggestion from one of its runs is on a version in progress or Locked.
4. F7: operations can take an accepted suggestion off a Draft, with a reason: Accepted to Withdrawn on mon.valuation_suggestion (a new state on the new table, not on mon.valuation), which removes the keys from that Draft through editDraft and ends the bar for its reviewers. Only while the version is a Draft.
5. A report whose run promoted figures: the document moves; the quarter of the wrong position keeps its figures until a normal promotion replaces them; the system opens a data exception on that quarter (wall-safe by migration 0012).
Run time. System: every consequence above. Person: the relink and its reason, the corrected classification, any correction of a mark or a quarter.
Proof. Wall test: a statement of the walled position linked to the open one is relinked; afterwards a viewer gets 404 on the document, the run, the values and the suggestion. Rejection tests for each forbidden transition.
Context check. One sentence is wrong (F2, see VS-2). Missing: F7.

VS-11. Private credit
Choice: keep: no suggestion. Decided, no code.
Rules. Rules 10 and 12. Rule 12.1 is not safe for a loan as written: credit MOIC and IRR use fair value plus accrued interest as the terminal value (docs/08 section 10), and a capital account balance already includes accrued income, so the balance taken as fair value would count the accrued interest twice. No credit schema, fixture or cross-check exists. A value needs its own rule first.
Run time. System: answers private_credit before any source is read. Person: marks the loan by hand as today.
Proof. The existing "none when" test and its sentence.

VS-12. Positions not in US dollars
Choice: keep: no suggestion, nothing converted. One change: make it fail loudly by rule, not by prompt.
Rules. docs/03 line 9; rule 10 (a rate nobody approved is an invented number); a rate source is a new, possibly paid, data source and not the build's to add. F4: SEC-8.3 treats document content as untrusted and SEC-8.4 asks for declared units; neither holds for currency today.
Build. The statement schema gains a required field for the currency the statement is stated in, read with its page; a reviewer accepts or types it. Rule: a statement whose stated currency is not USD gives no value, reason statement_not_usd, before any line is read. Prompt version 2, evals with the existing "another currency" example, docs/08 12.1 reason order, fixture 29 and the Python twin first.
Run time. System: refuses by the field. Person: confirms the currency in review.
Proof. A euro statement whose model answer wrongly says USD_M for the lines still gives no value because the accepted currency is EUR.

VS-13. Who reads the suggestion keys on a valuation
Choice: keep as built: every role that reads the valuation reads its inputs. Decided, no code.
Rules. No wall is crossed: mon.valuation and its inputs are one column under pb.investment_visible. The keys carry no person. A viewer inside the wall already reads the statement lines (stg.extracted_value, matrix line), the quarter's figures and the reference mark; new to them are the suggested value and the other sources' values, estimates about the same position. SEC-9.3: hiding keys while serving the lock text hides nothing, and withholding the lock text removes the reader's check of the hash. SEC-5.1: a filter in the API over a column the database serves whole makes the layers disagree. SEC-9.4 wants the lineage beside the number.
Condition from VS-4: the accept reason stays off the keys.
Proof. ValuationDetail tests as they are; add one that no key holds a user id.

VS-14. History tables and the wall
Choice: build it now, for every walled record, in one migration, with the client rule on the client tables' history in the same change.
Rules. SEC-5.3 and SEC-5.1 are musts, and the database is its own layer. Today it is not: operations, approvers and auditors read 2 images in mon.valuation_history while they read 1 valuation (matrix), and the same for cash flows, quarters, underwriting cases, suggestions, and for core.walled_record_history and core.wall_member_history, which tell operations and auditors which records are walled and who is inside. No route reads them, but a route, a report or a direct analytics read would. The change only narrows who reads, so it is the build's to take; a policy is not a role.
Build. Migration: audit.add_history takes the visibility clause; each existing history table gets the role clause and its base table's clause read from row_image (investment_id through pb.investment_visible; row_id for core.investment; investment or commitment for cash flows and notices; the parent for tickets, plan points and the deal tables; the client clause for core.client, core.lp_commitment, core.commitment, deal.allocation, rpt.package, rpt.qa_result; wall membership for the two wall tables). Flag and alias history unchanged.
Same decision, next slice (F5): audit.event rows about a walled record follow the wall (a function over entity and entity_id), since GET /audit/events answers the reason text of, for example, a dismissal on a walled position to four roles, platform admin included; ops.task follows the record it regards and its title drops the investment number.
Consequence to record: an auditor outside a wall stops reading that wall's history, as they already do not read its rows; the evidence pack (SEC-11.5) is produced per reader or by a named member.
Proof. READ_MATRIX: mon.valuation_history and mon.valuation_suggestion_history become 1 and 3 for operations, approver and auditor outside the wall (the open position's images), and an operations member of the wall reads all; pnpm test:rls; check:classification.
Context check. "Details never leave the database" is true; the reason and the entity id do (F5).

VS-15. A share the extractor read from an own-account statement
Choice: a review decision "not stated in the document", by a named person.
Rules. Rule 10 stays: a share read and not accepted is missing, never 100%. Typing 1.0000 records a stated share the document does not contain, with no page (SEC-8.4, SEC-9.4), and drops the line "Read as our own account". A person's explicit finding that the document states no share is a fact someone stands behind; that person becomes a source reviewer.
Build. stg.extracted_value.decision gains not_stated (a staging table); extractionRunCommandBody; finalValuesOf treats it as absent; shareLeftOpen is false for a share decided not_stated; the reviewer is added to the suggestion's source reviewers; docs/08 12.1 and fixture 29 first; docs/18 section 7; preview twin. A typed share of exactly 1 stays allowed (a vehicle we wholly own).
Risk named: the same decision on a true whole-vehicle statement gives 83,760,000.00 where ours is 20,940,000.00. It is caught by the contributions check (VS-4, now with a reason and sealed), the variance flag and the bar on that reviewer.
Run time. System: reads the lines as our account once a person says no share is stated. Person: that one decision.
Proof. controls-suggestion.test.ts: undecided or rejected share, no value; not_stated, 20,940,000.00 with ownAccount true and the reviewer barred at both approvals.

VS-16. Who may act on a suggestion
Choice: keep: operations alone. Decided, no code.
Rules. Starting a Draft is operations' alone (docs/18 section 1; policy insert_ops, 0006 lines 160 to 161). Accept by the deal team would widen who starts a valuation; dismiss by the deal team would let a first-step approver silence a source. Both widen a right. The route, the machine and the policy decide_operations agree (SEC-5.1).
Proof. Existing role rejections in machines.test.ts and the matrix write cases.

VS-17. An accounting import: refresh only, or raise
Choice: keep: refresh only. Decided, no code beyond VS-7.
Rules. SEC-9.4: a suggestion names an approved run and its document (both NOT NULL, checked by the guard trigger). A suggestion raised by an import would name neither, and until F3 is fixed its figures would have nobody behind them, so the import's approver could approve the mark. The booked fair value in the file is compared with the Locked mark and never written; that stays.
Run time. System: refreshes every Open suggestion in the promotion. Person: starts a Draft by hand for a position that only ever gets imports.
Proof. The existing "refresh after an import" test, extended by VS-7.

VS-18. A cash flow approved later, a position that closes
Choice: refresh the position's Open suggestions in the transaction that approves a cash flow, as lock and reopen do.
Rules. No wrong value can be accepted today (accept derives again and answers 409), so this is truth of what is shown, not integrity. One rule in one place: whatever changes an input refreshes. The cost is that reconcile can answer the 409 of the advisory lock; the switch valuation.suggestions turns the refresh off if it ever stands in the way of booking cash.
Build. writeCashFlow's caller calls refreshPosition for a flow keyed to a position (and, after VS-1, to a commitment's position). For a closing position: nothing to hook (F8); the command that one day writes exit_date calls the same refresh.
Run time. System only.
Proof. API test: Open report-based suggestion at 7,831,632.54, a contribution reconciled inside the window, the suggestion is superseded (figures_changed) and replaced by one with the reason flows_since_reference_mark; an expense, after VS-5, changes nothing.
Context check. The second half of the question describes an event the API cannot yet produce.

VS-19. applyToDraft and the Draft's date
Choice: keep: the Draft keeps its date. Decided, no code.
Rules. The Draft and the statement report for the same quarter by alignedQuarterEnd, so rule 10 is not touched. A Draft's date cannot move (unique key, version numbers). Refusing would push operations to type the value by hand, which loses the sealed source and bars nobody: the weaker control. Both dates are in the lock text.
Proof. The existing applyToDraft test; add that the version screen shows both dates when they differ.

VS-20. One variance flag for a quarter's change and for two sources
Choice: keep one flag. Decided, no code.
Rules. Rule 9 and one rule in one place. "Sources differ" blocks nothing and the other sources' values are sealed in the keys, so a second threshold changes a badge and no control.

VS-21. The tie tolerances and the 2%
Choice: keep the values and keep fixed figures by unit. One change to what the contributions check compares.
Rules. The figures are exactly the rounding bound, five lines times half a unit of the last printed place: 2,500 for thousands at no decimals, 25,000 for millions and 25,000,000 for billions at two. Working the bound out from the decimals of the extracted text would let a model that drops a trailing zero loosen the check tenfold (250,000 for one decimal in millions): the model's formatting would set a control. The tight side is the safe side, and with VS-4 a false "does not tie" costs a reason, not a wrong number. If real statements print one decimal, a person edits the config figure.
Build (tests first, fixture 29). The record side of the check is contributions only, while a statement's contributions to date commonly include amounts called for fees and expenses (ILPA itemizes them). The check answers which record the statement agrees with: contributions, or contributions plus fees and expenses, and differs only when it is outside 2% of both.
Proof. Fixture cases on each edge for both records; a whole-vehicle statement (300% off) still differs from both.

VS-22. The five second lock wait
Choice: keep in code. Decided.
Rules. Rule 9 lists business settings; definitions.json feeds the Data Dictionary page. No control depends on the figure: two derivations cannot both write whatever the wait (advisory lock plus the one-Open index).

VS-23. Outbox event and telling someone
Choice: neither now. Decided; record the outbox as one platform gap in docs/09, and make docs/18 line 3 true for all workflows together.
Rules. One rule in one place. SEC-5.3 and SEC-11.4: an event or a task about a walled position must name nothing; ops.task is read by everyone and its reopen title already carries the investment number (F5). No task for suggestions until ops.task follows the record it regards. The Suggestions tab, the board column and the work lists are how "as they come in" is met.
Proof. A test that no suggestion code writes ops.task.

VS-24. Measuring the statement extraction on a real model
Choice: the prototype measure stands for the prototype; the real measure is a release gate. Approving an endpoint and its key is the owner's act.
Rules. SEC-8.1, SEC-8.2, SEC-8.8, CLAUDE.md 3b and 3d (no credentials here). The system behaves correctly whatever the answer: every value is reviewed by a person, a failed output never writes, and the switch exists.
Build now. Put the hard cases of tools/synthetic/src/statement-examples.ts (lines that do not tie, a whole vehicle, another currency) and injection cases for a statement into the gold set of capital_account_extraction, so the gate is ready and VS-9, VS-12 and VS-15 are measured.
Person: the owner approves an endpoint. System: pnpm test:evals blocks a release.

VS-25. The status of decision 0023
Choice: every item moves to decided, in a new record that supersedes the section "Open for the owner"; 0023's status becomes Accepted. Use 0025: the Packages commits cite 0024 and no file of that number exists yet.
Rules. SEC-16.2: controls that run in code should not sit under "a first draft". Reword docs/08 line 202, the config note, docs/04 line 176, docs/11 E4 and E15, docs/decisions/README.md.
What stays with a person, by name: approving a model endpoint (VS-24), publishing (VS-26), merging (VS-27), and whether the service role may read client-directed commitments (VS-1).

VS-26. The published preview and media
Choice: with the next publish. Nothing to build. A session never deploys; publishing is the owner's approval in GitHub Actions, after the preview's own synthetic-data scan.

VS-27. How the feature goes to review
Choice: stacked pull requests by layer, in dependency order, not one.
Rules. CLAUDE.md 3b (about 800 lines), SEC-16.2, and SEC-17.3: row-level security and segregation of duties need a second human reviewer, so migration 0023 with its policies, and the valuationMachine precondition with sourceReviewers, each stand alone and are labelled. The Packages work now on the same branch goes on its own branch (3d: one issue per branch). Opening them is the build's; approving and merging is a person's.

VS-28. A statement for a quarter whose mark is Locked
Choice: no suggestion and no reopen (as built), plus a data exception when the statement's value is the variance flag or more away from the Locked mark of the same quarter.
Rules. Rule 5 and SEC-9.3: only a person reopens. SEC-9.5 (tie-outs): staying silent when a final statement disagrees with a Locked mark is a gap the data on hand can close. ops.data_exception is an existing workflow (rule 7), and an exception about an extraction run is read only by those who can read the run (migration 0012), so it is safe behind a wall.
Build (tests first: it is rule 12.5 with the Locked mark as the base; docs/08 text and a case in fixture 30). At approval of a position-level statement whose bar is mark_locked for its own quarter, the system works out S; at or above valuationVarianceFlag it opens an exception, rule id of its own, with both figures.
Worked example: Locked 20,940,000.00. A final statement of 23,200,000.00 is 2,260,000.00 more, 10.79%: exception. One of 21,500,000.00 is 2.67%: none, and the run page says it agrees.
Run time. System: the comparison and the exception. Person: acknowledges, and reopens with a reason if the mark should change.
Proof. API test for both figures; a test that no suggestion row and no valuation row is written; the exception is unseen outside the wall.

Sources consulted for the public standards (secondary summaries, primary texts not fetched):
- [IPEV Valuation Guidelines, December 2018 (contents: Valuing Fund Interests, Adjustments to Net Asset Value)](https://www.ivca.ie/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/IPEV-Valuation-Guidelines-December-2018.pdf)
- [Grant Thornton, key updates to the IPEV Guidelines for 2026](https://www.grantthornton.co.uk/insights/key-updates-to-the-ipev-guidelines-what-fund-managers-need-to-know-for-2026/)
- [ILPA Reporting Template definitions](https://ilpa.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Reporting-Template-Definitions.pdf)
- [Citco, ILPA reporting and new performance template](https://www.citco.com/insights/ilpa-reporting-and-new-performance-template)

## product:valuation-suggestions

PRODUCT LENS: valuation suggestions from capital account statements and sponsor reports (VS-1 to VS-28)

Read only. Nothing in /home/user/Portfolio-Beach was created, changed or run, and no file was written anywhere. Figures in the worked examples were checked by hand.

FIVE FINDINGS THAT CUT ACROSS THE QUESTIONS (each is used below)

A. The two statement checks arrive too late. They are worked out only after the run is approved (suggestionReading, packages/workflows/src/controls-suggestion.ts lines 942 to 983), and an approved run cannot be corrected (apps/api/src/extraction/review.service.ts line 153). So a misread line is found at the one moment it can no longer be fixed. The same pure function (readStatement) can run over the reviewer's decisions before approval and show both checks on the review page.
B. Operations decides one suggestion at a time, each with a dialog (apps/web/src/pages/valuations/Suggestions.tsx). With forty statements that is forty dialogs. The valuation board already has a batch (POST /valuations/batch-commands). The system can sort clean from flagged itself.
C. Fees are booked as their own flow type. A fee notice writes flow_type.fee (FLOW_OF_NOTICE, apps/api/src/capital/capital.service.ts line 132), and summarizeFlows keeps fees out of contributions (packages/calc/src/cashflows.ts lines 85 to 88). A sponsor's statement counts every amount called as a contribution. So for any position with fee notices a correct statement raises "Contributions differ", and any fee flow makes rule 12.2 "not calculable" (sources.ts passes every flow date).
D. Reviewed figures are thrown away or retyped. The report schema reads fund_fair_value, fund_cost and sponsor_ownership_pct (config/extraction_schema.json), a person accepts them, and no rule reads them. After a reopen the refresh never creates a suggestion, so a statement value a person already reviewed must be typed by hand.
E. A capital account statement that links to no position and no sponsor fund is still Classified when the classifier is confident (documents.service.ts classify: statementHold answers null when the statement names no position, whatever links.sponsorFundId is). Classified is terminal, so it becomes an orphan nobody is told about.

==== VS-1 ====
Choice: a position per commitment (option a), marked through the one valuation workflow, so rule 12.1 applies as written. This is the answer in which the system does the work: for a fund of funds programme the fund statements are most of the forty, and today each is read, reviewed, approved and leads nowhere.
Reasons. Rule 5 and rule 7 leave only this: a mark must go through staging and approval, and there may be one valuation workflow, so the mark lives in mon.valuation, which needs an investment row. Option b changes mon.valuation, a table the accounting export reads (config/accounting_layout.json reads valuation.fair_value), so it is not the build's to take. Option c is a second workflow. Options d and e leave the primary programme with no NAV at all. Practice: an investor values a fund interest from its share of the sponsor's reported net asset value, adjusted for calls and distributions since (IPEV Valuation Guidelines, the chapter on valuing fund interests, sections 4.1 and 4.2; the net asset value practical expedient of US accounting standards), and the ILPA Reporting Template's capital account is a roll forward from beginning to ending NAV for one investor.
What must be true so no rule is bent:
1. No schema change to core.investment, core.portfolio_company or mon.valuation (all read by the accounting layout). The link between a commitment and its position goes in a new table (commitment id unique, investment id unique, with history). Rows only in the existing tables. The stand-in company row is not new behaviour: apps/api/src/pipeline/convert.ts already makes one from the deal's company name.
2. No wider read. core.investment is under the wall only, while a client-directed commitment is under the client rule (SEC-5.2). So pb.investment_visible gains one clause: a position that stands for a commitment is visible only to a caller who can see that commitment. That narrows, and only for the new rows. If the build stages the work, pooled commitments come first and a client-directed one answers its own reason until the clause is in.
3. Flows are not re-keyed. The policy on mon.cash_flow reads the commitment rule only when investment_id is null (migration 0006 line 172). Moving a commitment's flows onto the position would drop the client rule. One loader says "the flows of a position that stands for a commitment are that commitment's flows", and apps/api/src/suggestions/sources.ts reads through it for the contributions check.
4. A person approves the position (rule 5 names positions). Going forward the closing workflow does that; it must also write the core.commitment row and the link in the same transaction (see the correction below). For commitments already on record the synthetic generator seeds the position and link (docs/14 changes).
5. Company-level readers stay closed by the one list EQUITY_DEAL_TYPES. NAV series, portfolio totals, look-through and packages should include fund interests: an investor's NAV includes them. Each reader needs one test saying which side it is on.
6. One statement, one account. The prompt reads the balance net of accrued carried interest when both are printed (the ILPA template shows ending NAV net and gross). That is a new prompt version and an eval run.
Run time. The system decides which commitment a fund statement is for: the fund has one commitment, or the account named links exactly to one vehicle; otherwise the reviewer picks, shown each commitment with its amount and called to date. It then gives S, the tie, and the contributions check against the commitment's approved flows. A statement listing several accounts with no balance for one raises none and says so. A person still accepts, prepares and approves.
Worked example (US$ millions, one account): beginning 41.20, contributions 3.00, distributions 1.10, net gain 1.45, ending 44.55. The lines add up. S = 44,550,000.00. Against a previous quarter mark of 41,200,000.00: change 3,350,000.00, 8.13106796%, no variance; net of flows 1,450,000.00, 3.51941748%.
The commitments board must show, per commitment: the Locked mark with its date and age, called, distributed, unfunded, statement received or late, and a Suggested column like the board's Missing marks card.
Follow-up decided in principle, own slice, tests first: fund statements arrive a quarter late, so a fourth basis, last in the order: the last Locked mark plus contributions less distributions since (navRollForward exists), raised when operations generates the quarter's expected documents and replaced by the statement when it comes.
Corrections to the context. (i) "As closing a primary fund deal already creates" overstates: closing writes a core.investment and no core.commitment (no file under apps/api/src/pipeline touches schema.commitment), so a fund closed in the app never reaches the commitments board or the tracker. (ii) The synthetic dataset has no primary fund position (13 co-investments, 5 continuation vehicles, 2 credit), so how every reader treats one is untested. (iii) Point 3 treats NAV readers picking up fund positions as a risk; for this team it is the purpose.

==== VS-2 ====
Choice: as built. Statement, then the report's equity value, then the multiple held; one list in config. Decided, no code change.
Reasons. The statement is the sponsor's stated value of our own interest; the report rules are our estimate from company figures, so the stated value ranks first (IPEV, reported value as the starting point). A blend hides the source. An order by deal type has no basis: a continuation vehicle's statement is as much the stated value as a co-investment's. "Newer first" is already right where it matters (a later statement replaces; a later report does not displace a statement). "Statement only" would leave a quarter with only a report blank, and the person would start from nothing.
If the VS-3 addition is built the list becomes four: statement, sponsor's holding value, equity value, multiple held. suggestionSettings already refuses any list that is not each basis once.
Run time. The system picks and shows the others with their differences. One thing the row should add once VS-8 is in: for a report-based suggestion, "a statement for this quarter is expected and not yet received", so operations can choose to wait.
Context: correct. Note that "Sources differ" can in practice only appear on a statement-based suggestion, since a statement with a value always wins.

==== VS-3 ====
Choice: equity stays enterprise value less net debt, never total_equity. Rules 12.2 and 12.3 stay as written. One rule is added ahead of them, in its own slice.
Reasons. total_equity may be book equity, which is not a fair value. The held multiple is a legitimate estimate calibrated to the last mark, but it is the platform's, not the sponsor's (see VS-6 for its label).
The addition (finding D). When the approved report states the sponsor fund's own fair value of its holding, that is the sponsor's valuation of the stake itself, after the things enterprise value less net debt ignores. Rule: S = M0 x F1 / F0, F being fund_fair_value as accepted in the approved run of each quarter, read from the run's final values (finalValuesOf), so no column is added to mon.quarterly_performance. No value, with a reason, when F is missing in either quarter, when fund_cost differs between the two quarters (the fund bought or sold, so the ratio no longer carries over), or under rule 12.2's own reasons. Example in line with worked example 4: M0 7,310,450.25, F0 105,200,000.00, F1 112,700,000.00, fund cost unchanged: S = 7,831,632.54.
Build: docs/08 section 12 text, a fixture, property tests and the Python twin first; a fourth value in SUGGESTION_BASES and in the basis check of mon.valuation_suggestion (the new table); sourceOrder; the workings card.
Run time. The system picks the rule the figures allow and says which. A person gets only "not calculable" with its reason.
Context: correct.

==== VS-4 ====
Choice: suggest, never hold the value back. But a failed check stops being silent, and the system removes the false alarms itself.
Reasons. The ending balance is what the sponsor states; a tie failure usually means one of the other four lines was misread or the statement prints lines the schema does not read (the ILPA layout has separate lines for fees, expenses, income, realized and unrealized gain and carry, and the schema has one net_gain_loss). Holding the value back would punish a correct statement. But today nothing downstream knows: accept is not gated and the Draft's keys carry neither result, so both approvers see a failed check only if they open the suggestion.
Build.
1. Before approval (finding A): the run detail carries a preview from readStatement over the current decisions, and ExtractionRun.tsx shows "The lines add up" or "are US$ X apart" and "Contributions agree" or "differ" beside the existing "Once approved, this is read as" line. The reviewer fixes a misread line while it can still be edited.
2. Count fees (finding C): the contributions check agrees when the statement's figure is within tolerance of the approved contributions, or of contributions plus fees and expenses, and says which. Example: record 12,400,000.00 with 310,000.00 of fee flows; statement 12.71 in millions. Today 2.5%, flagged. With fees counted, equal. Tests first: docs/08 rule 12.1, fixture 29, the Python twin.
3. At accept and applyToDraft: when the lines do not tie or contributions differ, a reason is required. A precondition in the docs/18 section 14 table with rejection tests in machines.test.ts. The reason and both check results go into the Draft's keys (suggestion.check.ties, .tieDifference, .contributionsStatement, .contributionsRecord, .contributionsDifferencePct, .acceptReason), so the version screen shows them to both approvers and the lock hash seals them. No migration.
4. Different words for the two checks: the tie says "check the reading"; contributions says "check this is the right account, share and unit". When the ratio is close to 1,000 or 1,000,000 the sentence says it looks like a unit error.
5. Batch (finding B): on the Suggestions tab, "Open drafts for the N suggestions with no flag", each in its own transaction as the valuation batch does. Flagged ones stay one by one.
Run time. The system settles: ties or not, agrees or not, and why. A person sees only the flagged ones, with the two figures side by side, and types why the value still stands or dismisses.
Context: correct. Add that after approval the lines cannot be corrected.

==== VS-5 ====
Choice: no value when a contribution or a distribution moved since the reference mark, as built. Rolling the scaled mark forward is set aside because it is wrong. One change: fee and expense flows do not count.
Reasons, with figures. M0 = 10,000,000 for 10% of E0 = 100,000,000. The company raises 20,000,000 and grows 6,000,000, so E1 = 126,000,000. If we took part pro rata (we paid 2,000,000) the true value is 12,600,000: scaling alone gives 12,600,000, scale then roll gives 14,600,000. If we did not take part our share is 8.33% and the true value is 10,500,000: both give 12,600,000. So the roll forward is right in neither case and scaling is right only when we took part pro rata, which the report alone cannot say. The statement can, and rule 12.1 is not affected.
The change (finding C): a fee or an expense buys or sells none of the company, so it must not stop the rule. Today one fee notice in the quarter turns every report-based suggestion for the position into "not calculable". hasFlowBetween takes only contribution, distribution, recallable and principal flows. Tests first: docs/08 rule 12.2 and "which marks and flows count", fixture 31, apps/worker-py/src/calc_check/marks.py.
What the person sees must improve. Today: "Cash has moved since the last Locked mark" and a Dismiss button. It should name the flows (date, type, amount, a link to the notice) and say what settles it: "the capital account statement for this quarter", with its tracker status once VS-8 is in.
Context: correct that every flow type counts today.

==== VS-6 ====
Choice: "Sponsor mark" for a statement and "Indication from sponsor report" for rule 12.2, as built. The multiple held gets its own term, for example valuation_method.held_multiple_estimate, "Estimate at the multiple held". A fund interest marked from its statement (VS-1) carries "Sponsor mark".
Reasons. There are three grades of distance from what the sponsor said: it stated our value; it stated the company's value and we scaled; it stated no value and the platform held last quarter's multiple. Calling the third an indication from the sponsor's report tells an auditor reading the lock text something that is not so. A separate label for a statement against a hand-typed sponsor mark is not needed: the keys and the "Started from a suggestion" line already say it.
Build: one taxonomy row by migration, SUGGESTION_METHODS in packages/calc/src/terms.ts with its Python twin, the two tests that name labels. Locked versions keep the code they were locked with.
Run time: the system sets the method; operations can still change it on the Draft.
Context: correct.

==== VS-7 ====
Choice: keep a bar, make it exact, and make sure it is never a surprise. Bar the final approval (lock) only; read the bar from the suggestion whose keys the version carries; warn at the point of approving the run.
Reasons. As built, the deal lead who reviews a statement's reading cannot give the deal team approval of the mark from it. That is the natural person for both, and on a walled position with few members it can leave nobody, with no way out. The existing rules already put at least two people on every mark (preparer is neither approver). SEC-5.6 speaks of the final approver. The owner's recorded guidance of 2026-11-11 is no safeguards beyond those the guide names (docs/decisions/0013-phase3-controls.md line 86).
On rule b. The bar exists only on this branch; before the feature docs/18 section 1 held the preparer rule alone. Measured against what is merged, a bar on lock only is still a narrowing. If the decider reads rule b as forbidding any loosening of what the branch built, keep both bars and still build points 2 and 3.
Build.
1. notSourceReviewer stays on lock and leaves dealTeamApprove (machines.ts, the docs/18 section 1 table, rejection tests).
2. The list comes from the suggestion named by suggestion.id in the version's own inputs. A reopen copies the keys, so the rule still follows the mark, and a suggestion that was replaced on the Draft no longer bars its reviewers (today it does, though none of its figures remain).
3. The run page says before approval: "Approving this run means you cannot give the final approval of the valuation it leads to." The version screen names the source reviewers.
A figure cited to the record keeps naming nobody; barring import approvers would be a new safeguard.
Run time: the system knows who is barred and says so before the person hits the refusal.
Context: the first approval step is the deal team's alone; run reviewers are the deal team and operations.

==== VS-8 ====
Choice: expect a position-level statement only from a position that has sent one, and let the system learn that from the documents.
Reasons. Whether a sponsor issues one is a fact about the outside world, but this rule behaves correctly whatever the answer: a sponsor that never sends is never chased, one that starts is tracked from then on, one that stops drops off by itself after showing as missing.
Rule: when the quarter's rows are generated (apps/api/src/documents/expected.service.ts), a held position gets a capital_account_statement row if a Classified position-level statement was received for it for either of the two quarters before. The look-back is a new key under documents in config/definitions.json. No schema change: the unique key on (investment_id, period_end, doc_type) exists. expectedParentOf and its preview twin answer the investment for a statement linked to a position; the pinned test in statement-guards.test.ts changes; the access matrix counts for doc.expected_document change.
With VS-1 this becomes one rule: a statement is expected per position, always for a fund interest and by history for the rest. The row keyed on the sponsor fund cannot serve a fund with two commitments (one row, two statements).
Run time: the system decides what to expect. A person sees late and missing as today, and the Missing marks card can say "statement expected, not yet received".
Context: correct.

==== VS-9 ====
Choice: decide the kind from who issued the statement and from the book, hold what the book cannot settle, and remember the person's answer so the same sponsor vehicle never asks twice.
Reasons. Real statements cannot enter the repository, so the rule must be safe whatever they look like. Today "names no position" means "fund statement, never held". The most common real case for a co-investor is a sponsor where we hold both a commitment and co-investments, and a co-investment vehicle's statement that mentions the main fund then fills the fund's row and raises nothing, for good.
Rule, one pure function beside statementHold in packages/validation/src/documents.ts, tested as a table:
1. It names a position that links to exactly one: position-level, as built. Ambiguous or disagreeing: a person, as built.
2. It names none. Fund-level only when the issuing entity links exactly to a sponsor fund on record (name or core.fund_alias) and that fund has a commitment.
3. The issuing entity links exactly to a remembered vehicle name (core.company_alias, exact, as today) that comes to one position: position-level.
4. Anything else, including a statement that links to nothing at all (finding E): the review queue.
Build: classifier prompt version 2 and classifierOutputSchema gain issuerName (whose capital account this is) and companyName from the title block; keywordClassifier follows; pnpm test:evals. Evidence keeps reasons in words only (SEC-5.3). When a reviewer puts a held statement on a position, the page offers "remember this name for INV-nnnn", which writes an alias through the existing maintenance path.
Run time. The system links everything exact. A person sees, once per unknown vehicle: "This statement is from an entity not on record", with the candidates (the fund's commitment with amount and called to date; the positions held through that fund), picks one, and may tick remember.
Context: correct; add finding E.

==== VS-10 ====
Choice: build relinking, and allow it after approval for a statement. The mistake is found through the suggestion, which exists only after approval, so the record's "refused once a run is approved" refuses it exactly when it is needed.
Build. A new row in docs/18 section 6: Classified to NeedsReview, by the deal team or operations, reason required, only by someone who can see the position it is on (a 404 otherwise), with rejection tests. For a document whose approved runs promoted nothing (a statement): those runs are marked withdrawn (a column on doc.extraction_run, not an accounting table), the expected row it filled is emptied, the derivation refreshes the old position so its Open suggestion closes, and the document goes back through classification, extraction and review on the right position. For a document with a run that promoted figures to mon.quarterly_performance: refused, and the sentence says what to do (approve the right report or an import for that quarter first), because only an approved promotion changes that record (rule 5). If the wrong value is already on a Locked mark nothing reopens by itself: the version screen says its source was relinked and a person decides.
This also ends a defect in what is built: the wrongly linked statement stays "on hand" and keeps raising "Sources differ" beside every later suggestion for that quarter.
Run time: the system does the consequences; the person only says "wrong position" and why. RELINK_NOTE in apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts goes.
Context: correct.

==== VS-11 ====
Choice: rule 12.1 applies to a private credit position when a position-level statement is approved for it. The report rules stay off for credit. A credit rule of its own is not decided here.
Reasons. The ending balance of our capital account in a vehicle is the sponsor's stated value of our interest whatever the vehicle holds. Today suggestionBar answers private_credit before any source is read, so a person approves the statement, is told suggestions are not built for credit, and types the same balance into a Draft. Whether credit sponsors send such statements does not need to be known: if one arrives and links, the rule is right; if none arrives, nothing changes.
Build: no new math. suggestionBar lets credit through; readSources already reads no report when there is no quarterly record. Tests in controls-suggestion.test.ts and suggestions.test.ts, docs/18 section 14 "none when", docs/04 line 183, and the sentence in NO_SUGGESTION_SENTENCE.
Run time: the system suggests from the statement; with no statement nothing is raised and the page says why.
Context: correct.

==== VS-12 ====
Choice: as built. No suggestion for a position not held in US dollars, and nothing converted. Decided, no code change.
Reasons. docs/03 line 9 says mixed currency math must fail loudly. A rate nobody approved would be an invented number (rule 10). A rate table is a new data source and possibly a paid one, which is not the build's to add. Every Draft is written in USD.
Run time: the run page already says why. Worth recording as a platform matter and not a suggestions one: a person who starts a Draft by hand for such a position is typing a dollar figure converted off the platform.
Context: correct.

==== VS-13 ====
Choice: as built. Every role that reads the valuation reads the keys.
Reasons. The keys are part of the valuation and the lock text covers them. Leaving them out of inputs while serving the lock text hides nothing; withholding the lock text takes away the reader's check of text against hash. The figures behind the keys are already readable by those roles inside the wall; the only new figures are the suggested value, which is the fair value when accepted, and another source's value. One valuation should have one view.
Build: nothing. Record it as decided. The reason and check keys of VS-4 follow the same rule.
Context: correct.

==== VS-14 ====
Choice: yes, history follows the wall of its base record, for every walled record, in one migration with the access matrix to match.
Reasons. It only narrows, so rule b lets the build take it, and SEC-5.3 with SEC-5.1 asks for it: today the database shows a walled record's images to operations, approvers and auditors outside the wall. No page reads history, so no user sees a change now; it has to be done before any change history page or evidence pack export is built.
Build (the security lens owns the detail): audit.add_history and the tables it already made; the clause reads the wall key from row_image; the *_history rows of READ_MATRIX. The client rule on client history needs no change, since the three history roles all read every client. audit.event stays as it is: its details never leave the database through the API.
Context: correct.

==== VS-15 ====
Choice: a review decision "Not in the document", made by a named person, and the checks shown before approval so the reviewer sees which reading is right.
Reasons. Today the reviewer of an own-account statement whose share was misread has two bad choices: reject the share and then the lines, so a correct statement gives nothing; or type 100%, which records a stated share the document does not contain and drops the line "Read as our own account". Rule 10 stands: a share nobody stood behind is still missing, never 100%. The new decision is a person standing behind "there is no share here".
Build: 'not_stated' in the decision check of stg.extracted_value (a staging table); a command in extractionRunCommandBody; finalValuesOf gives no entry for it; shareLeftOpen is false for it; the reviewer is a source reviewer; the preview twin (apps/web/src/preview/routes/extraction.ts); docs/18 section 7; docs/08 rule 12.1 and fixture 29 first.
The system does the thinking (finding A): the review page shows both readings. Example: "As 25.00% of the vehicle: contributions to date 3,100,000 against 12,400,000 on record, differ. As our own account: 12,400,000 against 12,400,000, agree." The reviewer decides at a glance, in either direction, which also covers the accepted wrong share that today only shows up after approval.
Run time: the person sees the two readings and picks; nothing is asked when the share was accepted or the statement states none.
Context: correct.

==== VS-16 ====
Choice: operations alone act, as built. Decided.
Reasons. Starting a Draft is operations' in the valuation workflow and in the insert policy on mon.valuation, so letting the deal team accept would widen who can start a valuation. Dismissal silences a source and is remembered, so it stays with the role that owns the valuation. Both would be permission changes, which are not the build's.
What changes without touching a permission: the batch of finding B, and a count on the work list entry "Valuation suggestions" (config/role_focus.json gives a label and a sentence, no number), for example "7 open, 2 need a look", read under the caller's own access.
Context: correct.

==== VS-17 ====
Choice: as built. An approved accounting import refreshes Open suggestions and raises none. Decided.
Reasons. A suggestion is raised by a document a person reviewed, and every suggestion names an approved run and its document (SEC-9.4). Raising one from an import would need a suggestion that names an intake row instead. It would also put a report-based indication beside the fair value the same file carries, which by design is compared with the Locked mark and never written. The refresh keeps anything Open honest, which is what matters.
Set aside: raising from an import with the intake file as source. Revisit only if imports become the main way quarterly figures arrive.
Run time: nothing for a person.
Context: correct.

==== VS-18 ====
Choice: refresh the position's Open suggestions in the transaction that approves a cash flow for it, and in the one that exits or deactivates it, as lock and reopen already do.
Reasons. Today a report-based suggestion can keep showing a value the rule no longer supports until someone clicks accept and gets a 409. A figure on a list that the system already knows is stale is worse than no figure. With VS-5 only a contribution, a distribution, a recallable or a principal flow can change the answer, but refreshing on every approved flow of the position is the simpler single rule.
Build: writeCashFlow in apps/api/src/capital/capital.service.ts calls refreshPosition when the flow has an investment, or the position that stands for its commitment after VS-1; the same where a position's exit date or active flag is written. The funding command can then answer the 409 "Another approval for this position and quarter is in progress"; that is rare and the same behaviour as lock. Tests in suggestions.test.ts and the preview hooks in suggestionDerivation.ts; docs/18 section 14 "When it runs" and section 3.
Run time: all the system's.
Context: correct.

==== VS-19 ====
Choice: as built. The Draft keeps its own period end. Decided.
Reasons. Version numbers follow the exact period end and the workflow has no edit that moves a date. Refusing would leave the person with a Draft that cannot be withdrawn and a value that cannot be used. The two dates already count as the same quarter everywhere (alignedQuarterEnd).
One sentence to add when the dates differ: applySentence in apps/web/src/pages/valuations/suggestions.ts should say "The statement is for Jun 30, 2025; the draft is dated Jun 27, 2025 and keeps its date", and the version screen should show both.
Context: correct. Drafts dated off the quarter end mostly come from the weekly intake, which opens them at the report date.

==== VS-20 ====
Choice: one flag, as built. Decided.
Reasons. "Sources differ" is advisory and can only arise between a statement and a report-based estimate (VS-2). The estimate ignores things the statement includes, so a tighter test would mostly produce noise. One function and one setting is one rule in one place. If the board's flag is tuned both move together, and the record should say so. Sources differing asks for no reason at accept: the statement wins by the order.
Context: correct.

==== VS-21 ====
Choice: work the tie tolerance out from the places each line was printed with: half a unit of the last printed place, per line, summed over the five lines. Keep the 2% for contributions as the starting value, with fees counted (VS-4).
Reasons. The three configured figures are exactly this formula at an assumed precision: 5 x 500 = 2,500 for whole thousands, 5 x 5,000 = 25,000 for millions to two places, 5 x 5,000,000 = 25,000,000 for billions to two places. A sponsor that prints millions to one place needs 5 x 50,000 = 250,000 and fails every quarter while correct. Example: true lines 18.44, 2.04, 0.35, 0.86, 20.99 print as 18.4, 2.0, 0.4, 0.9, 21.0; the printed lines give 20.9, which is 100,000 from 21.0: "Does not tie" at 25,000, ties at 250,000. With a reason required at accept (VS-4) a false flag costs a person time every quarter, so the tolerance has to be right. The system has the printed text of every staged value, so it can settle this itself.
Build: docs/08 rule 12.1 text, fixture 29 (worked examples 2 and 8 plus a one-place example), the Python twin, then statementTieTolerance. The half unit is one config key; tieToleranceByUnit stays as the fallback for a line a reviewer typed. The 2% is a config value the team can tune in config/local; the rule is right at any value, so it is not an owner question.
Context: correct.

==== VS-22 ====
Choice: stays a constant in code. Decided.
Reasons. It is a wait on a database lock, not a business threshold. docs/17 line 46 asks that timeouts exist, not that they sit in config, and no other technical wait in apps/api/src is in config/definitions.json. A user never needs a different value for one firm than another.
Context: correct.

==== VS-23 ====
Choice: no outbox event and no task per suggestion now. Show a count where people already look.
Reasons. The outbox is a gap of the whole platform; writing suggestion events alone would be one rule in two places once the relay exists. A task per suggestion would be forty tasks in a week, and ops.task is readable by every signed-in user, so a task titled with an investment number would tell people outside a wall that a walled position exists (SEC-5.3).
Build: the count of VS-16 on the work list for operations and the deal team, and the same number on the Suggestions tab label, each read under the caller's own access. That answers "as they come in" without a new channel.
Context: correct.

==== VS-24 ====
Choice: keep the prototype measure, widen what it measures now, and do not describe the reading as measured on a real model anywhere.
Reasons. Approving a development model endpoint is an act only the owner can perform. The system behaves correctly whatever the answer, because no read value is used until a person accepts it and the checks of VS-4 catch misreads. So this blocks nothing.
Build now, with the mock: the gold set is 161 fields in one layout. Add the layouts that matter from tools/synthetic/src/statement-examples.ts and from these decisions: lines that do not tie, a whole vehicle with a share, another currency, a balance printed net and gross of carry, separate fee and gain lines, one place of decimals, an issuer that is a co-investment vehicle. The same set then measures a real endpoint the day one is approved.
Owner only: approving an endpoint, and only with synthetic statements.
Context: correct.

==== VS-25 ====
Choice: every item moves to decided, in a new decision record that takes the place of the "Open for the owner" section of 0023. The number is 0025, since the Reporting work cites 0024.
Build: 0023's status becomes Accepted and points to the new record; each item has its choice, its reason and the alternative set aside. The words "a first draft for the owner to change", "a draft set" and "wait on how a commitment carries a mark" change in docs/08 line 202, the note in config/definitions.json, docs/04 line 176, docs/11 rows E4 and E15, docs/decisions/README.md line 20, and docs/SESSION_LOG.md is appended. Page copy changes with the decisions: the fund_commitment and private_credit sentences in NO_SUGGESTION_SENTENCE, and RELINK_NOTE.
Decided as built, no code: VS-2, 12, 13, 16, 17, 19, 20, 22. Decided with a change: VS-1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 14, 15, 18, 21, 23, 24, 28. Process: VS-25, 26, 27.
Context: correct.

==== VS-26 ====
Choice: with the next publish, after the decided changes land. Nothing to build.
Reasons. Publishing is a person's approval in GitHub Actions, so the act is the owner's. Recording the media now would be redone once VS-1, 4 and 28 change the pages.
Context: correct.

==== VS-27 ====
Choice: one pull request per layer, stacked in dependency order, cut at the slice boundaries the commits already have: the calculation; the machine and shared rules; the table and the four guards; the schema, documents and routes; the commands; the pages; the preview and the browser journey. The Reporting work goes in its own pull request on top, and each decided change above is a further small one.
Reasons. One pull request this size cannot be reviewed (CLAUDE.md section 3b). Eleven or more dependent ones is more than a single owner should have to merge in order. Merging is the owner's act.
Context: correct.

==== VS-28 ====
Choice: change it. A statement whose value differs from the Locked mark of its own quarter stays in the queue until a person uses it or dismisses it with a reason. The system works out the difference itself; nothing reopens by itself.
Reasons. Final statements after a first lock are routine, and for fund interests (VS-1) they are the normal order of events. As built the person gets one sentence on the run page, "a mark for this quarter or a later one is Locked", with no figure, and if they then reopen the mark they must type the reviewed balance by hand (finding D).
Rule. mark_locked still applies when a later quarter is Locked, when the source is a report (an estimate never challenges a Locked mark), and when the statement's value is within a configured share of the Locked mark, in which case the run page says "agrees with the Locked mark". Otherwise the statement's suggestion opens, or stays Open at lock, like any other. Its row offers "Reopen the Locked mark" as a link to the version, where operations or an approver reopens with a reason as today; the reopened Draft then takes the value through applyToDraft. Or operations dismisses it with a reason, such as "trued up next quarter", which is what an auditor will ask to see. Rule 5 and SEC-9.3 hold: every step is a person's command.
Example: Locked 20,940,000.00; the final statement states 21,400,000.00; 460,000.00 above, 2.20%.
Build: a new key, for example valuationSuggestions.lockedMarkDifferencePct, starting at 1%. The right figure is the team's materiality and the rule is right at any value, so it is tunable config and not an owner question. suggestionBar and derivationStep with their tests; docs/18 section 14; fixture 31 if lockedForOrAfter splits; runOutcomeText; a "Locked mark differs" state in offerFor; the board.
Run time. The system decides whether it matters and shows both figures with the difference. A person sees only the ones that differ and chooses between reopen and dismiss.
Correction to the context: "a later approval can then open a suggestion" after a reopen is true only of a new approval. The statement already approved is not offered again, because a refresh never creates one. That is the retyping this decision removes.

PUBLIC SOURCES AND WHAT WAS NOT VERIFIED
Searched with generic queries only. Confirmed: the IPEV Valuation Guidelines of December 2022 have a chapter on valuing fund interests with sections 4.1 General and 4.2 Adjustments to Net Asset Value, and reported NAV is the starting point; the ILPA Reporting Template (definitions of January 2025, in use from the first quarter of 2026) is a roll forward from beginning to ending NAV with contributions including deemed and in-kind amounts. Not verified: the wording of IPEV section 4.2 on calls and distributions between dates; a third-party page saying a December 2025 edition takes effect for periods beginning on or after 1 April 2026; the exact ILPA line labels for NAV net and gross of carry. The net asset value practical expedient is cited from general knowledge.
- https://www.privateequityvaluation.com/Valuation-Guidelines
- https://www.dirittobancario.it/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/IPEV-Valuation-Guidelines.pdf
- https://www.pwc.com/jg/en/events/document/ipev-2022-update-summary-26-january-2023.pdf
- https://ilpa.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Reporting-Template-Definitions.pdf
- https://ilpa.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/RT-Definitions-Redline-Comment-Period-ILPA-Reporting-Template-to-Updated-ILPA-Reporting-Template.pdf
